Versal dg deformation of Calabi–Yau manifolds
Hayato Morimura

Abstract
. We prove the equivalence of the deformation theory for a higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifold and that for its dg category of perfect complexes by giving a natural isomorphism of the deformation functors. As a consequence, the dg category of perfect complexes on a versal deformation of the original manifold provides a versal Morita deformation of its dg category of perfect complexes. Besides the classical uniqueness up to étale neiborhood of the base, we prove another sort of uniqueness of versal Morita deformations. Namely, given a pair of derived-equivalent higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifolds, the dg categories of perfect complexes of their algebraic deformations over a common base, which always exist, become quasi-equivalent close to effectivizations. Then the base change along the corresponding first order approximation yields quasi-equivalent versal Morita deformations. We introduce the generic fiber of the versal Morita deformation as a Drinfeld quotient, which is quasi-equivalent to the dg category of perfect complexes on the generic fiber of the versal deformation.



1.
Introduction

The derived category of coherent sheaves on an algebraic variety is an intensively studied invariant
which carries rich information about geometric properties of the variety.
For instance,
given a smooth projective variety
either of whose canonical or anticanonical bundle is ample,
one can reconstruct the variety from its derived category

[
BO01
]
.
The condition guarantees the absence of nontrivial autoequivalences of the derived category.
Such autoequivalences often stem from the derived equivalence of nonisomorphic,
sometimes even nonbirational Calabi–Yau manifolds.
According to the homological mirror symmetry conjecture by Kontsevich,
derived-equivalent Calabi–Yau manifolds should share their mirror partner.
Usually,
the homological mirror symmetry is considered for families of Kähler manifolds.

A goal of this paper is to study the relationship between
deformations
and
the derived category
of a higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifold.
There seems to be a consensus among some experts
that
deforming
an algebraic variety
and
its derived category
are essentially the same.
Philosophically,
it is reasonable
since their Hochschild cohomology,
which in general is known to control deformations of a mathematical object,
are isomorphic.
However,
before

[
LV06b
]

we were not given the correct framework to study deformations of even
linear
nor
abelian categories.
To every second Hochschild cocycle on a smooth projective variety,
Toda associated the category of twisted coherent sheaves on the corresponding noncommutative scheme over the ring of dual numbers

[
Tod09
]
.
In

[
DLL17
]

Dinh–Liu–Lowen showed that
Toda’s construction indeed yields flat abelian first order deformations of the category of coherent sheaves on the variety in the sense of

[
LV06b
]
.
We fill the gap between this point
and
the conclusion
stated below more precisely.

Let

X_{0}

be a Calabi–Yau manifold of dimension more than two in the strict sense,
i.e.,
a smooth projective
\mathbf{k}
-variety with

\omega_{X_{0}}\cong\mathscr{O}_{X_{0}}

and

H^{i}(\mathscr{O}_{X_{0}})=0

for

0<i<\dim X_{0}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

the dg category of perfect complexes on

X_{0}
.
The deformation functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}\colon\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Set}

sends each local artinian
\mathbf{k}
-algebra

A\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

with residue field

\mathbf{k}

to the set of equivalence classes of
A
-deformations of

X_{0}

and each morphism

B\to A

in

\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

to the map

\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(B)\to\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(A)

induced by the base change.
Consider another deformation functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}\colon\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Set}

which sends each

A\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

to the set of isomorphism classes of Morita
A
-deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

and each morphism

B\to A

in

\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

to the map

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(B)\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(A)

induced by the derived dg functor

-\otimes^{L}_{B}A
.
Our first main result claims that
the deformation theory for

X_{0}

is equivalent to
that for

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

in the following sense.

Theorem 1.1
.

(Theorem
7.1
)

There is a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\zeta\colon\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

of deformation functors.

In particular,
Morita deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

is controlled by the Kodaira–Spencer differential graded Lie algebra.
To obtain

\zeta

we need to consider certain maximal partial curved dg deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})
.
Curved dg deformations of a dg category is a special case of
curved
A_{\infty}
-deformations of an
A_{\infty}
-category.
Let

(\mathfrak{a},\mu)

be a dg category over

\mathbf{R}\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

with a square zero extension

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbf{I}\to\mathbf{S}\to\mathbf{R}\to 0.

Choose generators

\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{l})

of

\mathbf{I}

regarded as a free
\mathbf{R}
-module of rank

l
.
By

[
Low08
, Theorem 4.11]

there is a bijection

(1.1)

\displaystyle H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{cdg}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S}),\ \phi\mapsto\mathfrak{a}_{\phi}=(\mathfrak{a}[\epsilon],\mu+\phi\epsilon)

where

\phi

is a Hochschild cocycle.
In other words,
curved dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{a}

are classified by the direct sum of the second Hochschild cohomology.

Assume that

\mathfrak{a}

is an
\mathbf{R}
-linear category.
We denote by

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{a})

the dg category of bounded below complexes of
\mathfrak{a}
-objects.
Then by

[
Low08
, Theorem 4.8]

the characteristic morphism

\displaystyle\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{a}}\colon H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}\to\mathfrak{Z}^{\bullet}K^{+}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}

maps

\phi\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}

to obstructions against deforming objects of

K^{+}(\mathfrak{a})

to objects of

K^{+}(\mathfrak{a}_{\phi})
.
In particular,
for each

C\in K^{+}(\mathfrak{a})

there exists a lift to

K^{+}(\mathfrak{a}_{\phi})

if and only if

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\phi)_{C}=0
.
The characteristic morphism

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{a}}

is induced by a
B_{\infty}
-section

\displaystyle\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{a}))^{\oplus l}

of the canonical projection,
which is a quasi-isomorphism of
B_{\infty}
-algebras

[
Low08
, Theorem 3.22]
.
Hence
(
1.1
)
induces another bijection

(1.2)

\displaystyle H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{cdg}_{\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{a})}(\mathbf{S}),\ \phi\mapsto\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{a})_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)}=(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{a})[\epsilon],\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\mu+\phi\epsilon)).

From the proof of

[
Low08
, Theorem 4.8]

it follows that

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\phi)_{C}=0

if and only if
the curvature element

(\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\mu+\phi\epsilon))_{0,C}

vanishes
for each

C\in\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{a})
.
Hence any full dg subcategory of

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{a})

spanned by object

C

with

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\phi)_{C}=0

dg deforms along the restriction of

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)
.

Let

X

be an
\mathbf{R}
-deformation of

X_{0}

and

X_{\phi}

its deformation along a cocycle

\phi\in HH^{2}(X)^{\oplus l}=H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})^{\oplus l}
.
The above argument can be adapted to our setting
so that
for each

E\in\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

the curvature element vanishes
if and only if
there exists a lift of

E\in\operatorname{Perf}(X)

to

\operatorname{Perf}(X_{\phi})
.
With a little more effort
one can apply

[
KL09
, Proposition 3.12]

to obtain

Theorem 1.2
.

(Theorem
6.8
)

There is a bijection

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)}(\mathbf{S})\to\operatorname{Def}^{cdg}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)}(\mathbf{S})

between
the set of isomorphism classes of Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformations
and
the set of isomorphism classes of curved dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformations
of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)
.

In particular,
giving curved dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

is equivalent to giving its Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformations.
Consider the dg category

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})

of perfect complexes on

X_{\phi}
.
It defines a Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)
.
Let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\phi)=\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}

be the image of the derived base change.
Then any
h
-flat resolution

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}

defines a dg deformation

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg,\Gamma}(X_{\phi})}

of

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)
,
where

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg,\Gamma}(X_{\phi})}

is the full dg subcategory of

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}

consisting the collection of one chosen lift of each object in

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)
.
There is an isomorphism

\displaystyle HH^{2}(X)^{\oplus l}\cong H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))^{\oplus l}

induced by the
B_{\infty}
-section

\displaystyle\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}\colon\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)))^{\oplus l}\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))))^{\oplus l},

where

\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))\subset\operatorname{Qch}(X)

is the full
\mathbf{R}
-linear subcategory of injective objects.
We denote by

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)

the image of

\phi

under the isomorphism,
which defines another dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)}

of

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)

along

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)
.
Deformations
and
taking the dg category of perfect complexes
intertwine in the following sense.

Theorem 1.3
.

(Theorem
6.9
)

There is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg,\Gamma}(X_{\phi})}\simeq\mathfrak{m}(\phi)_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)}

of dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)
.
In particular,
the Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})

defines a maximal partial dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformaiton of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

along

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)
.

This is the key to prove
Theorem
1.1
.
Unwinding Toda’s construction,
from

[
DLL17
, Theorem 5.12]

we obtain an equivalence

\operatorname{Qch}(X)_{\phi}\simeq\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi})

of Grothendieck abelian categories,
where

\operatorname{Qch}(X)_{\phi}

is the flat abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

along

\phi\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}_{ab}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))^{\oplus l}
.
Here,
we use the same symbol

\phi

to denote the image under the isomorphism

\displaystyle HH^{2}(X)^{\oplus l}\cong H^{2}\mathbf{C}_{ab}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))^{\oplus l}.

Via the induced equivalence

\displaystyle D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))\simeq D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)_{\phi})

we regard

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})

as the full dg subcategory of compact objects of

D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)_{\phi})
.
Based on the idea in the proof of

[
Low08
, Theorem 4.15]
,
we compare the dg structure on

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg,\Gamma}(X_{\phi})}

with that on

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)}
.

Working with Morita deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})
,
by

[
Coh
, Corollary 5.7]

we may apply

[
BFN10
, Theorem 1.2]

to obtain reductions.
In particular,
given a deformation

(X_{B},i_{B})\in\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(B)

and
a morphism

B\to A

in

\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}
,
there is a Morita equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{B})\otimes^{L}_{B}A\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{B})\otimes^{L}_{B}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(A)\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A})

of
A
-linear dg categories,
where

-\otimes^{L}_{B}-

is the derived pointwise tensor product of dg categories.
Further application of

[
BFN10
, Theorem 1.2]

shows that
any universal formal family for

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

is effective.
If we ignore the set theoretical issues,
the deformation functor

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

can naturally be extended to a functor defined on the category

\operatorname{Alg}^{aug}(\mathbf{k})

of augmented noetherian
\mathbf{k}
-algebras.
Although we do not know
whether it would be locally of finite presentation
(colimit preserving),
one can always construct a versal Morita deformation
via geometric realization in the following sense.

Corollary 1.4
.

(Corollary
7.4
)

Any effective universal formal family for

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

is algebraizable.
In particular,
an algebraization is given by

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})

where

(\operatorname{Spec}S,s,X_{S})

is a versal deformation of

X_{0}
.

The versal Morita deformation

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})

may be regarded as a family of Morita deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})
.
More generally,
for such a family determined by an enough nice
S
-scheme

X_{S}

we introduce its generic fiber as follows.

Definition 1.5
.

Let

X_{S}

be a smooth separated scheme
over a noetherian connected regular affine
\mathbf{k}
-scheme

\operatorname{Spec}S

whose closed points are

\mathbf{k}
-rational.
Then the

dg categorical generic fiber

of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})

is the Drinfeld quotient

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})/\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})_{0},

where

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})_{0}\subset\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})

is the full dg subcategory of perfect complexes with
S
-torsion cohomology.

We impose a technical assumption on

S

to include also the case
where

S

is a formal power series ring.
The Drinfeld quotient is a natural dg enhancement of the categorical generic fiber introduced in

[
Morb
]
,
which is in turn based on the categorical general fiber by Huybrechts–Macrì–Stellari

[
HMS11
]
.
Taking the generic fiber
and
the dg category of perfect complexes
intertwine in the following sense.

Proposition 1.6
.

(Proposition
7.5
)

Let

X_{S}

be a smooth separated scheme
over a noetherian connected regular affine
\mathbf{k}
-scheme

\operatorname{Spec}S

whose closed points are

\mathbf{k}
-rational.
Then there is a quasi-equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})/\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})_{0}\simeq_{qeq}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{Q(S)})

where

Q(S)

is the quotient field of

S

and

X_{Q(S)}

is the generic fiber of

X_{S}
.

Another goal of this paper is to show the uniqueness of versal Morita deformations
with respect to geometric realizations.
Recall that
up to étale neighborhood of the base
versal deformations
of

X_{0}

are unique.
Namely,
if

(\operatorname{Spec}S,s,X_{S})

(\operatorname{Spec}S^{\prime},s^{\prime},X_{S^{\prime}})

are two versal deformations of

X_{0}
,
then there is another versal deformation

(\operatorname{Spec}S^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime},X_{S^{\prime\prime}})

such that

(\operatorname{Spec}S^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime})

is an étale neighborhood of

s,s^{\prime}

in

\operatorname{Spec}S,\operatorname{Spec}S^{\prime}

respectively
and

X_{S^{\prime\prime}}

is the pullback along the corresponding étale morphisms.
The deformation functor

\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}

has an effective universal formal family

(R,\xi)
,
where

R

is a regular complete local noetherian
\mathbf{k}
-algebra.
Choose an isomorphism

R\cong\mathbf{k}\llbracket t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}\rrbracket

and
let

T=\mathbf{k}[t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}]

with

d=\dim_{\mathbf{k}}\text{H}^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X_{0}})
.
There is a filtered inductive system

\{R_{i}\}_{i\in I}

of finitely generated
T
-subalgebras of

R

whose colimit is

R
.
Then

(\operatorname{Spec}S,s)

is an étale neighborhood of

t

in

\operatorname{Spec}T

with

t

corresponding to the maximal ideal

(t_{1},\ldots,t_{d})\subset T
,
and

X_{S}

is the pullback of a deformation

X_{R_{j}}

of

X_{0}

along a first order approximation

R_{j}\to S

of

R_{j}\hookrightarrow R

for sufficiently large

j\in I
.
Hence the ambiguity of

X_{S}

stems from the choice of

j\in I
,
besides the choice of étale neighborhoods.

In

[
Mora
]

the author constructed smooth projective versal deformations

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

of

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

over a common nonsingular affine variety

\operatorname{Spec}S
,
while deforming simultaneously the Fourier–Mukai kernel connecting deformations of

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}
.
By
Corollary

1.4

we have two versal Morita deformations

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{S})

of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})
.
Theorem
1.1

together with the construction of versal deformations
suggests that

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{S})

should be determined only by
quasi-equivalent universal formal families
and
the same sufficiently large index

j\in I
.
From this observation we arrive at our second main result.

Theorem 1.7
.

(Theorem
8.3
)

Let

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

be derived-equivalent Calabi–Yau manifolds of dimension more than two
and

\mathcal{P}_{0}\in D^{b}(X_{0}\times_{\mathbf{k}}X^{\prime}_{0})

the Fourier–Mukai kernels.
Then there exists an index

j\in I

such that
for all

k\geq j

the integral functors

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}\colon\operatorname{Perf}(X_{R_{k}})\to\operatorname{Perf}(X^{\prime}_{R_{k}})

defined by deformations

\mathcal{P}_{k}

of

\mathcal{P}_{0}

are equivalences of triangulated categories of perfect complexes.
In particular,
the dg categories

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R_{k}}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{R_{k}})

of perfect complexes are quasi-equivalent.

Theorem
1.7

tells us that,
given two algebraic Morita deformations

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R_{k}}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{R_{k}})

geometrically realized by algebraic deformations

X_{R_{k}},X^{\prime}_{R_{k}}

of two derived-equivalent higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifolds

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}
,
if

X_{R_{k}},X^{\prime}_{R_{k}}

are enough close to effectivizations

X_{R},X^{\prime}_{R}

then

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R_{k}}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{R_{k}})

are Morita equivalent.
The base change along the homomorphism

R_{k}\to S

yields Morita equivalent versal Morita deformations

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{S})
.
In other words,
up to Morita equivalence
the versal Morita deformation

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})

does not depend on the choice of geometric realizations in the following sense.

Corollary 1.8
.

(Corollary
8.4
)

Let

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

be derived-equivalent Calabi–Yau manifolds of dimension more than two
and

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

their smooth projective versal deformations
over a common nonsingular affine
\mathbf{k}
-variety

\operatorname{Spec}S
.
Let

\{R_{i}\}_{i\in I}

be a filtered inductive system of finitely generated
T
-subalgebras

R_{i}\subset R

whose colimit is

R

with

\displaystyle T=\mathbf{k}[t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}],\ R\cong\mathbf{k}\llbracket t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}\rrbracket,\ d=\dim_{\mathbf{k}}H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X_{0}}).

Assume that

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

correspond to a first order approximation

R_{j}\to S

of

R_{j}\hookrightarrow R

for sufficiently large

j\in I
.
Then

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

are derived-equivalent.
In particular,
the dg categories

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{S})

of perfect complexes are quasi-equivalent.

The uniqueness result also holds for the dg categorical generic fiber.
Corollary

1.8

slightly improves

[
Mora
, Theorem 1.1]
,
which extends the derived equivalence from special to general fibers.
Here,
the advantage is that
we do not have to shrink the base

\operatorname{Spec}S

as long as the construction passes enough close to effectivizations.
In particular,
beginning with a pair of general fibers,
one obtains the derived equivalence of special fibers contained in the versal deformations.
Hence the above corollary partially provides a method for the opposite direction,
i.e.,
how to extend the derived equivalence from general to special fibers.

Notations and conventions
.

We work over an algebraically closed field
\bf{k}
of characteristic
0
throughout this paper.
For an augmented
\bf{k}
-algebra

A

by

\mathfrak{m}_{A}

we denote its augmentation ideal.
All higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifolds we treat
are
smooth projective
\mathbf{k}
-varieties

X_{0}

of dimension more than two with

\omega_{X_{0}}\cong\mathscr{O}_{X_{0}}

and

H^{i}(\mathscr{O}_{X_{0}})=0

for

0<i<\dim X_{0}
.

Acknowledgements
.

The author is supported by SISSA PhD scholarships in Mathematics.
The author would like to thank Yukinobu Toda for pointing out mistakes in earlier version.

2.
Hochschild cohomology of relatively smooth proper schemes

In this section,
we review various kinds of complexes
whose cohomology controls deformations of associated mathematical objects,
mainly following the exposition from

[
DLL17
, Section 2, 3]
.
We always assume that
all algebras have units,
morphism of algebras preserve units,
and
modules are unital.
In the sequel,
we fix
a local artinian
\mathbf{k}
-algebra

\mathbf{R}

with residue field

\mathbf{k}

and
its square zero extension

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbf{I}\to\mathbf{S}\to\mathbf{R}\to 0,

and
choose generators

\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{l})

of

\mathbf{I}

regarded as a free
\mathbf{R}
-module of rank

l
.
For smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-schemes,
we explain the correspondence between
its relative Hochschild cohomology
and
cohomology of the Gerstenharber–Shack complex
associated with its restricted structure sheaf.

2.1.
Relative Hochschild cohomology of schemes

Let

X

be a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme.
We denote by

\Delta_{\mathbf{R}}\colon X_{\mathbf{R}}\hookrightarrow X\times_{\mathbf{R}}X

the relative diagonal embedding.
The

relative Hochschild cohomology

is defined as the graded
\mathbf{R}
-algebra

\displaystyle HH^{\bullet}(X/\mathbf{R})=\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{X\times_{\mathbf{R}}X}(\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{\mathbf{R}}},\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{\mathbf{R}}})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{X}(\Delta^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{\mathbf{R}}},\mathscr{O}_{X}).

Here,
the multiplication in

HH^{\bullet}(X/\mathbf{R})

is given
by the composition in

D^{b}(X\times_{\mathbf{R}}X)
.
Then the natural map

\mathbf{R}\to\operatorname{End}_{X\times_{\mathbf{R}}X}(\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{\mathbf{R}}})

induces the
\mathbf{R}
-algebra structure.
There is a quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle\Delta^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{\mathbf{R}}}\cong\bigoplus_{i}\Omega^{i}_{X/\mathbf{R}}[i]

called the

relative Hochschild–Kostant–Rosenberg isomorphism
,
which induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{X}(\Delta^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{\mathbf{R}}},\mathscr{O}_{X})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{X}(\bigoplus_{i}\Omega^{i}_{X/\mathbf{R}}[i],\mathscr{O}_{X})\to\bigoplus_{i}H^{\bullet-i}(X,\wedge^{i}\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})

where

\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}}

is the relative tangent sheaf
and

\Omega_{X/\mathbf{R}}

is its dual.
We also call the compositions

\displaystyle\text{I}_{X/\mathbf{R}}^{\text{HKR}}\colon\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{X\times_{\mathbf{R}}X}(\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{\mathbf{R}}},\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{\mathbf{R}}})=HH^{n}(X/\mathbf{R})\to HT^{n}(X/\mathbf{R})=\bigoplus_{p+q=n}H^{p}(X,\wedge^{q}\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})

the relative Hochschild–Kostant–Rosenberg isomorphisms.

2.2.
Hochschild cohomology of algebras

Let

A=(A,m)

be an
\mathbf{R}
-algebra
and

M

an
A
-bimodule.
The

Hochschild complex

\mathbf{C}(A,M)

has

\mathbf{C}^{n}(A,M)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{R}}(A^{\otimes n},M)

as its
n
-th term
and

d^{n}_{Hoch}\colon\mathbf{C}^{n}(A,M)\to\mathbf{C}^{n+1}(A,M)
,
called the

Hochschild differential
,
as its differential
which is given by

\displaystyle d^{n}_{Hoch}(\phi)(a_{n},a_{n-1},\ldots,a_{0})=\

\displaystyle a_{n}\phi(a_{n-1},\ldots,a_{0})

\displaystyle+\sum^{n-1}_{i=0}(-1)^{i+1}\phi(a_{n},\ldots,a_{n-i}a_{n-i-1},\ldots,a_{0})

\displaystyle+(-1)^{n+1}\phi(a_{n},\ldots,a_{1})a_{0}.

A cochain

\phi\in\mathbf{C}^{n}(A,M)

is

normalized

if

\phi(a_{n-1},\ldots,a_{0})=0

whenever

a_{i}=1

for some

0\leq i\leq n-1
.
The normalized cochains form a subcomplex

\bar{\mathbf{C}}(A,M)

quasi-isomorphic to

\mathbf{C}(A,M)

via the inclusion.
When

M=A
,
we call

\mathbf{C}(A)=\mathbf{C}(A,A)

the

Hochschild complex

and

H^{n}\mathbf{C}(A)

the
n
-th

Hochschild cohomology

of

A
.
Note that
the multiplication

m

on

A

belongs to

\mathbf{C}^{2}(A)
.

The direct sum of the second normalized Hochschild cohomology of

A

classifies
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

A

up to equivalence.
Recall that
an

\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

A

is an
\mathbf{S}
-algebra

(\bar{A},\bar{m})=(A[\epsilon]=A\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S},m+\mathbf{m}\epsilon)

with

\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{C}^{2}(A)^{\oplus l}

such that
the unit of

\bar{A}

is the same as that of
A
.
Two deformations

(\bar{A},\bar{m}),(\bar{A}^{\prime},\bar{m}^{\prime})

are

equivalent

if there is an isomorphism of the form

1+\mathbf{g}\epsilon\colon\bar{A}\to\bar{A}^{\prime}

with

\mathbf{g}\in\mathbf{C}^{1}(A)^{\oplus l}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{alg}_{A}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

A
.
It is known that
there is a bijection

\displaystyle H^{2}\bar{\mathbf{C}}(A)^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{alg}_{A}(\mathbf{S}),\ \mathbf{m}\mapsto(A[\epsilon],m+\mathbf{m}\epsilon),\ \mathbf{m}\in Z^{2}\bar{\mathbf{C}}(A)^{\oplus l}.

2.3.
Simplicial cohomology of presheaves

Let

\mathfrak{U}

be a small category
and

\mathcal{N}(\mathfrak{U})

its simplicial nerve.
We write

\displaystyle\sigma=(d\sigma=U_{0}\xrightarrow{u_{1}}U_{1}\xrightarrow{u_{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{u_{p}}U_{p}\xrightarrow{u_{p+1}}U_{p+1}=c\sigma)

for a
(p+1)
-simplex

\sigma\in\mathcal{N}_{p+1}(\mathfrak{U})
.
Let

(\mathscr{F},f),(\mathscr{G},g)

be presheaves of
\mathbf{R}
-modules with restriction maps

f^{u}\colon\mathscr{F}(U)\to\mathscr{F}(V),g^{u}\colon\mathscr{G}(U)\to\mathscr{G}(V)

for

u\colon V\to U

in

\mathfrak{U}
.
We write

f^{\sigma}

for the map

f^{u_{p+1}\ldots u_{2}u_{1}}\colon\mathscr{F}(U_{p+1})\to\mathscr{F}(U_{0})
.
Consider a complex whose
p
-th term is

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{p}_{simp}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F})=\prod_{\tau\in\mathcal{N}_{p}(\mathfrak{U})}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{R}}\left(\mathscr{G}(c\tau),\mathscr{F}(d\tau)\right).

and
whose differential

d^{p}_{simp}

is defined as follows.
Recall that
we have the maps

\displaystyle\partial_{i}\colon\mathcal{N}_{p+1}(\mathfrak{U})\to\mathcal{N}_{p}(\mathfrak{U}),\ \sigma\mapsto\partial_{i}\sigma,

for

i=0,1,\ldots,p+1

given by

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\partial_{i}\sigma=(U_{0}\xrightarrow{u_{1}}\cdots U_{i-1}\xrightarrow{u_{i+1}u_{i}}U_{i+1}\xrightarrow{u_{i+1}}\cdots\xrightarrow{u_{p}}U_{p}\xrightarrow{u_{p+1}}U_{p+1}),\ i\neq 0,p+1,\\
\partial_{0}\sigma=(U_{1}\xrightarrow{u_{2}}U_{2}\xrightarrow{u_{3}}\cdots\xrightarrow{u_{p}}U_{p}\xrightarrow{u_{p+1}}U_{p+1}),\\
\partial_{p+1}\sigma=(U_{0}\xrightarrow{u_{1}}U_{1}\xrightarrow{u_{2}}\cdots\xrightarrow{u_{p}}U_{p}).\end{gathered}

Each
\partial_{i}
induces a map

\displaystyle d_{i}\colon\mathbf{C}^{p}_{simp}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F})\to\mathbf{C}^{p+1}_{simp}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F}),\ \phi=(\phi^{\tau})_{\tau}\mapsto d_{i}\phi=((d_{i}\phi)^{\sigma})_{\sigma}

given by

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}(d_{i}\phi)^{\sigma}=\phi^{\partial_{i}\sigma},\ i\neq 0,p+1,\\
(d_{0}\phi)^{\sigma}=f^{u_{1}}\circ\phi^{\partial_{0}\sigma},\\
(d_{p+1}\phi)^{\sigma}=\phi^{\partial_{p+1}\sigma}\circ g^{u_{p+1}}.\end{gathered}

Then one defines

\displaystyle d_{simp}=\sum^{p+1}_{i=0}(-1)^{i}d_{i}\colon\mathbf{C}^{p}_{simp}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F})\to\mathbf{C}^{p+1}_{simp}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F}).

When

\mathscr{G}

is the constant presheaf

\mathbf{R}
,
we call

H^{p}(\mathfrak{U},\mathscr{F})=H^{p}\mathbf{C}_{simp}(\mathscr{F})=H^{p}\mathbf{C}_{simp}(\mathbf{R},\mathscr{F})

the

simplicial presheaf cohomology

of
\mathscr{F}
.
A
(p+1)
-simplex

\sigma\in\mathcal{N}_{p+1}(\mathfrak{U})

is

degenerate

if

u_{i}=1_{U_{i}}

for some

1\leq i\leq p+1
.
A
p
-cochain

\phi=(\phi^{\tau})_{\tau}\in\mathbf{C}^{p}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F})

is

reduced

if

\phi^{\tau}=0

whenever
\tau
is degenerate.
All
0
-cochains are reduced by convention.
The reduced cohains are preserved by
d_{simp}

and
form a subcomplex

\mathbf{C}^{\prime}_{simp}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F})
,
which is quasi-isomorphic to

\mathbf{C}_{simp}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{F})

by

[
DLL17
, Proposition 2.9]
.

The direct sum of the first reduced simplicial presheaf cohomology of

\mathscr{F}

classifies
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathscr{F}

up to equivalence.
Recall that
an

\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

\mathscr{F}

is a presheaf of
\mathbf{S}
-modules

(\bar{\mathscr{F}},\bar{f})=(\mathscr{F}[\epsilon],f+\mathbf{f}\epsilon)

with

\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{C}^{1}_{simp}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F})^{\oplus l}
.
Two deformations

(\bar{\mathscr{F}},\bar{f}),(\bar{\mathscr{F}}^{\prime},\bar{f}^{\prime})

are

equivalent

if there is an isomorphism of the form

1+\mathbf{g}\epsilon

with

\mathbf{g}\in\mathbf{C}^{0}_{simp}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F})^{\oplus l}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{psh}_{\mathscr{F}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathscr{F}
.

Lemma 2.1
.

(
[
DLL17
, Proposition 2.11]
)

Let

(\mathscr{F},f)

be a presheaf of
\mathbf{R}
-modules.
Then there is a bijection

\displaystyle H^{1}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}_{simp}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{psh}_{\mathscr{F}}(\mathbf{S}),\ \phi\mapsto(\mathscr{F}[\epsilon],f+\mathbf{f}\epsilon),\ \mathbf{f}\in Z^{1}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}_{simp}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F})^{\oplus l}.

Another cocycle

\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\in Z^{1}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}_{simp}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F})^{\oplus l}

maps to an equivalent deformation
if and only if
there is an element

\mathbf{g}\in\mathbf{C}^{\prime 0}_{simp}(\mathscr{F},\mathscr{F})

satisfying

\mathbf{f}^{\prime}-\mathbf{f}=d_{simp}(\mathbf{g})
.

2.4.
Gerstenharber–Schack complexes

Let

\mathfrak{U}

be a small category
and

(\mathscr{A},m,f)

a presheaf of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras on

\mathfrak{U}
.
The

Gerstenharber–Schack complex

\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})

introduced in
[
GS88
]

is the total complex of the double complex
whose
(p,q)
-term
for

p,q\geq 0

is

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{p,q}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})=\prod_{\tau\in\mathcal{N}_{p}(\mathfrak{U})}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{R}}(\mathscr{A}(c\tau)^{\otimes q},\mathscr{A}(d\tau)),

where
we regard

\mathscr{A}(d\tau)

as an

\mathscr{A}(c\tau)
-bimodule
via

f^{\tau}\colon\mathscr{A}(c\tau)\to\mathscr{A}(d\tau)
.
When

q

is fixed,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{\bullet,q}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})=\mathbf{C}_{simp}(\mathscr{A}^{\otimes q},\mathscr{A})

endowed with the simplicial differential

d_{simp}

horizontally.
When

p

is fixed,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{p,\bullet}_{GS}=\prod_{\tau\in\mathcal{N}_{p}(\mathfrak{U})}\mathbf{C}\left(\mathscr{A}(c\tau),\mathscr{A}(d\tau)\right)

endowed with the product Hochschild differential

d_{Hoch}

vertically.
The differential

\displaystyle d^{n}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})\colon\mathbf{C}^{n}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})\to\mathbf{C}^{n+1}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})

is defined as

d^{n}_{GS}=(-1)^{n+1}d_{simp}+d_{Hoch}
.

A cochain

\phi=(\phi^{\tau})_{\tau}\in\mathbf{C}^{p,q}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})

is

normalized

if

\phi^{\tau}

is normalized for each
p
-simplex

\tau
,
and
it is

reduced

if

\phi^{\tau}=0

whenever

\tau

is degenerate.
The normalized cochains form a subcomplex

\bar{\mathbf{C}}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})

of

\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})

called the

normalized Hochschild complex

of

\mathscr{A}
,
and
the normalized reduced cochains
form a subcomplex

\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})

of

\bar{\mathbf{C}}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})

called the

normalized reduced Hochschild complex

of

\mathscr{A}
.
These three complexes are quasi-isomorphic
via the inclusions.
Eliminating the bottom row from

\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})
,
one obtains a subcomplex

\mathbf{C}_{tGS}(\mathscr{A})

called the

truncated Hochschild complex
.
There is a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbf{C}_{tGS}(\mathscr{A})\to\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})\to\mathbf{C}_{simp}(\mathscr{A})\to 0.

Since

\mathbf{R}

is commutative,
one can apply

[
DLL17
, Proposition 2.14]

to see that
the sequence splits
and
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})=\mathbf{C}_{tGS}(\mathscr{A})\oplus\mathbf{C}_{simp}(\mathscr{A}).

Similarly,
we have

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})=\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{tGS}(\mathscr{A})\oplus\mathbf{C}^{\prime}_{simp}(\mathscr{A}).

The direct sum of the second normalized reduced Gerstenharber–Schack cohomology of

\mathscr{A}

classifies twisted
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathscr{A}

up to equivalence.
Recall that
a

twisted presheaf

\mathscr{A}=(\mathscr{A},m,f,c,z)

of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras on

\mathfrak{U}

consists of the following data:

•

for each

U\in\mathfrak{U}

an
\mathbf{R}
-algebra

(\mathscr{A}(U),m^{U})
,

•

for each

u\colon V\to U

in

\mathfrak{U}

a homomorphism of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras

f^{u}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)\to\mathscr{A}(V)
,

•

for each pair

u\colon V\to U,\ v\colon W\to V

in

\mathfrak{U}

an invertible element

c^{u,v}\in\mathscr{A}(W)

satisfying for any

a\in\mathscr{A}(U)

\displaystyle c^{u,v}f^{v}(f^{u}(a))=f^{uv}(a)c^{u,v}.

•

for each

U\in\mathfrak{U}

an invertible element

z^{U}\in\mathscr{A}(U)

satisfying for any

a\in\mathscr{A}(U)

\displaystyle z^{U}a=f^{1_{U}}(a)z^{U}.

Moreover, these data must satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}c^{u,vw}c^{v,w}=c^{uv,w}f^{w}(c^{u,v}),\\
c^{u,1_{V}}z^{V}=1,\ \ c^{1_{U},u}f^{u}(z^{U})=1\end{gathered}

for each triple

u\colon V\to U,\ v\colon W\to V,\ w\colon T\to W

in

\mathfrak{U}
.
When

c^{u,v},z^{U}

are central for all

u,v

and

U
,
we call

\mathscr{A}

a

twisted presheaf with central twists

and
denote by

|\mathscr{A}|=(\mathscr{A},m,f)

the underlying ordinary presheaf.

For twisted sheaves

\mathscr{A}=(\mathscr{A},m,f,c,z),\mathscr{A}^{\prime}=(\mathscr{A}^{\prime},m^{\prime},f^{\prime},c^{\prime},z^{\prime})

of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras on

\mathfrak{U}
,
a

morphism

(g,h)\colon\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{A}^{\prime}

consists of the following data:

•

for each

U\in\mathfrak{U}

a homomorphism of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras

g^{U}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)\to\mathscr{A}^{\prime}(U)
,

•

for each

u\colon V\to U

in

\mathfrak{U}

an invertible element

h^{u}\in\mathscr{A}^{\prime}(V)
.

Moreover, these data must satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}m^{\prime V}(g^{V}f^{u}(a),h^{u})=m^{\prime V}(h^{u},f^{\prime u}(g^{U}(a))),\\
m^{\prime W}(h^{uv},c^{\prime u,v})=m^{\prime W}(g^{W}(c^{u,v}),h^{v},f^{\prime v}(h^{u})),\\
m^{\prime U}(h^{1_{U}},z^{\prime U})=g^{U}(z^{U})\end{gathered}

for all

u,v

and

a\in\mathscr{A}(U)
.
Morphisms can be composed
and
the identity

1_{\mathscr{A}}

is given by

g^{U}=1_{\mathscr{A}(U)}

and

h^{u}=1\in\mathscr{A}(V)
.
When

g^{U}

are isomorphisms of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras for all

U
,
we call

(g,h)
an

isomorphism
.
Any twisted presheaf

(\mathscr{A},m,f,c,z)

is isomorphic to the one of the form

(\mathscr{A}^{\prime},m^{\prime},f^{\prime},c^{\prime},1)
.

Let

\mathscr{A}=(\mathscr{A},m,f,c)

be a twisted presheaf of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras on

\mathfrak{U}
.
A

twisted
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

\mathscr{A}

is a twisted presheaf

\displaystyle\overline{\mathscr{A}}=(\overline{\mathscr{A}},\overline{m},\overline{f},\overline{c})=(\mathscr{A}[\epsilon],m+\mathbf{m}\epsilon,f+\mathbf{f}\epsilon,c+\mathbf{c}\epsilon)

of
\mathbf{S}
-algebras
such that

(\overline{\mathscr{A}}(U),\overline{m}^{U})

is an
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

(\mathscr{A}(U),m^{U})

for each

U\in\mathfrak{U}

with

\displaystyle(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},\mathbf{c})\in\mathbf{C}^{2}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}=\mathbf{C}^{0,2}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}\oplus\mathbf{C}^{1,1}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}\oplus\mathbf{C}^{2,0}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}.

Two twisted deformations

(\overline{\mathscr{A}},\overline{m},\overline{f},\overline{c}),(\overline{\mathscr{A}^{\prime}},\overline{m^{\prime}},\overline{f^{\prime}},\overline{c^{\prime}})

are

equivalent

if there is an isomorphism of the form

(1+\mathbf{g}\epsilon,1+\mathbf{h}\epsilon)

with

\displaystyle(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\in\mathbf{C}^{1}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}=\mathbf{C}^{0,1}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}\oplus\mathbf{C}^{1,0}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}.

We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{tw}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of twisted
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathscr{A}
.

When

c=1
,
A

presheaf
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

\mathscr{A}

is a twisted
\mathbf{S}
-deformation with

\mathbf{c}=0
.
Two presheaf deformations

(\overline{\mathscr{A}},\overline{m},\overline{f}),(\overline{\mathscr{A}^{\prime}},\overline{m^{\prime}},\overline{f^{\prime}})

are

equivalent

if there is an isomorphism of the form

1+\mathbf{g}\epsilon

with

\mathbf{g}\in\mathbf{C}^{0,1}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{psh}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of presheaf
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathscr{A}
.

Lemma 2.2
.

(
[
DLL17
, Theorem 2.21]
)

Let

(\mathscr{A},m,f)

be a presheaf of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras on

\mathfrak{U}
.
Then there is a bijection

\displaystyle H^{2}\bar{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{tw}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathbf{S}),\ (\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},\mathbf{c})\mapsto(\mathscr{A}[\epsilon],m+\mathbf{m}\epsilon,f+\mathbf{f}\epsilon,c+\mathbf{c}\epsilon),\ (\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},\mathbf{c})\in Z^{2}\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}.

Another cocycle

(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{f}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})\in Z^{2}\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}

maps to an equivalent deformation
if and only if
there is an element

(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})\in\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime 1}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}

satisfying

(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},\mathbf{f}^{\prime},\mathbf{c}^{\prime})-(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},\mathbf{c})=d_{GS}(\mathbf{g},\mathbf{h})
.
In particular,
there is a bijection

\displaystyle H^{2}\bar{\mathbf{C}^{\prime}}_{tGS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{psh}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathbf{S}),\ (\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f})\mapsto(\mathscr{A}[\epsilon],m+\mathbf{m}\epsilon,f+\mathbf{f}\epsilon).

2.5.
Hodge decomposition

In the group algebra

\mathbb{Q}S_{n}

of the
n
-th symmetric group

S_{n}
,
there is a collection of pairwise orthogonal idempotents

e_{n}(r)

for

1\leq r\leq n

such that

\sum^{n}_{r=1}e_{n}(r)=1

[
GS87
, Theorem 1.2]
.
Put

e_{n}(0)=0
,

e_{0}(0)=1\in\mathbb{Q}
,
and

e_{n}(r)=0

for

r>n
.
Let

A

be a commutative
\mathbf{R}
-algebra
and

M

a symmetric
A
-bimodule.
The subcomplex

\mathbf{C}(A,M)_{r}\subset\mathbf{C}(A,M)

whose
n
-th term is

\mathbf{C}(A,M)e_{n}(r)

gives rise to a Hodge decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(A,M)=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbf{C}(A,M)_{r}.

Assume that

\mathscr{A}

is a presheaf of commutative
\mathbf{R}
-algebras.
Then the Hodge decomposition

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}(\mathscr{A}(c\tau)^{\otimes q},\mathscr{A}(d\tau))=\bigoplus^{q}_{r=0}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathscr{A}(c\tau)^{\otimes q},\mathscr{A}(d\tau))_{r}

induces a decomposition of the double complex

\mathbf{C}^{n-q,q}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})

preserved by

d_{Hoch}

and

d_{simp}
.
Hence one obtains a

Hodge decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})_{r}.

Taking cohomology yields a decomposition for

H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})
.

Assume further the following.

•

The restriction map

f^{u}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)\to\mathscr{A}(V)

is a flat epimorphism of rings for each

u\colon V\to U
.

•

The algebra

\mathscr{A}(U)

is essentially of finite type
and
smooth
\mathbf{R}
-algebra for each

U
.

Recall that
a homomorphism of rings is called an epimorphism
if it is an epimorphism in the category of noncommutative rings.
For instance,
every surjective homomorphism of commutative rings is an epimorphism.
Then one obtains the

presheaf of differential

\Omega_{\mathscr{A}}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A})

with

\Omega_{\mathscr{A}}(U)=\Omega_{\mathscr{A}(U)/\mathbf{R}}
.
Since we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathscr{A}(V)\otimes_{\mathscr{A}(U)}\Omega_{\mathscr{A}(U)}\cong\Omega_{\mathscr{A}(V)}

by the first additional assumption,
the induced restriction maps

\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{A}(U)/\mathbf{R}}\to\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{A}(V)/\mathbf{R}}

yield the

tangent presheaf

\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{A}}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A})

with

\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{A}}(U)=\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{A}(U)/\mathbf{R}}
.
From the second additional assumption it follows that
antisymmetrizations

\wedge^{n}\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{A}(U)}\to H^{n}\mathbf{C}(\mathscr{A}(U))

are isomorphisms.

Lemma 2.3
.

(
[
DLL17
, Theorem 3.3]
)

Let

\mathfrak{U}

be a small category
and

\mathscr{A}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{CAlg}(\mathbf{R})

a presheaf of commutative algebras.
Assume that
the algebra

\mathscr{A}(U)

is essentially of finite type
and
smooth
\mathbf{R}
-algebra for each

U
.
Assume further that
the restriction map

f^{u}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)\to\mathscr{A}(V)

is a flat epimorphism of rings for each

u\colon V\to U
.
Then there is a canonical bijection

(2.1)

\displaystyle H^{n}\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})=\bigoplus^{n}_{r=0}H^{n}\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})_{r}\cong\bigoplus_{p+q=n}H^{p}(\mathfrak{U},\wedge^{q}\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{A}}).

From the proof,
one sees that
any Gerstenharber–Shack cohomology class

\mathfrak{c}_{GS}

is represented by a normalized reduced

decomposable

cocycle

\theta_{0,n},\theta_{1,n-1},\ldots,\theta_{n,0}

in the sense that

\theta_{n-r,r}

are reduced
and
belong to

\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{n-r,r}(\mathscr{A})_{r}
.
Each

\theta_{n-r,r}=(\theta^{\tau}_{n-r,r})_{\tau\in\mathcal{N}_{n-r}(\mathfrak{U})}

lifts to a unique simplicial cocycle

\Theta_{n-r,r}=(\Theta^{\tau}_{n-r,r})_{\tau\in\mathcal{N}_{n-r}(\mathfrak{U})}\in\mathbf{C}^{\prime n-r}_{simp}(\wedge^{r}\mathscr{T}_{\mathscr{A}})
.
The image of

\mathfrak{c}_{GS}

under the bijection is the cohomology class

\mathfrak{c}_{simp}

represented by

\Theta_{0,n},\Theta_{1,n-1},\ldots,\Theta_{n,0}
.

2.6.
Comparison with relative Hochschild cohomology

We describe the relation between
simplicial cohomology
and

\mathrm{\breve{C}ech}
cohomology
for a presheaf

\mathscr{F}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbf{R})

in the case where

\mathfrak{U}

is a poset with binary meets.
We use the symbol

\cap

to denote meets in

\mathfrak{U}
.
For a
p
-sequence

\tau=(U^{\tau}_{0},U^{\tau}_{1},\ldots,U^{\tau}_{p})\in\mathfrak{U}^{p+1}

we denote by

\cap\tau

the meet of all coordinates of

\tau
.
The

\mathrm{\breve{C}ech}
complex

\breve{\mathbf{C}}(\mathscr{F})

of

\mathscr{F}

has

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{p}(\mathscr{F})=\prod_{\tau\in\mathfrak{U}^{p+1}}\mathscr{F}(\cap\tau)

as the
p
-th term with the usual differentials.
A

\mathrm{\breve{C}ech}
cochain

\psi=(\psi^{\tau})_{\tau}

is

alternating

if

\psi^{\tau}=0

whenever two coordinates of

\tau

are equal,
and

\psi^{\tau s}=(-1)^{\operatorname{sign}(s)}\psi^{\tau}

for any permutation

s

of the set

\{0,1,\ldots,p\}
.
Here,
we regard

\tau

as a set theoretic map

\{0,1,\ldots,p\}\to\mathfrak{U}
.
The alternating

\mathrm{\breve{C}ech}
cochains
form a subcomplex

\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}(\mathscr{F})

which is quasi-isomorphic to

\breve{\mathbf{C}}(\mathscr{F})

via the inclusion.

To a
p
-sequence

\tau
,
one associates a
p
-simplex

\displaystyle\bar{\tau}=(d\bar{\tau}=\cap^{p}_{j=0}U^{\tau}_{j}\to\cap^{p}_{j=1}U^{\tau}_{j}\to\cdots\to\cap^{p}_{j=p-1}U^{\tau}_{j}\to U^{\tau}_{p}=c\bar{\tau}).

Conversely,
any
p
-simplex

\mu

can be regarded as a
p
-sequence

\tilde{\mu}

by forgetting the inclusions.
Define a map

\delta_{i}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{p+1}\to\mathfrak{U}^{p}

for

i=1,\ldots,p

as

\displaystyle\delta_{i}\tau=(U^{\tau}_{0},\ldots,U^{\tau}_{i-2},U^{\tau}_{i-1}\cap U^{\tau}_{i},U^{\tau}_{i+1},\ldots,U^{\tau}_{p}).

There are morphisms

\iota\colon\mathbf{C}^{\prime}_{simp}\to\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}(\mathscr{F}),\ \pi\colon\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}(\mathscr{F})\to\mathbf{C}^{\prime}_{simp}

of complexes defined as

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\iota(\phi)^{\tau}=\sum_{s\in\mathfrak{S}_{p+1}}(-1)^{\operatorname{sign}(s)}\phi^{\overline{\tau s}},\ \phi\in\mathbf{C}^{\prime}_{simp}(\mathscr{F}),\ \tau\in\mathfrak{U}^{p+1},\\
\pi(\psi)^{\mu}=\psi^{\tilde{\mu}},\ \psi\in\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}(\mathscr{F}),\ \mu\in\mathcal{N}_{p}(\mathfrak{U}),\end{gathered}

which induce mutually inverse isomorphisms between

H^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{U},\mathscr{F})

and

\breve{H}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{U},\mathscr{F})

[
DLL17
, Lemma 3.9]
.

Now,
for a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme

X

we give an alternative description of the relative Hochschild cohomology.
As explained above,
we have

HH^{\bullet}(X/\mathbf{R})\cong HT^{\bullet}(X/\mathbf{R})
.
Choose a finite affine open cover

\mathfrak{U}

closed under intersections.
By definition

\mathfrak{U}

is semi-separating,
i.e.,

\mathfrak{U}

is closed under finite intersections.
For every quasi-coherent sheaf

\mathscr{F}

on

X
,
one can apply

[
DLL17
, Lemma 3.9]

and
Leray’s theorem

[
Har77
, Theorem 4.5]

to obtain

(2.2)

\displaystyle H^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{U},\mathscr{F}|_{\mathfrak{U}})\cong\breve{H}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{U},\mathscr{F}|_{\mathfrak{U}})\cong\breve{H}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{U},\mathscr{F})\cong H^{\bullet}(X,\mathscr{F})

with

\mathscr{F}|_{\mathfrak{U}}

regarded as a presheaf on

\mathfrak{U}
.
Since

X

is smooth over

\mathbf{R}

and
open immersions

V\hookrightarrow U

in

\mathfrak{U}

define flat epimorphisms

\mathscr{O}_{X}(U)\to\mathscr{O}_{X}(V)
,
combining
(
2.1
)
with
(
2.2
),
we obtain

Lemma 2.4
.

(
[
DLL17
, Corollary 3.4]
)
Let

X

be a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme
with a finite affine open cover

\mathfrak{U}

closed under intersections.
Let

\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}},\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}}|_{\mathfrak{U}}

be the restrictions of

\mathscr{O}_{X},\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}}

to

\mathfrak{U}

respectively.
Then there are canonical isomorphisms

(2.3)

\displaystyle H^{n}\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})=\bigoplus^{n}_{r=0}H^{n}\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})_{r}\cong\bigoplus_{p+q=n}H^{p}(\mathfrak{U},\wedge^{q}\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}}|_{\mathfrak{U}})\cong HH^{n}(X/\mathbf{R}),

where the first isomorphism respects the Hodge decomposition.

3.
Deformations of relatively smooth proper schemes

In this section,
we review the classical deformation theory of schemes.
The main reference is

[
Har10
]
.
We explain how deformations of smooth proper
\mathbf{k}
-varieties extend to Toda’s construction

[
Tod09
]
,
which can be adapted to deformations of relatively smooth proper schemes along square zero extensions in a straightforward way.
When the original scheme is a deformation of a higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifold,
Toda’s construction gives the category of quasi-coherent sheaves on deformations of the Calabi–Yau manifold.

3.1.
Deformations of schemes

Let

X

be a

\mathbf{k}
-scheme
and

A

a local artinian
\mathbf{k}
-algebra
with residue field

\mathbf{k}
.
An

A
-deformation

of

X

is a pair

(X_{A},i_{A})
,
where

X_{A}

is a scheme flat over

A

and

i_{A}\colon X\hookrightarrow X_{A}

is a closed immersion
such that
the induced map

X\to X_{A}\times_{A}\mathbf{k}

is an isomorphism.
Two deformations

(X_{A},i_{A}),(X^{\prime}_{A},i^{\prime}_{A})

are

equivalent

if there is an
A
-isomorphism

X_{A}\to X^{\prime}_{A}

compatible with

i_{A},i^{\prime}_{A}
.
The deformation functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}_{X}\colon\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Set}

sends each

A\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

to the set of equivalence classes of
A
-deformations of

X
.

Assume that

X

is projective over

\mathbf{k}
.
Then

\operatorname{Def}_{X}

satisfies Schlessinger’s criterion
and
there exists
a

miniversal formal family

(R,\xi)

for

\operatorname{Def}_{X}
,
where

R

is a complete local noetherian
\mathbf{k}
-algebra with residue field

\mathbf{k}
,
and

\xi=\{\xi_{n}\}_{n}

belongs to the limit

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{Def}}_{X}(R)=\displaystyle\lim_{\longleftarrow}\operatorname{Def}_{X}(R/\mathfrak{m}^{n}_{R})

of the inverse system

\displaystyle\cdots\to\operatorname{Def}_{X}(R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{n+2})\to\operatorname{Def}_{X}(R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{n+1})\to\operatorname{Def}_{X}(R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{n})\to\cdots

induced by the natural quotient maps

R/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}_{R}\to R/\mathfrak{m}^{n}_{R}
.
The formal family

\xi

corresponds to a natural transformation

\displaystyle h_{R}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{k}-alg}}(R,-)\to\operatorname{Def}_{X},

which sends each

g\in h_{R}(A)

factorizing through

R\to R/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}_{R}\xrightarrow{g_{n}}A

to

\operatorname{Def}_{X}(g_{n})(\xi_{n})
.

Let

X_{n}

be the schemes which define

\xi_{n}
.
There is a noetherian formal scheme

\mathscr{X}

over

R

such that

X_{n}\cong\mathscr{X}\times_{R}R/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}_{R}

for each

n
.
By abuse of notation,
we use the same symbol

\xi

to denote

\mathscr{X}
.
Thus any scheme which defines an equivalence class

[X_{A},i_{A}]

can be obtained as the pullback of

\xi

along some morphism of noetherian formal schemes

\operatorname{Spec}A\to\operatorname{Spf}R
.
If

X

has no infinitesimal automorphisms
which restrict to the identity of

X
,
then every equivalence class

[X_{A},i_{A}]

becomes just a deformation

(X_{A},i_{A})

and
we have a natural isomorphism

h_{R}\cong\operatorname{Def}_{X}
.
In this case,
we call

\operatorname{Def}_{X}

prorepresentable

and

(R,\xi)

a

universal formal family

for

\operatorname{Def}_{X}
.

3.2.
Algebraization

Let

X

be a projective
\mathbf{k}
-variety.
We call a miniversal formal family

(R,\xi)

for

\operatorname{Def}_{X}

effective

when there exists a scheme

X_{R}

flat and of finite type over

R

whose formal completion along the closed fiber

X

is isomorphic to

\xi
.
By

[
GD61
, Theorem III5.4.5]

the family

(R,\xi)

is effective
if deformations of any invertible sheaf on

X

are unobstructed.
Note that
this is the case,
for instance,
if we have

H^{2}(\mathscr{O}_{X})=0
.
From the proof,
one sees that

X_{R}

is projective over

R
.
We will call such

X_{R}

an

effectivization

of

\xi
.

The deformation functor

\operatorname{Def}_{X}

can naturally be extended to a functor defined on the category

\operatorname{Alg}^{aug}(\mathbf{k})

of augmented noetherian
\mathbf{k}
-algebras.
By abuse of notation,
we use the same symbol

\operatorname{Def}_{X}

to denote the extended functor,
which sends each

(\mathbf{P},\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{P}})\in\operatorname{Alg}^{aug}(\mathbf{k})

to the set of equivalence classes of deformations over

(\mathbf{P},\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{P}})
.
Since the functor

\operatorname{Def}_{X}

is locally of finite presentation,
by

[
Art69b
, Theorem 1.6]

the miniversal formal family is

algebraizable
,
i.e.,
there exists a triple

(S,s,X_{S})

where

S

is an algebraic
\mathbf{k}
-scheme
with a distinguished closed point

s\in S
,
and

X_{S}

is a flat and of finite type
S
-scheme
whose formal completion along the closed fiber

X

over

s

is isomorphic to

\xi
.
We call the scheme

X_{S}

a

versal deformation

over

S
.
When there exists a versal deformation,
we say that
the miniversal formal family

(R,\xi)

is

algebraizable
.

3.3.
Deformations of higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifolds

Here,
we focus on a special case
where
several interesting results hold.
Let

X_{0}

be a Calabi–Yau manifold of dimension more than two.
Then the deformation functor

\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}

has an effective universal formal family

(R,\xi)
.
Since deformations of Calabi–Yau manifolds are unobstructed,
the complete local noetherian ring

R

is regular
and
we have

\displaystyle R\cong\mathbf{k}\llbracket t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}\rrbracket

with

d=\dim_{\mathbf{k}}H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X_{0}})
.
Every
A
-deformation of

X_{0}

is smooth projective over

A
,
as we have

Lemma 3.1
.

(
[
Mora
, Lemma 2.4]
)

The effectivization

X_{R}

for

(R,\xi)

is regular
and
the morphism

\pi_{R}\colon X_{R}\to\operatorname{Spec}R

is smooth of relative dimension

\dim X_{0}
.

Now,
we briefly recall the construction of

X_{S}
.
Consider the extended functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}\colon\operatorname{Alg}^{aug}(\mathbf{k})\to\operatorname{Set}.

Fix an isomorphism

R\cong\mathbf{k}\llbracket t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}\rrbracket
.
Let

T=\mathbf{k}[t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}]

and

t\in\operatorname{Spec}T

be the closed point corresponding to maximal ideal

(t_{1},\ldots,t_{d})
.
There is a filtered inductive system

\{R_{i}\}_{i\in I}

of finitely generated
T
-subalgebras of

R

whose colimit is

R
.
Since

\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}

is locally of finite presentation,

[X_{R},i_{R}]

is the image of some element

\zeta_{i}\in\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}\left(\left(R_{i},\mathfrak{m}_{R_{i}}\right)\right)

by the canonical map

\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}\left(\left(R_{i},\mathfrak{m}_{R_{i}}\right)\right)\to\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(R)
.
By

[
Art69a
, Corollary 2.1]

there exists an étale neighborhood

\operatorname{Spec}S

of

t

in

\operatorname{Spec}T

with first order approximation

\varphi\colon R_{i}\to S

of

R_{i}\hookrightarrow R
.
Let

[X_{S},i_{S}]

be the image of

\zeta_{i}

by the map

\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(\varphi)
.
From miniversality of

(R,\xi)
,
it follows that
the formal completion of

X_{S}

along the closed fiber

X_{0}

over

s\in\operatorname{Spec}S

is isomorphic to

\xi
,
where

s

is the distinguished closed point mapping to

t
.
By construction,

\operatorname{Spec}S

is a nonsingular affine
\mathbf{k}
-variety
and

X_{S}

is flat of finite type over

S
.
Exploiting inherited
smoothness
and
projectivity
of

X_{R}

by terms in the projective system

\{X_{R_{i}}\}_{i\in I}

for sufficiently large indices,
one can show

Lemma 3.2
.

(
[
Mora
, Lemma 2.3]
)

Let

X_{0}

be a Calabi–Yau manifold of dimension more than two.
Then there exists a nonsingular affine
\mathbf{k}
-variety

\operatorname{Spec}S

with a versal deformation

X_{S}

which is smooth projective of relative dimension

\dim X_{0}

over

S
.

3.4.
T^{i}
functors

Let

A\to B

be a ring homomorphism
and

M

a
B
-module.
Define the groups

T^{i}(B/A,M)

for

i=0,1,2

as the
i
-th cohomology of the complex

\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(L_{\bullet},M)
,
where

\displaystyle L_{\bullet}=L_{2}\xrightarrow{d_{2}}L_{1}\xrightarrow{d_{1}}L_{0}

is the

cotangent complex
.
When the ring homomorphism

A\to B

is a surjection with kernel

J
,

L_{\bullet}

is given as follows.
Choose a free
A
-module

P

and
a surjection

j\colon P\to J

with kernel

Q
.
We have two short exaxt sequences

\displaystyle 0\to J\to A\to B\to 0,\ 0\to Q\to P\xrightarrow{j}J\to 0.

Let

P_{0}

be the submodule of

P

generated by all relations of the form

j(a)b-j(b)a

for

a,b\in P
.
From

j(P_{0})=0

it follows

P_{0}\subset Q
.
Take

L_{2}=Q/P_{0}
,

L_{1}=P\otimes_{A}B
,
and

L_{0}=0
.
Note that

L_{2}

is a
B
-module.
Indeed,
for

a\in J

there is an element

a^{\prime}\in P

such that

a=j(a^{\prime})
.
Then we have

ax\equiv j(x)a^{\prime}\equiv 0

modulo

P_{0}

for

x\in Q
.
The differential

d_{2}\colon L_{2}\to L_{1}

is the map induced by the inclusion

Q\to P

and

d_{1}=0
.
By

[
Har10
, Lemma 3.2]

the
B
-modules

T^{i}(B/A,M)

do not depend on the choice of

P

up to isomorphism.

Lemma 3.3
.

(
[
Har10
, Theorem 3.4]
)

Let

A\to B

be a homomorphism of rings.
Then

\displaystyle T^{i}(B/A,-)\colon\operatorname{Mod}(B)\to\operatorname{Mod}(B),\ i=0,1,2

define covariant additive functor.

The construction of

T^{i}

functors is compatible with localization
and
one obtains sheaves

\mathscr{T}^{i}(X/Y,\mathscr{F}),\ i=0,1,2

for any morphism of
\mathbf{k}
-schemes

f\colon X\to Y

and
any quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-module

\mathscr{F}

[
Har10
, Exercise 3.5]
.
The sections of

\mathscr{T}^{i}(X/Y,\mathscr{F})

over

U=\operatorname{Spec}B\subset f^{-1}(V)

give

T^{i}(B/A,M)
,
where

V=\operatorname{Spec}A\subset Y

and

\mathscr{F}|_{U}=\tilde{M}

for some
B
-module

M
.

3.5.
Infinitesimal extension of schemes

Let

X

be a scheme of finite type over

\mathbf{R}

and

\mathscr{F}

a coherent sheaf on

X
.
An

infinitesimal extension

of

X

by

\mathscr{F}

is a pair

(Y,\mathfrak{I})
,
where

Y

is a scheme of finite type over

\mathbf{S}

and

\mathfrak{I}\subset\mathscr{O}_{Y}

is an ideal sheaf
such that

\mathfrak{I}^{2}=0
,

(Y,\mathscr{O}_{Y}/\mathfrak{I})\cong(X,\mathscr{O}_{X})
,
and

\mathfrak{I}\cong\mathscr{F}

as an
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-module.
Two infinitesimal extensions

(Y,\mathfrak{I}),(Y^{\prime},\mathfrak{I}^{\prime})

are

equivalent

if there is an isomorphism

\mathscr{O}_{Y}\to\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime}}

which makes the diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 158.81335pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 158.81335pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

commute.
The trivial extension is a sheaf

\mathscr{O}_{X}\oplus\mathscr{F}

of abelian group endowed with the ring structure by

\displaystyle(a,f)\cdot(a^{\prime},f^{\prime})=(aa^{\prime},af^{\prime}+a^{\prime}f).

Assume that

X

is smooth proper over

\mathbf{R}
.
Recall that
for the square zero extension

(3.1)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbf{I}\to\mathbf{S}\to\mathbf{R}\to 0

we have

\mathbf{I}\cong\mathbf{R}^{\oplus l}

as an
\mathbf{R}
-module.
Note that
given an infinitesimal extension

(Y,\mathfrak{I})

of

X

by

\mathscr{O}^{\oplus l}_{X}
,

Y

is flat over

\mathbf{S}

since

\mathscr{O}_{X}

is flat over

\mathbf{R}

and

\mathscr{O}^{\oplus l}_{X}\to\mathscr{O}_{Y}

is injective

[
Har10
, Proposition 2.2]
.
Below,
we collect fundamental results necessary to describe the relation between
deformations
and
extensions
of schemes.

Lemma 3.4
.

(
[
Har10
, Exercise 4.7]
)

Let

X

be a smooth
\mathbf{R}
-scheme
and

g\colon Y\to X

a morphism from an affine
\mathbf{R}
-scheme

Y

to

X
,
and

i_{\mathbf{S}}\colon Y\hookrightarrow Y^{\prime}

an
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

Y
.
Then

g

lifts to a morphism

h\colon Y^{\prime}\to X

such that

h\circ i_{\mathbf{S}}=g
.

Lemma 3.5
.

(
[
Har10
, Proposition 3.6, Exercise 5.2]
)

Let

A\to B

be a homomorphism of rings,

M

a
B
-module,
and

B^{\prime}

an extension of

B

by

M
.
Then the automorphism group of

B^{\prime}

is given by

\displaystyle T^{0}(B/A,M)=\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(\Omega_{B/A},M)=\operatorname{Der}_{A}(B,M).

Lemma 3.6
.

(
[
Har10
, Theorem 5.1]
)

Let

A\to B

be a homomorphism of rings
and

M

a
B
-module.
Then there is a bijection
between
the set of equivalence classes of

B

by

M

and
the group

T^{1}(B/A,M)
.
The trivial extension corresponds to the zero element.

Lemma 3.7
.

(
[
Har10
, Theorem 4.11]
)

Let

f\colon X\to Y

be an of finite type morphism of noetherian
\mathbf{k}
-schemes.
Then

f

is smooth
if and only if
it is flat
and

\mathscr{T}^{1}(X/Y,\mathscr{F})=0

for every coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-module

\mathscr{F}
.

Now,
we are ready to show relevant results to our setting.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let

X

be a smooth separated
\mathbf{R}
-scheme.
Then every
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

(Y,j)

of

X

is locally trivial.

Proof.

Since

Y

is flat over

\mathbf{S}
,
(
3.1
)
induces a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathscr{O}^{\oplus l}_{X}\to\mathscr{O}_{Y}\to\mathscr{O}_{X}\to 0,

which defines
equivalence classes of infinitesimal extensions of coordinate rings on affine open subschemes of

X
.
Let

i_{\mathbf{S}}\colon\operatorname{Spec}B\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Spec}A

be the induced deformation of any affine open subscheme.
Since

\operatorname{Spec}B

is smooth over

\mathbf{R}
,
by
Lemma
3.4

the identity

\operatorname{Spec}B\to\operatorname{Spec}B

lifts to a morphism

h\colon\operatorname{Spec}A\to\operatorname{Spec}B

such that

h\circ i_{\mathbf{S}}=\operatorname{id}
.
The lift

h

induces a morphism

\operatorname{Spec}A\to\operatorname{Spec}B\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}

of schemes flat of finite type over

\mathbf{S}
.
Now,
one can apply

[
Har10
, Exercise 4.2]

to see that
the induced morphism is an isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 3.9
.

Let

X

be a smooth separated
\mathbf{R}
-scheme.
Then there is a bijection

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}_{X}(\mathbf{S})\cong H^{1}(X,\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})^{\oplus l},

where

\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}}

is the relative tangent sheaf on

X
.

Proof.

Let

(Y,j)

be an
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

X
.
Take an affine open cover

\mathfrak{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}

of

X
.
By
Lemma
3.8

we may assume that
the induced deformations

U_{i}\hookrightarrow V_{i}\subset Y

are trivial.
Choose isomorphisms

\varphi_{i}\colon U_{i}\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}\to V_{i}

and
write

\varphi_{ij}

for the composition

\varphi^{-1}_{j}\circ\varphi_{i}

on

U_{ij}\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}
,
where the intersections

U_{ij}=U_{i}\cap U_{j}

are again affine
as

X

is separated over

\mathbf{R}
.
Let

\operatorname{Spec}B=U_{ij}

and

\operatorname{Spec}A=U_{ij}\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}
.
According to
Lemma
3.5
,
the set of automorphisms of extensions

\tilde{A}

of

\tilde{B}

by

\tilde{B}^{\oplus l}

bijectively corresponds to

T^{0}(B/\mathbf{R},B^{\oplus l})\cong\operatorname{Hom}(\Omega_{B/\mathbf{R}},B)^{\oplus l}
.
Then

\{\varphi_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I}

define a collection

\{\theta_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I}

of sections

\theta_{ij}\in H^{0}(U_{ij},\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})^{\oplus l}

on

U_{ij}
.
One checks

\theta_{ij}+\theta_{jk}+\theta_{ki}=0

and

\{\theta_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I}

is a
\rm{\breve{C}ech}

1
-cocycle
with respect to

\mathfrak{U}
.
Another choice of isomorphisms

\varphi^{\prime}_{i}\colon U_{i}\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}\to V_{i}

yields a collection

\{\varphi^{\prime}_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I}

of automorphisms
such that

\varphi^{\prime}_{ij}=(\varphi^{-1}_{j}\circ\varphi^{\prime}_{j})^{-1}\circ\varphi_{ij}\circ(\varphi^{-1}_{i}\circ\varphi^{\prime}_{i})
.
It follows

\theta^{\prime}_{ij}=\theta_{ij}+\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j}

for some sections

\alpha_{i}\in H^{0}(U_{i},\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})^{\oplus l}
.
Thus we obtain a well defined assignment

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}_{X}(\mathbf{S})\to H^{1}(X,\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})^{\oplus l},\ [Y,j]\mapsto\{\theta_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I},

as

\{\theta_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I}

does not depends on
\mathfrak{U}
.

Conversely,
an element of

H^{1}(X,\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})^{\oplus l}

can be represented by
\rm{\breve{C}ech}

2
-cocycle

\{\theta_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I}

with respect to

\mathfrak{U}
.
As explained above,
the cocycle define automorphisms of the trivial deformations

U_{ij}\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}
,
which glue to yield a global deformation

(Y^{\prime},j^{\prime})

of

X
.
Clearly,
this construction gives the inverse assignment.
∎

Corollary 3.10
.

There is a canonical bijection between

\operatorname{Def}_{X}(\mathbf{S})

and
the set of equivalence classes of infinitesimal extensions of

X

by

\mathscr{O}^{\oplus l}_{X}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma
3.6

and
Lemma
3.7

any extension of

X

by

\mathscr{O}^{\oplus l}_{X}

is locally trivial.
Then
due to
Lemma
3.5

the claim follows from the same argument as in the proof of
Proposition

3.9
.
∎

3.6.
Toda’s construction

Let

X_{0}

be a smooth projective
\mathbf{k}
-variety.
In

[
Tod09
]

Toda constructed the category of

\tilde{\alpha}
-twisted sheaves on the noncommutative scheme

(X_{0},\mathscr{O}^{(\beta,\gamma)}_{X_{0}})

over the ring of dual numbers for each

[\phi_{0}]\in HT^{2}(X_{0})

represented by a cocycle

\displaystyle(\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},\gamma_{0})\in H^{2}(\mathscr{O}_{X_{0}})\oplus H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X_{0}})\oplus H^{0}(\wedge^{2}\mathscr{T}_{X_{0}}).

Here,
we apply his idea to a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme

X

and

[\phi]\in HT^{2}(X/\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}

represented by

\displaystyle(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\left((\alpha^{1},\ldots,\alpha^{l}),(\beta^{1},\ldots,\beta^{l}),(\gamma^{1},\ldots,\gamma^{l})\right)\in H^{2}(\mathscr{O}_{X})^{\oplus l}\oplus H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})^{\oplus l}\oplus H^{0}(\wedge^{2}\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})^{\oplus l}.

Take a finite affine open cover

\mathfrak{U}=\{U_{i}\}^{N}_{i=1}

of

X

and
let

\mathfrak{U}\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}=\{U_{i}\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}\}^{N}_{i=1}
.
Consider the extension of

X

by

\mathscr{O}^{\oplus l}_{X}

whose equivalence class corresponds to

\beta
,
giving rise to an classical
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

X_{\beta}

of

X

by Corollary

3.10
.
We modify the multiplication on

\mathscr{O}_{X}\oplus\mathcal{C}(\mathfrak{U},\mathscr{O}^{\oplus l}_{X})

as

\displaystyle(a,\{b^{1}_{i}\},\ldots,\{b^{l}_{i}\})*_{\gamma}(c,\{d^{1}_{i}\},\ldots,\{d^{l}_{i}\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(ac,\{ad^{1}_{i}+b^{1}_{i}c+\gamma^{1}_{i}(a,c)\},\ldots,\{ad^{l}_{i}+b^{l}_{i}c+\gamma^{l}_{i}(a,c)\}),

where

\gamma^{j}\colon\mathscr{O}_{X}\times\mathscr{O}_{X}\to\mathscr{O}_{X}

are regarded as bidifferential operators.
We denote by

X_{(\beta,\gamma)}=(X_{\beta},\mathscr{O}^{\gamma}_{X_{\beta}})

the resulting noncommutative
\mathbf{S}
-scheme.
By the standard argument,
one sees that
up to isomorphism
the scheme does not depend on the choice of

\mathfrak{U}

and

\mathrm{\breve{C}ech}
representative of

\gamma
.
From

\alpha

one obtains an element

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}=\{1-\alpha^{1}_{i_{0}i_{1}i_{2}}\epsilon_{1}-\cdots-\alpha^{l}_{i_{0}i_{1}i_{2}}\epsilon_{l}\}_{i_{0}i_{1}i_{2}}\in\mathbf{C}^{2}(X_{\beta},Z(\mathscr{O}^{\gamma}_{X_{\beta}})^{*}),

which is a cocycle.
Then
\tilde{\alpha}
-twisted sheaves on

X_{(\beta,\gamma)}

form a category

\operatorname{Mod}(X_{(\beta,\gamma)},\tilde{\alpha})
.
By the similar argument to

[
Căl00
, Lemma 1.2.3, 1.2.8]
,
one sees that
up to equivalence
the category does not depend on the choice of

\mathfrak{U}

and

\mathrm{\breve{C}ech}

representative of

\alpha
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Qch}(X,\phi)

the full abelian subcategory
spanned by
\tilde{\alpha}
-twisted quasi-coherent sheaves.

Assume that

X

is an
\mathbf{R}
-deformation of a higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifold.
Then we have

\displaystyle HT^{2}(X/\mathbf{R})=H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}}).

In this case,
Toda’s construction yields nothing but the category of quasi-coherent sheaves on the
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

X

along

\phi
.

Proposition 3.11
.

Let

X_{0}

be a Calabi–Yau manifold with

\dim X_{0}>2

and

X

an
\mathbf{R}
-deformation of

X_{0}
.
Then for every cocycle

\phi\in HT^{2}(X/\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}=H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})^{\oplus l}

we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Qch}(X,\phi)=\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}),

where

X_{\phi}

is the
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

X

along

\phi
.

4.
Deformations of linear and abelian categories

In this section,
we review the deformation theory of linear and abelian categories
developed by
Lowen
and
van den Bergh in

[
LV06b
]
,
introducing the fundamental notion of flatness.
As explained there,
when considering only flat nilpotent deformations over a certain class of rings,
one avoids any set theoretic issue by choosing sufficiently large universe.
Moreover,
both
linear
and
abelian deformations
reduce to strict linear deformations
without affecting the deformation theory up to equivalence.
Along square zero extensions,
flat deformations of
linear
and
abelian categories
are controlled by the second Hochschild cohomology of the corresponding linear categories.

4.1.
Universes

First,
we need to extend the Zermelo–Fraenkel axioms of the set theory
to avoid foundational issues in the deformation theory of categories.
One solution is the theory of universes
introduced by Grothendieck
with the axiom of choice
and
the universe axiom.
A

universe

is a set

\mathcal{U}

with the following properties:

•

if

x\in\mathcal{U}

and

y\in x

then

y\in\mathcal{U}
,

•

if

x,y\in\mathcal{U}

then

\{x,y\}\in\mathcal{U}
,

•

if

x\in\mathcal{U}

then
the powerset

\mathcal{P}(x)

of
x
is in
\mathcal{U}
,

•

if

(x_{i})_{i\in I}

is a family of objects of

\mathcal{U}

indexed by an element of

\mathcal{U}

then

\bigcup_{i\in I}x_{i}\in\mathcal{U}
,

•

if

U\in U

and

f\colon U\to\mathcal{U}

is a function
then

\{f(x)\ |\ x\in U\}\in\mathcal{U}
.

A universe

\mathcal{U}

containing

\mathbb{N}

is a model for the Zermelo–Fraenkel axioms of the set theory
with the axiom of choice.
Since the known nonempty universe only contains finite sets,
the

universe axiom

is added,
which imposes every set to be an element of a universe.

Consider the category

\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Set}

whose objects are elements of

\mathcal{U}

and
whose morphisms are ordinary maps between sets in

\mathcal{U}
.
The category

\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Cat}

consists of categories
whose objects and morphisms respectively form sets
being an element of

\mathcal{U}
.
Similarly,
by requiring the underlying sets to belong to

\mathcal{U}
,
we obtain categories with a structure
such as
abelian groups and rings.
We call a category

\mathcal{U}
-small

when its objects and morphisms respectively form sets
with the same cardinality
as an element of

\mathcal{U}
,
and

essentially
\mathcal{U}
-small

when it is equivalent to a
\mathcal{U}
-small category.
A

\mathcal{U}
-category

is a category
whose
\operatorname{Hom}
-sets have the the same cardinality
as an element of

\mathcal{U}
.
The axiom of choice allows us
to replace a
\mathcal{U}-
category

\mathscr{C}

by an equivalent category

\mathscr{C}^{\prime}

with

\operatorname{Ob}(\mathscr{C})=\operatorname{Ob}(\mathscr{C}^{\prime})

and

\mathscr{C}^{\prime}(C,D)\in\mathcal{U}

for all

C,D\in\operatorname{Ob}(\mathscr{C}^{\prime})
.
When

\mathscr{C}

is abelian
with a generator,
we call

\mathscr{C}

\mathcal{U}
-Grothendieck
.
Every
\mathcal{U}
-Grothendieck category

\mathscr{C}

admits
\mathcal{U}
-small colimits
and

\mathcal{U}
-small filtered colimits are exact in

\mathscr{C}
.

Throughout the paper,
we work with a fixed universe

\mathcal{U}

containing

\mathbb{N}
.
All the notion based on universes will be with respect to

\mathcal{U}

and
all the related symbols will be tacitly prefixed by

\mathcal{U}
.
By taking

\mathcal{U}

sufficiently large,
we may assume all categories to be small.
Unless otherwise specified,
we will be free from any issue caused by the choice of universes.

4.2.
Flatness

The notion of flatness for abelian categories
was introduced in

[
LV06b
]
.
For a while,
we temporarily drop the assumption on

\mathbf{R}

and

\mathbf{S}

imposed at the beginning of
Section
2
.
Let

\mathbf{R}

be a commutative ring.
An

\mathbf{R}
-linear category

is a category

\mathfrak{a}

enriched over the abelian category

\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbf{R})

of
\mathbf{R}
-modules.
Namely,

\mathfrak{a}

is a pre-additive category
together with a ring map

\rho\colon\mathbf{R}\to\operatorname{Nat}(1_{\mathfrak{a}},1_{\mathfrak{a}})

inducing a ring map

\rho_{A}\colon\mathbf{R}\to\mathfrak{a}(A,A)

for each

A\in\mathfrak{a}

and
an action of

\mathbf{R}

on each Hom-set.

Assume that

\mathbf{R}

is coherent,
i.e.,
any finitely generated ideal is finitely presented as an
\mathbf{R}
-module.
Typical examples are given by noetherian rings.
We denote by

\operatorname{mod}(\mathbf{R})

the full abelian subcategory of finitely presented
\mathbf{R}
-modules.
Let

\mathscr{C}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category.
We call an object

C\in\mathscr{C}

flat

if the natural finite colimit preserving functor

(-)\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}C\colon\operatorname{mod}(\mathbf{R})\to\mathscr{C}

is exact,
and

coflat

if the natural finite limit preserving functor

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{R}}(-,C)\colon\operatorname{mod}(\mathbf{R})\to\mathscr{C}

is exact.

An
\mathbf{R}
-linear category

\mathfrak{a}

is

flat

if its Hom-sets are flat
\mathbf{R}
-modules.
Namely,
the functors

-\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathfrak{a}(A,A^{\prime})\colon\operatorname{mod}(\mathbf{R})\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbf{R})

are exact for all

A,A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{a}
.
An
\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category

\mathscr{C}

is

flat

if for each

Y\in\operatorname{mod}(\mathbf{R})

the functor

\operatorname{Tor}^{\mathbf{R}}_{1}(Y,-)\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}

is co-effaceble,
i.e.,
for each

C\in\mathscr{C}

there is an epimorphism

f\colon C^{\prime}\to C

with

\operatorname{Tor}^{\mathbf{R}}_{1}(Y,f)=0

[
LV06b
, Proposition 3.1]
.
Here,

\operatorname{Tor}^{\mathbf{R}}_{i}(Y,-)

is the left derived functor of the finite colimit preserving functor

Y\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}(-)\colon\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{C}
.
The flatness has the following characterizations

[
LV06b
, Proposition 3.3, 3.4, 3.6, 3.7]
.

•

\mathscr{C}
is flat if and only if
\mathscr{C}^{op}
is flat.

•

\mathscr{C}
is flat if and only if all injectives in
\mathscr{C}
are coflat.

•

\mathscr{C}
is flat if and only if
\operatorname{Ind}(\mathscr{C})
is flat.

•

\mathfrak{a}
is flat if and only if the abelian category
\operatorname{Mod}(\mathfrak{a})
is flat.

Here,

\operatorname{Ind}(\mathscr{C})

is the category of ind-objects,
i.e.,
the full subcategory of

\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{C})

consisting of left exact functors,
where

\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{C})

is the category of covariant additive functors from

\mathscr{C}

to the category

\operatorname{Ab}

of abelian groups.
Note that
we are assuming all categories to be small in our fixed universe

\mathcal{U}
.

4.3.
Base change

We fix a homomorphism

\theta\colon\mathbf{S}\to\mathbf{R}

of commutative rings.
For an
\mathbf{R}
-module

M
,
by

\overline{M}

we denote

M

regarded as an
\mathbf{S}
-module via

\theta
.
Let

\mathfrak{a}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear category.
We have the category

\overline{\mathfrak{a}}

with

\operatorname{Ob}(\overline{\mathfrak{a}})=\operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak{a})

and

\overline{\mathfrak{a}}(A,A^{\prime})=\overline{\mathfrak{a}(A,A^{\prime})}
.
For an
\mathbf{S}
-linear category

\mathfrak{b}
,
we denote by

\mathfrak{b}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}

the
\mathbf{R}
-linear category
with

\operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak{b}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R})=\operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak{b})

and

(\mathfrak{b}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R})(B,B^{\prime})=\mathfrak{b}(B,B^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}
.
The functor

(-)\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}

is left adjoint to

\overline{(-)}

in the sense that
there is a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Add}(\mathbf{R})(\mathfrak{b}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R},\mathfrak{a})}\simeq\operatorname{Add}(\mathbf{S})(\mathfrak{b},\overline{\mathfrak{a}})

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear categories,
where

\operatorname{Add}(\mathbf{S})

is the category of
\mathbf{S}
-linear functors.

Let

(\mathfrak{b},\rho)

be an
\mathbf{S}
-linear category.
We have the category

\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{R}}

of
\mathbf{R}
-linear objects
whose objects are pairs

(B,\varphi)

where

B\in\mathfrak{b}

and

\varphi\colon\mathbf{R}\to\mathfrak{b}(B,B)

is a ring map with

\varphi\circ\theta=\rho_{B}
,
and
whose morphisms are those of

\mathfrak{b}

compatible with the ring maps.
An object

B\in\mathfrak{b}

belongs to

\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{R}}

if and only if

1_{B}

is annihilated by the kernel of

\theta
.
Taking
\mathbf{R}
-linear objects defines a functor

(-)_{\mathbf{R}}\colon\mathfrak{b}\to\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{R}}
,
which is right adjoint to

\overline{(-)}

in the sense that
there is a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Add}(\mathbf{R})(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}_{\mathbf{R}})}\simeq\operatorname{Add}(\mathbf{S})(\overline{\mathfrak{a}},\mathfrak{b})

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear categories.
If

\mathscr{D}

is an
\mathbf{S}
-linear abelian category,
then

\mathscr{D}_{\mathbf{R}}

is also abelian
and
by

[
LV06b
, Proposition 4.2]

the forgetful functor

\mathscr{D}_{\mathbf{R}}\to\mathscr{D}

is exact.
From

\left(\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbf{S})\right)_{\mathbf{R}}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbf{R})
,
it follows

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Add}(\mathbf{R})(\mathfrak{a},\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbf{R}))}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}(\mathfrak{a})

for any
\mathbf{R}
-linear category

\mathfrak{a}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

(
[
LV06b
, Proposition 4.4(1)]
)

Let

\mathfrak{b}

be an
\mathbf{S}
-linear category.
Then there is an equivalence

\operatorname{Mod}(\mathfrak{b}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R})\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathfrak{b})_{\mathbf{R}}

of
\mathbf{R}
-linear categories
which makes the diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 30.63197pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.63197pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
