commutes,
where
the left vertical arrow is the dual to

\mathfrak{b}\to\mathfrak{b}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}

and
the right vertical arrow is the forgetful functor.

4.4.
Deformations of linear categories

Let

\mathfrak{a}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear category.
A

linear
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

\mathfrak{a}

is an
\mathbf{S}
-linear category

\mathfrak{b}

together with an
\mathbf{S}
-linear functor

\mathfrak{b}\to\overline{\mathfrak{a}}

inducing an equivalence

\mathfrak{b}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathfrak{a}
.
Two deformations

f\colon\mathfrak{b}\to\overline{\mathfrak{a}},\ f^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}\to\overline{\mathfrak{a}}

are

equivalent

if there is an equivalence

\Phi\colon\mathfrak{b}\to\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear categories
such that

f^{\prime}\circ\Phi

is natural isomorphic to

f
.
When

\mathfrak{b}

is flat over

\mathbf{R}
,
we call the deformation

\mathfrak{b}

flat
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of flat linear
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{a}
.
The notation will be justified below with respect to the choice of universe.
When

\mathfrak{b}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathfrak{a}

is an isomorphism,
we call the deformation

\mathfrak{b}

strict
.
Two strict linear deformations

f\colon\mathfrak{b}\to\overline{\mathfrak{a}},\ f^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}\to\overline{\mathfrak{a}}

are

equivalent

if there is an isomorphism

\Phi\colon\mathfrak{b}\to\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear categories
such that

f^{\prime}\circ\Phi=f
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{s-lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of strict flat linear
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{a}
.
Also this notation will be justified below.

4.5.
Deformations of abelian categories

Let

\mathscr{C}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category.
An

abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

\mathscr{C}

is an
\mathbf{S}
-linear abelian category

\mathscr{D}

together with an
\mathbf{S}
-linear functor

\overline{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathscr{D}

inducing an equivalence

\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}_{\mathbf{R}}
.
When

\mathscr{D}

is flat over

\mathbf{R}
,
we call the deformation

\mathscr{D}

flat.
Two deformations

g\colon\overline{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathscr{D},g^{\prime}\colon\overline{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathscr{D}^{\prime}

are

equivalent

if there is an equivalence

\Psi\colon\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{D}^{\prime}

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear abelian categories
such that

\Psi\circ g^{\prime}

is natural isomorphic to

g
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of flat abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathscr{C}
.
The notation will be justified below
with respect to the choice of universe.
When

\mathscr{C}\to\mathscr{D}_{\mathbf{R}}

is an isomorphism,
we call the deformation

\mathscr{D}

strict
.
Two strict abelian deformations

g\colon\overline{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathscr{D},g^{\prime}\colon\overline{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathscr{D}^{\prime}

are

equivalent

if there is an isomorphism

\Psi\colon\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{D}^{\prime}

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear abelian categories
such that

\Psi\circ g^{\prime}=g
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{s-ab}_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of strict flat abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathscr{C}
.
Also this notation will be justified below.

Assume that

\theta\colon\mathbf{S}\to\mathbf{R}

is a homomorphism of coherent commutative rings
with
\mathbf{R}
being finitely presented as an
\mathbf{S}
-module.
Then the bifunctors

\displaystyle(-)\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}(-)\colon\mathscr{D}\times\operatorname{mod}(\mathbf{S})\to\mathscr{D},\ \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{S}}(-,-)\colon\operatorname{mod}(\mathbf{S})\times\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{D}

yield respectively left and right adjoint

\displaystyle(-)\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\colon\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{D}_{\mathbf{R}}\simeq\mathscr{C},\ \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{S}}(\mathbf{R},-)\colon\mathscr{D}\to\mathscr{D}_{\mathbf{R}}\simeq\mathscr{C}

to the natural inclusion functor

\mathscr{C}\simeq\mathscr{D}_{\mathbf{R}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}

[
LV06b
, Proposition 4.3]
.
They agree with the adjoints in the Section

4.3
.

4.6.
Flat nilpotent deformations of categories

Assume further that

\theta

is surjective.
Then for an
\mathbf{S}
-linear abelian category

\mathscr{D}

the forgetful functor

\mathscr{D}_{\mathbf{R}}\to\mathscr{D}

is fully faithful.
When the kernel

I=\ker\theta

is nilpotent,
we call both linear and abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformations

nilpotent
.
From now on,
we restrict our attention to flat nilpotent deformations.
The following properties of
\mathbf{R}
-linear category

\mathfrak{a}

and

\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category

\mathscr{C}

are respectively preserved under flat nilpotent linear and abelian deformations

[
LV06b
, Proposition 6.7, 6.9, Theorem 6.16, 6.29, 6.36]
.

•

\mathfrak{a},\mathscr{C}

are essentially small.

•

\mathscr{C}

has enough injectives.

•

\mathscr{C}

is a Grothendieck category.

•

\mathscr{C}

is a locally coherent Grothendieck category.

Here,
we call

\mathscr{C}

locally coherent Grothendieck

when it is Grothendieck
and
generated by a small abelian subcategory of finitely presented objects.

4.7.
Deformation pseudofunctors

In order to be careful about our choices of universes,
we temporarily make them explicit in the notation.
Let

\mathcal{U}

be a universe containing the field

\mathbf{k}
.
We denote by

\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}

the category whose objects are coherent commutative
\mathcal{U}
-rings
and
whose morphisms are surjective ring maps
with finitely generated nilpotent kernels.
We are interested in the category

\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}
.
Fix some other universe
\mathcal{W}
.
A

deformation pseudofunctor

is a pseudofunctor

D\colon\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}\to\mathcal{W}-\operatorname{Gd}
.
Two deformation pseudofunctors

D,D^{\prime}

are

equivalent

if there is a pseudonatural transformation

\mu\colon D\to D^{\prime}

such that
for each

\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}

we have an equivalence

D(\mathbf{R})\to D^{\prime}(\mathbf{R})

of categories.
For any enlargement

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}

of

\mathcal{U}
,
the canonical functor

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}\to\mathcal{U}^{\prime}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}

is an equivalence of categories

[
LV06b
, Proposition 8.1]
.
Thus the deformation pseudofunctor is independent of the choice of

\mathcal{U}
up to equivalence.

Let

\mathfrak{a}

be a flat
\mathbf{k}
-linear
\mathcal{U}
-category
and

\mathscr{C}

a flat
\mathbf{k}
-linear abelian
\mathcal{U}
-category.
Fix a universe

\mathcal{V}

such that

\mathfrak{a},\mathscr{C}

are essentially
\mathcal{V}
-small
and

\mathcal{U}\in\mathcal{V}
.
For

\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}

we consider the groupoid

\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{R})

whose objects are flat linear
\mathbf{R}
-deformations of
\mathfrak{a}

belonging to

\mathcal{V}
,
and
whose morphisms are equivalences of deformations
up to natural isomorphism of functors.
Also we consider the groupoid

\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathbf{R})

whose objects are flat abelian
\mathbf{R}
-deformations of

\mathscr{C}

belonging to
\mathcal{V}
,
and
whose morphisms are equivalences of deformations
up to natural isomorphism of functors.
Enlarging the universe

\mathcal{W}

if necessary,
we may assume that

\mathcal{V}\in\mathcal{W}

and
we obtain deformation pseudofunctors

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}},\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}\colon\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}\to\mathcal{W}-\operatorname{Gd}.

The universe

\mathcal{W}

is a purely technical device
which guarantees

\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}},\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}

taking values in categories.
Moreover,
whether two deformation pseudofunctors are equivalent
is preserved under enlargement of

\mathcal{W}
.
On the other hand,
by

[
LV06b
, Proposition 8.3]

for any enlargement

\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}

of

\mathcal{V}
,
the canonical pseudonatural transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}\to\mathcal{V}^{\prime}-\operatorname{def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}},\ \mathcal{V}-\operatorname{def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathcal{V}^{\prime}-\operatorname{def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}

define equivalences of deformation pseudofunctors.

In summary,
as long as we consider flat nilpotent deformations,
the choice of universe does not affect deformation pseudofunctors
up to equivalence.
Thus we simply write

\operatorname{def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}},\operatorname{def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}

for deformation pseudofunctors.
Since they have small skeletons

[
LV06b
, Theorem 8.4, 8.5]
,
we also write

\operatorname{Def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}},\operatorname{Def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}

for deformation functors

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{Def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}},\mathcal{V}-\operatorname{Def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}\colon\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}\to\mathcal{W}-\operatorname{Set}

which take values in sets.

Finally,
we collect relevant results on deformations of linear and abelian categories.

Lemma 4.2
.

(
[
LV06b
, Theorem 8.16]
)

Let

\mathbf{S}\to\mathbf{R}

be a morphism in

\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}

and

\mathfrak{a}

an essentially small flat
\mathbf{R}
-linear category.
Then there is a bijection

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})\to\operatorname{Def}^{ab}_{\operatorname{Mod}(\mathfrak{a})}(\mathbf{S}),\ \mathfrak{b}\mapsto\operatorname{Mod}(\mathfrak{b}).

In particular,
deformations of a module category are module categories.

Lemma 4.3
.

(
[
LV06b
, Theorem 8.17]
)

Let

\mathbf{S}\to\mathbf{R}

be a morphism in

\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}

and

\mathscr{C}

an essentially small flat
\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category with enough injectives.
Then there is a bijection

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{lin}_{\operatorname{Inj}(\mathscr{C})}(\mathbf{S})\to\operatorname{Def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathbf{S}),\ \mathfrak{j}\mapsto(\operatorname{mod}(\mathfrak{j}))^{op}.

Lemma 4.4
.

(
[
LV06b
, Proposition B.3]
)

Let

\mathbf{S}\to\mathbf{R}

be a morphism in

\mathcal{U}-\operatorname{Rng}^{0}/\mathbf{k}

and

\mathfrak{a}

an essentially small flat
\mathbf{R}
-linear category.
Then the map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{s-lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})\to\operatorname{Def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})

induced by the canonical pseudofunctor

\displaystyle\operatorname{def}^{s-lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}\to\operatorname{def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}

is bijective.

Now,
let again

\mathbf{R}

be the fixed local artinian
\mathbf{k}
-algebra
with residue field

\mathbf{k}
,
the square zero extension

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbf{I}\to\mathbf{S}\to\mathbf{R}\to 0,

and
the chosen generators

\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{l})

of

\mathbf{I}

regarded as a free
\mathbf{R}
-module of rank

l
.

Lemma 4.5
.

(
[
Low08
, Proposition 4.2]
)

Let

(\mathfrak{a},m)

be a flat
\mathbf{R}
-linear category with compositions

m
.
Then there is a bijection

(4.1)

\displaystyle H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{lin}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S}),\ \phi\mapsto(\mathfrak{a}[\epsilon],m+\phi\epsilon),\ \phi\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}.

Another cocycle

\phi^{\prime}\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}

maps to an isomorphic linear deformation
if and only if
there is an element

h\in\mathbf{C}^{1}(\mathfrak{a})

satisfying

\phi^{\prime}-\phi=d_{m}(h)
.

Lemma 4.6
.

(
[
LV06a
, Theorem 3.1]
)

Let

\mathscr{C}

be a flat
\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category.
Then there is a bijection

(4.2)

\displaystyle H^{2}\mathbf{C}_{ab}(\mathscr{C})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{ab}_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathbf{S}).

Here,

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

is the Hochschild object associated with

\mathfrak{a}
.
The compositions

m

is an element of

\displaystyle\prod_{A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}\in\mathfrak{a}}[\mathfrak{a}(A_{1},A_{2})\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{1}),\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{2})]^{0}

rather than the ring map

\rho

defining the
\mathbf{R}
-linear structure.
For an
\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category

\mathscr{C}
,
the associated Hochschild object is defined as

\mathbf{C}_{ab}(\mathscr{C})=\mathbf{C}_{sh}(\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Inj}(\mathscr{C})))
,
where

\mathbf{C}_{sh}(\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Inj}(\mathscr{C})))

is the Shukla complex associated with

\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Inj}(\mathscr{C}))
.
Note that
we have

H^{*}\mathbf{C}_{sh}(\mathfrak{a})=H^{*}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})
.
We will review the definitions in Section
6
.

4.8.
Examples

Let

X

be a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme.
Since it is noetherian,
the category

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

of quasi-coherent sheaves on

X

has enough injectives.
We denote by

\mathfrak{i}=\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))

the full
\mathbf{R}
-linear subcategory of injective objects.
Since

X

is flat separated,
by

[
DLL17
, Proposition 4.28, 4.30(2)]

the
\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

is flat.
From

[
LV06b
, Proposition 2.9(6)]

it follows that
the
\mathbf{R}
-linear category

\mathfrak{i}

is flat.
Then by
Lemma
4.5

and
Lemma
4.6

or
Lemma
4.3

both
flat linear
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{i}

and
flat abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

are classified by

H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})^{\oplus l}
.

5.
The category of quasi-coherent sheaves

In this section,
we review an alternative description of Toda’s construction
in terms of
the descent category of the category of twisted quasi-coherent presheaves
over
the restricted structure sheaf,
following the exposition from

[
DLL17
, Section 4,5]
.
It follows that,
for square zero extension of relatively smooth
\mathbf{R}
-schemes,
Toda’s construction coincides with the deformation of the category of quasi-coherent sheaves along the corresponding Hochschild cocycle.
As a consequence,
deforming the category of the quasi-coherent sheaves
is equivalent to
deforming the complex structure
for higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifolds.
In particular,
deformations of the category of quasi-coherent sheaves are given by the category of quasi-coherent sheaves on deformations.

5.1.
Descent categories

Let

\mathfrak{U}

be a small category
and

\operatorname{Cat}(\mathbf{R})

the category of small
\mathbf{R}
-linear categories and
\mathbf{R}
-linear functors.
A

prestack

\mathscr{A}

is a pseudofunctor

\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Cat}(\mathbf{R})

consists of the following data:

•

for each

U\in\mathfrak{U}

an
\mathbf{R}
-linear category

\mathscr{A}(U)
,

•

for each

u\colon V\to U

in

\mathfrak{U}

an
\mathbf{R}
-linear functor

f^{u}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)\to\mathscr{A}(V)
,

•

for each pair

u\colon V\to U,\ v\colon W\to V

in

\mathfrak{U}

a natural isomorphism

c^{u,v}\colon f^{v}f^{u}\to f^{uv}
,

•

for each

U\in\mathfrak{U}

a natural isomorphism

z^{U}\colon 1_{\mathscr{A}(U)}\to f^{1_{U}}
.

Moreover, these data must satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}c^{u,vw}(c^{v,w}\circ f^{u})=c^{uv,w}(f^{w}\circ c^{u,v}),\\
c^{u,1_{V}}(z^{V}\circ f^{u})=1,\ \ c^{1_{U},u}(f^{u}\circ z^{U})=1\end{gathered}

for each triple

u\colon V\to U,\ v\colon W\to V,\ w\colon T\to W

in

\mathfrak{U}
.
With

\mathscr{A}(U)

regarded as one-objected categories,
a twisted presheaf

\mathscr{A}

of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras provides an example of a prestack of
\mathbf{R}
-linear category.

For prestacks

\mathscr{A}=(\mathscr{A},m,f,c,z),\mathscr{A}^{\prime}=(\mathscr{A}^{\prime},m^{\prime},f^{\prime},c^{\prime},z^{\prime})

of

\mathbf{R}
-linear category on

\mathfrak{U}
,
a

morphism

(g,h)\colon\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{A}^{\prime}

is a pseudonatural transformation
which consists of the following data:

•

for each

U\in\mathfrak{U}

an
\mathbf{R}
-linear functor

g^{U}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)\to\mathscr{A}^{\prime}(U)
,

•

for each

u\colon V\to U

in

\mathfrak{U}

a natural isomorphism

h^{u}\colon f^{\prime u}g^{U}\to g^{V}f^{u}
.

Moreover, these data must satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}h^{uv}(c^{\prime u,v}\circ g^{U})=(g^{W}\circ c^{u,v})(h^{v}\circ f^{u})(f^{\prime v}\circ h^{u}),\\
h^{1_{U}}(z^{\prime U}\circ g^{U})=g^{U}\circ z^{U}.\end{gathered}

for each pair

u\colon V\to U,\ v\colon W\to V

in

\mathfrak{U}
.
When

\mathscr{A}

is a twisted presheaf of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras,
morphisms of twisted presheaves of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras
coincide with
morphisms of prestacks.

A

pre-descent datum

in a prestack

\mathscr{A}

is a collection

(A_{U})_{U}

of objects

A_{U}\in\mathscr{A}(U)

with a morphism

\varphi_{u}\colon f^{u}A_{U}\to A_{V}

in

\mathscr{A}(V)

for each

u\colon V\to U

in

\mathfrak{U}
,
which satisfies

\displaystyle\varphi_{v}f^{v}\varphi_{u}=\varphi_{uv}c^{u,v,A_{U}}

given an additional

v\colon W\to V

in

\mathfrak{U}
.
A

morphism

of pre-descent data

g\colon(A_{U})_{U}\to(A^{\prime}_{U})_{U}

is a collection

(g_{U})_{U}

of compatible morphisms

g_{U}\colon A_{U}\to A^{\prime}_{U}
.
Pre-descent data and their morphisms form a category

\operatorname{PDes}(\mathscr{A})

equipped with a canonical functor

\displaystyle\pi_{V}\colon\operatorname{PDes}(\mathscr{A})\to\mathscr{A}(V),\ (A_{U})_{U}\mapsto A_{V}.

When all

\varphi_{u}

are isomorphisms,

(A_{U})_{U}

is called a

descent datum

and
we denote by

\operatorname{Des}(\mathscr{A})

the full subcategory of descent data.
Given limits and colimits in each

\mathscr{A}(U)

preserved by all

f^{u}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)\to\mathscr{A}(V)
,
there exist ones in

\operatorname{Des}(\mathscr{A})

preserved by all

\pi_{V}\colon\operatorname{Des}(\mathscr{A})\to\mathscr{A}(V)

[
DLL17
, Proposition 4.5(3)]
.
In particular,
if each category

\mathscr{A}(U)

is abelian
and
all

f^{u}

are exact,
then

\operatorname{Des}(\mathscr{A})

is abelian
and

\pi_{V}

are exact.

5.2.
The category of quasi-coherent modules over a prestack

The

category of right quasi-coherent modules

over a prestack

\mathscr{A}

is defined as

\displaystyle\operatorname{Qch}(\mathscr{A})=\operatorname{Qch}^{r}(\mathscr{A})=\operatorname{Des}(\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}),

where

\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}

is the

associated prestack

with a prestack

\mathscr{A}

given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}=\operatorname{Mod}^{r}_{\mathscr{A}}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Cat}(\mathbf{R}),\ U\mapsto\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}(U)=\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A}(U)),

whose restriction functor

\displaystyle-\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)\colon\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A}(U))\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A}(V))

is the unique colimit preserving functor extending

f^{u}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)\to\mathscr{A}(V)
.
The functor sends each

F\in\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A}(U))

to an
\mathbf{R}
-linear functor

F\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)\colon\mathscr{A}(V)^{op}\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbf{R})

such that

\displaystyle F\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)(B)=\bigoplus_{A\in\mathscr{A}(U)}F(A)\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathscr{A}(V)(B,f^{u}A)/\sim

for each

B\in\mathscr{A}(V)
.
Here,

\sim

denotes the equivalence relation defined as

\displaystyle F(a)(x)\otimes y\sim x\otimes f^{u}(a)y

for

x\in F(A^{\prime}),\ y\in\mathscr{A}(V)(B,f^{u}A)
,
and

a\colon A\to A^{\prime}

in

\mathscr{A}(U)
.

In the case
where

F=\mathscr{A}(U)(-,A^{\prime})

for some

A^{\prime}\in\mathscr{A}(U)
,

f^{u}

induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle\theta^{u}_{A^{\prime}}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)(-,A^{\prime})\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)\to\mathscr{A}(V)(-,f^{u}A^{\prime}).

If

u=1_{U}
,
then

z^{U}\colon 1_{\mathscr{A}(U)}\to f^{1_{U}}

induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mod}(z)^{U}\colon 1_{\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}(U)}\to-\otimes_{1_{U}}\mathscr{A}(U).

Since we have

\displaystyle F\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)\otimes_{v}\mathscr{A}(W)(C)=\bigoplus_{A\in\mathscr{A}(U),B\in\mathscr{A}(V)}F(A)\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathscr{A}(V)(B,f^{u}A)\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathscr{A}(W)(C,f^{v}B)/\sim,

\theta^{v}_{f^{u}A}

and

c^{u,v}

induce another isomorphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mod}(c^{u,v})\colon-\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)\otimes_{v}\mathscr{A}(W)\to-\otimes_{uv}\mathscr{A}(W).

When

\mathscr{A}

is a twisted presheaf of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras,

\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A}(U))

coincides with the category of right
\mathscr{A}(U)
-modules
whose restriction functor is the ordinary tensor product
and

\operatorname{Mod}(c)^{u,v},\operatorname{Mod}(z)^{U}

are respectively given by

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\operatorname{Mod}(c)^{u,v}_{M}\colon M\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)\otimes_{v}\mathscr{A}(W)\to M\otimes_{uv}\mathscr{A}(W),\ m\otimes a\otimes b\mapsto m\otimes c^{u,v}f^{v}(a)b,\\
\operatorname{Mod}(z)^{U}_{M}\colon M\to M\otimes_{1_{U}}\mathscr{A}(U),\ m\mapsto m\otimes z^{U}\end{gathered}

for any right

\mathscr{A}(U)
-module

M
.

5.3.
The category of twisted quasi-coherent presheaves over a twisted presheaf

Let

\mathscr{A}

be a presheaf of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras on

\mathfrak{U}
.
We denote by

\Pr(\mathscr{A}|_{U})

the category of presheaves of right
\mathscr{A}|_{U}
-modules on

\mathfrak{U}/U
,
where

\mathscr{A}|_{U}

is the induced presheaf on

\mathfrak{U}/U

with

\mathscr{A}|_{U}(V\to U)=\mathscr{A}(V)

for

U\in\mathfrak{U}

and

u\colon V\to U

in

\mathfrak{U}
.
Each

u\colon V\to U

in

\mathfrak{U}

induces a functor

u^{*}_{\Pr}\colon\Pr(\mathscr{A}|_{U})\to\Pr(\mathscr{A}|_{V})
.
Since we have

v^{*}_{\Pr}u^{*}_{\Pr}=(uv)^{*}_{\Pr}

and

(1_{U})^{*}_{\Pr}=1_{\Pr(\mathscr{A}|_{U})}

given an additional

v\colon W\to V

in

\mathfrak{U}
,
the assignments

U\mapsto\Pr(\mathscr{A}|_{U})

and

u\mapsto u^{*}_{\Pr}

define a functor

\displaystyle\Pr(\mathscr{A})\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Cat}(\mathbf{R}).

Let

M

be a right
\mathscr{A}(U)
-module.
Then

\tilde{M}(u)\coloneqq M\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)=M\otimes_{\mathscr{A}(U)}\mathscr{A}(V)

is a right
\mathscr{A}(V)
-module
with

\mathscr{A}(V)

regarded as a left
\mathscr{A}(U)
-module via

f^{u}
.
Suppose that

u^{\prime}\colon V^{\prime}\to U

satisfies

uv^{\prime}=u^{\prime}

for

v^{\prime}\colon V^{\prime}\to V
.
We have the right
\mathscr{A}(U)
-module homomorphism

1_{M}\otimes f^{v^{\prime}}\colon\tilde{M}(u)\to\tilde{M}(u^{\prime})
.
The assignments

u\mapsto\tilde{M}(u)

and

f^{v^{\prime}}\mapsto 1_{M}\otimes f^{v^{\prime}}

define a presheaf

\tilde{M}

of right
\mathscr{A}(U)
-modules on

\mathfrak{U}/U
.
Any
\mathscr{A}(U)
-module homomorphism

g\colon M\to N

induces a natural transform

\tilde{g}=\{\tilde{g}^{u}\coloneqq g\otimes 1_{\mathscr{A}(V)}\}_{u}
.
Thus the assignments

M\mapsto\tilde{M}

and

g\mapsto\tilde{g}

define a functor

(5.1)

\displaystyle Q^{U}\colon\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A}(U))\to\Pr(\mathscr{A}|_{U}).

We have the canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{can}^{u,v}_{M}\colon M\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)\otimes_{v}\mathscr{A}(W))\to M\otimes_{uv}\mathscr{A}(W),\ m\otimes a\otimes b\mapsto m\otimes f^{v}(a)b.

By

[
DLL17
, Lemma 4.10]

the functor

Q^{U}

is fully faithful
and
there is a natural isomorphism

(5.2)

\displaystyle\tau^{u}\colon u^{*}_{\Pr}Q^{U}\to Q^{V}(-\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V))

induced by

(\operatorname{can}^{u,v}_{M})^{-1}
.
A

quasi-coherent presheaf

over

\mathscr{A}_{U}

is defined as the essential image of some

\mathscr{A}(U)
-module

M

by

Q^{U}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{QPr}(\mathscr{A}|_{U})

the category of quasi-coherent presheaves over

\mathscr{A}|_{U}
.

When

\mathscr{A}

is a twisted presheaf with central twists

c
,
one can adapt

\operatorname{Mod}(c)^{u,v}

to

\Pr(c)^{u,v}

as follows.
For

\mathscr{F}\in\Pr(|\mathscr{A}||_{U})

and

w\colon T\to W

in

\mathfrak{U}/W

the central invertible element

f^{w}(c^{u,v})

in

\mathscr{A}(T)

gives an automorphism

\displaystyle f^{w}(c^{u,v})_{r}\colon\mathscr{F}(uvw)\to\mathscr{F}(uvw),\ m\mapsto mf^{w}(c^{u,v})

inducing an isomorphism

\displaystyle\Pr(c)^{u,v}_{\mathscr{F}}\colon v^{*}_{\Pr}u^{*}_{\Pr}(\mathscr{F})\to(uv)^{*}_{\Pr}(\mathscr{F})

in

\Pr(|\mathscr{A}||_{U})
.
Since we have

\displaystyle\Pr(c)^{u,vw}\Pr(c)^{v,w}=\Pr(c)^{uv,w}w^{*}_{\Pr}(\Pr(c)^{u,v}),

the assignments

U\mapsto\Pr(|\mathscr{A}||_{U})

and

u\mapsto u^{*}_{\Pr}

define a prestack

\displaystyle{\Pr}_{\mathscr{A}}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Cat}(\mathbf{R})

whose twist functor is given by

\Pr(c)

and

z

is given by the identity.
Restricting to the essential images

\operatorname{QPr}(|\mathscr{A}||_{U})
,
we obtain another prestack

\operatorname{QPr}_{\mathscr{A}}
.
The

category of right twisted quasi-coherent presheaves

over a twisted presheaf

\mathscr{A}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Alg}(\mathbf{R})

with central twists is defined as

\displaystyle\operatorname{QPr}(\mathscr{A})=\operatorname{Des}(\operatorname{QPr}_{\mathscr{A}}).

Lemma 5.1
.

(
[
DLL17
, Theorem 4.12]
)

Let

\mathscr{A}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Alg}(\mathbf{R})

be a twisted presheaf with central twists.
Then

Q=(Q^{U},\tau^{u})_{U,u}

defines an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Qch}(\mathscr{A})\simeq\operatorname{QPr}(\mathscr{A})

of
\mathbf{R}
-linear categories,
where

Q^{U},\tau^{u}

are given by

(
5.1
)
,

(
5.2
)

respectively.

5.4.
Deformations of the restricted structure sheaves

Let

\mathscr{A}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Cat}(\mathbf{R})

be a flat prestack.
Recall that

\mathscr{A}

is

flat

if
\mathbf{R}
-modules

\mathscr{A}(U)(A,A^{\prime})

are flat for all

U\in\mathfrak{U}

and

A,A^{\prime}\in\mathscr{A}(U)
.
An

\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

\mathscr{A}

is a flat prestack

\mathscr{B}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Cat}(\mathbf{S})

together with an equivalence of prestacks

\mathscr{B}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathscr{A}
,
i.e.,
for each

U

there is a morphism of prestacks inducing an equivalence

\mathscr{B}(U)\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathscr{A}(U)

of
\mathbf{R}
-linear categories

[
DLL17
, Proposition 4.7]
.
Two deformations

\mathscr{B},\mathscr{B}^{\prime}

are

equivalent

if there is an equivalence

\mathscr{B}\to\mathscr{B}^{\prime}

of prestacks compatible with equivalences

\mathscr{B}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathscr{A},\ \mathscr{B}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathscr{A}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{tw}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathscr{A}
.
When

\mathscr{B}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathscr{A}

is an isomorphism of prestacks,
i.e.,
for each

U

there is a morphism of prestacks inducing an isomorphism

\mathscr{B}(U)\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathscr{A}(U)

of
\mathbf{R}
-linear categories,
we call the deformation

\mathscr{B}

strict
.
Two strict deformations

\mathscr{B},\mathscr{B}^{\prime}

are

equivalent

if there is an isomorphism

\mathscr{B}\to\mathscr{B}^{\prime}

of prestacks inducing the identity on

\mathscr{A}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{s-tw}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of equivalence classes of strict
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathscr{A}
.
Recall that
twised presheaves of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras can be regarded as a prestack.
Due to the lemma below,
as long as we consider equivalence classes of twisted deformations of flat presheaves,
we may restrict our attention to strict twisted deformations.

Lemma 5.2
.

(
[
DLL17
, Proposition 5.9]
)

Let

(\mathscr{A},m,f,c,z)

be a flat prestack of
\mathbf{R}
-linear categories on

\mathfrak{U}
.
Then the canonical map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{s-tw}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathbf{S})\to\operatorname{Def}^{tw}_{\mathscr{A}}(\mathbf{S})

is bijective.

Let

\mathfrak{U}

be a finite poset with binary meets.
Then any prestack on

\mathfrak{U}

is

quasi-compact

since

\mathfrak{U}

is finite.
A prestack

\mathscr{A}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Cat}(\mathbf{R})

is

right semi-separated

if the associated prestack

\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}

is of affine localizations.
Namely,
for all

U,V,W\in U

with

v\colon V\to U,w\colon W\to U

in

\mathfrak{U}

and the pullback diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.99995pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.99995pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.99995pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.06871pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.06871pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

the following conditions are satisfied.

•

The category

\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}(U)

is Grothendieck abelian.

•

The functor

v^{*}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}(U)\to\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}(V)

is exact.

•

The functor

v^{*}

admits a fully faithful exact right adjoint

v_{*}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}(V)\to\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathscr{A}}(U)
.

•

There are natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle(v_{*}v^{*})(w_{*}w^{*})\cong(v\bar{w})_{*}(v\bar{w})^{*}\cong(w_{*}w^{*})(v_{*}v^{*}).

A presheaf

\mathscr{A}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Alg}(\mathbf{R})

is

right semi-separated

if so is

\mathscr{A}

with

\mathscr{A}(U)

regarded as one-objected categories.
Every right semi-separated prestack is

geometric
,
i.e.,
the restriction functor

\displaystyle-\otimes_{u}\mathscr{A}(V)\colon\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A}(U))\to\operatorname{Mod}(\mathscr{A}(V))

is exact.
Note that
for any geometric prestack

\mathscr{A}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Cat}(\mathbf{R})

on a small category

\operatorname{Qch}(\mathscr{A})

is a Grothendieck abelian category

[
DLL17
, Theorem 4.14]
.

Let

X

be a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme.
Choose a finite affine open cover

\mathfrak{U}

closed under intersections.
We denote by

\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}}

the restricted structure sheaf to

\mathfrak{U}
.
Since

U\cap V

is affine
as

X

is separated,
we have isomorphisms of
\mathscr{O}_{X}(U)
-modules

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{X}(V)\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}(U)}\mathscr{O}_{X}(W)\cong\mathscr{O}_{X}(U\cap V)\cong\mathscr{O}_{X}(W)\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}(U)}\mathscr{O}_{X}(V)

for all

U,V,W\in U

with

V,W\subset U
.
Since pushforwards along open immersions

V\hookrightarrow U

of affine schemes are fully faithful,
by

[
DLL17
, Lemma 3.1]

the restriction maps

\mathscr{O}_{X}(U)\to\mathscr{O}_{X}(V)

are flat epimorphism of rings.
Then one can apply

[
DLL17
, Proposition 4.28]

to see that
the presheaf

\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}}\colon\mathfrak{U}^{op}\to\operatorname{Alg}(\mathbf{R})

is right semi-separated.
Since

\mathscr{O}_{X}(U)

are flat
\mathbf{R}
-modules,
the category

\displaystyle\operatorname{Qch}(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})\simeq\operatorname{QPr}(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})\simeq\operatorname{Qch}(X)

is flat over

\mathbf{R}

and
Grothendieck abelian

[
DLL17
, Proposition 4.30]
.

Lemma 5.3
.

(
[
DLL17
, Theorem 5.10]
)

Let

X

be a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme
with a finite affine open cover

\mathfrak{U}

closed under intersections.
Then
every twisted
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of the restricted structure sheaf

\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}}

is a quasi-compact semi-separated presheaf on

\mathfrak{U}

and
there is a bijection

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{tw}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}}}(\mathbf{S})\to\operatorname{Def}^{ab}_{\operatorname{Qch}(X)}(\mathbf{S}),\ (\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})_{\phi}\mapsto\operatorname{Qch}((\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})_{\phi}),

where

\phi\in H^{2}\mathbf{C}_{GS}(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})^{\oplus l}

is a cocycle
and

(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})_{\phi}

is the twisted
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}}

along

\phi
.
In particular,
the category of right quasi-coherent modules over a twisted deformation of

\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}}

is given by an abelian deformation of the category

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

of quasi-coherent sheaves.

5.5.
Toda’s construction revisited

Let

\mathfrak{U}

be a small category
and

(\mathscr{A},m,f)

a presheaf of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras on

\mathfrak{U}
.
The

simplicial complex of presheaves

associated with

\mathscr{A}

is the complex

(\mathscr{A}^{\bullet},\varphi^{\bullet})

defined as follows.
Consider the presheaf of algebras

\mathscr{A}^{n}=(\mathscr{A}^{n},m^{n},f^{n})

for

n\geq 0

given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{n}(U)=\prod_{\tau\in\mathcal{N}_{n}(\mathfrak{U}/U)}\mathscr{A}|_{U}(\tau)

endowed with the product algebra structure

m^{n,U}
.
Here,

\tau\in\mathcal{N}_{n}(\mathfrak{U}/U)

is identified with the object

d\tau\to U\in\mathfrak{U}/U

by composing all morphisms of
\tau
,
and
the restriction map

\displaystyle f^{n,U}\colon\mathscr{A}^{n}(U)\to\mathscr{A}^{n}(V),\ (a^{\tau})_{\tau}\mapsto(a^{u\sigma})_{\sigma}

is induced by the natural map

\mathcal{N}_{n}(\mathfrak{U}/V)\to\mathcal{N}_{n}(\mathfrak{U}/U),\ \sigma\to u\sigma
.
Define morphisms of presheaves

\varphi^{n}\colon\mathscr{A}^{n}\to\mathscr{A}^{n+1}

as

\displaystyle\varphi^{n,U}\colon\prod_{\tau\in\mathcal{N}_{n}(\mathfrak{U}/U)}\mathscr{A}|_{U}(\tau)\to\prod_{\sigma\in\mathcal{N}_{n+1}(\mathfrak{U}/U)}\mathscr{A}|_{U}(\sigma),\ (a^{\tau})_{\tau}\mapsto\left(f^{u^{\sigma}_{1}}(a^{\partial_{0}\sigma})+\sum^{n+1}_{i=1}(-1)^{i}a^{\partial_{i}\sigma}\right)_{\sigma}

which specialize to

\displaystyle\varphi^{0,U}\colon\prod_{u\colon V\to U}\mathscr{A}(V)\to\prod_{u\colon V\to U,v\colon W\to V}\mathscr{A}(W),\ (a^{u})_{u}\mapsto f^{v}(a^{u})-a^{uv}.

Then one obtains the complex

(\mathscr{A}^{\bullet},\varphi^{\bullet})

with

\ker(\varphi^{0})\cong\mathscr{A}

[
DLL17
, Lemma 2.12]
.

Using a part of the simplicial complex of presheaves,
one can give an alternative description of Toda’s construction.
Since

\mathbf{R}

is commutative,
by

[
DLL17
, Proposition 2.14]

every normalized reduced cocycle

\displaystyle\phi=(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},\mathbf{c})=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{l},f_{1},\ldots,f_{l},c_{1},\ldots,c_{l})\in\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime 0,2}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}\oplus\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime 1,1}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}\oplus\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime 2,0}_{GS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}.

admits a weak decomposition

\displaystyle(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},\mathbf{c})=(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},0)+(0,0,\mathbf{c})\in\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime 2}_{tGS}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}\oplus\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime 2}_{simp}(\mathscr{A})^{\oplus l}.

From

[
DLL17
, Proposition 2.24]

it follows that
the twisted
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

\mathscr{A}_{\phi}

of

\mathscr{A}

along

\phi

has central twists
and
the underlying presheaf

|\mathscr{A}_{\phi}|

is the presheaf
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\mathscr{A}

along

|\phi|=(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},0)
.
Consider the morphism

F\colon\mathscr{A}\oplus(\mathscr{A}^{0})^{\oplus l}\to(\mathscr{A}^{1})^{\oplus l}

of presheaves defined as

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}F^{U}\colon\mathscr{A}(U)\oplus\prod_{u\colon V\to U}\mathscr{A}^{0}(V)^{\oplus l}\to\prod_{u\colon V\to U,v\colon W\to V}\mathscr{A}^{1}(W)^{\oplus l},\\
(a,(b^{u}_{1},\ldots,b^{u}_{l})_{u})\mapsto(f^{v}_{1}u^{*}(a)+v^{*}(b^{u}_{1})-b^{uv}_{1},\ldots,f^{v}_{l}u^{*}(a)+v^{*}(b^{u}_{l})-b^{uv}_{l})_{u,v},\end{gathered}

where we denote

f^{u}

by

u^{*}

for clarity.
Define the multiplication on

\mathscr{A}\oplus(\mathscr{A}^{0})^{\oplus l}

as

\displaystyle(a,(b^{u}_{1},\ldots,b^{u}_{l})_{u})\cdot(a^{\prime},(b^{\prime u}_{1},\ldots,b^{\prime u}_{l})_{u})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(aa^{\prime},(u^{*}(a)b^{\prime u}_{1}+b^{u}_{1}u^{*}(a^{\prime})+m_{1}(u^{*}(a),u^{*}(a^{\prime})),\ldots,u^{*}(a)b^{\prime u}_{l}+b^{u}_{l}u^{*}(a^{\prime})+m_{l}(u^{*}(a),u^{*}(a^{\prime})))_{u}).

With the scalar given by

\displaystyle(\lambda+\kappa_{1}\epsilon_{1}+\ldots+\kappa_{l}\epsilon_{l})(a,(b^{u}_{1},\ldots,b^{u}_{l})_{u})=(\lambda a,(\kappa_{1}u^{*}(a)+\lambda b^{u}_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{l}u^{*}(a)+\lambda b^{u}_{l})_{u}),

\mathscr{A}\oplus(\mathscr{A}^{0})^{\oplus l}

becomes an
\mathbf{S}
-algebra.
Then the morphism

G\colon|\mathscr{A}_{\phi}|\to\mathscr{A}\oplus(\mathscr{A}^{0})^{\oplus l}

of presheaves of
\mathbf{S}
-algebras defined as

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}G^{U}\colon|\mathscr{A}_{\phi}|(U)\to\mathscr{A}(U)\oplus\mathscr{A}^{0}(U)^{\oplus l},\\
a+b^{u}_{1}\epsilon_{1}+\cdots+b^{u}_{l}\epsilon_{l}\mapsto(a,(f^{u}_{1}(a)+u^{*}(b_{1}),\ldots,f^{u}_{l}(a)+u^{*}(b_{l}))_{u})\end{gathered}

yields an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to|\mathscr{A}_{\phi}|\xrightarrow{G}\mathscr{A}\oplus(\mathscr{A}^{0})^{\oplus l}\xrightarrow{F}(\mathscr{A}^{1})^{\oplus l}.

Consider the case
where

\mathscr{A}

is the restricted structure sheaf

\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}}

of a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme

X
.
Fix a finite affine open cover

\mathfrak{U}

of

X

closed under intersections.
As explained above,

\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}}

gives a quasi-compact right semi-separated presheaf of
\mathbf{R}
-algebras.
Since

\mathscr{O}_{X}(U)

is smooth
\mathbf{R}
-algebra for each

U\in\mathfrak{U}
,
we may assume further that

\phi=(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},\mathbf{c})

is decomposable.
We use the same symbol

\phi

to denote the cocycle

\displaystyle(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in H^{2}(\mathscr{O}_{X})\oplus H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})\oplus H^{0}(\wedge^{2}\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})

which is the image of

(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},\mathbf{c})

under the bijection
(
2.3
).
Then

\phi

defines the
\mathbf{S}
-linear abelian category

\operatorname{Qch}(X,\phi)

obtained by Toda’s construction.

Lemma 5.4
.

(
[
DLL17
, Theorem 5.12]
)

For a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme

X

with a finite affine open cover

\mathfrak{U}

closed under intersections,
let

(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})_{\phi}

be the twisted
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of the restricted structure sheaf

\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}}

along a normalized reduced decomposable cocycle

\displaystyle\phi=(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{f},\mathbf{c})\in\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime 0,2}_{GS}(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})^{\oplus l}\oplus\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime 1,1}_{GS}(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})^{\oplus l}\oplus\bar{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime 2,0}_{GS}(\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})^{\oplus l},

which maps to a cocycle

\displaystyle(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in H^{2}(\mathscr{O}_{X})\oplus H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})\oplus H^{0}(\wedge^{2}\mathscr{T}_{X/\mathbf{R}})

under the bijection

(
2.3
)
.
Then there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Qch}((\mathscr{O}_{X}|_{\mathfrak{U}})_{\phi})\simeq\operatorname{Qch}(X,\phi)

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear Grothendieck abelian categories,
where

\operatorname{Qch}(X,\phi)

is the abelian category obtained by Toda’s construction from

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

along

(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)
.

By
(
2.3
),
Lemma
2.2
,
and
Lemma
5.3

we obtain a bijection

(5.3)

\displaystyle HT^{2}(X/\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}\cong HH^{2}(X/\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}\cong HH^{2}_{ab}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))^{\oplus l}.

Let

\operatorname{Qch}(X)_{\phi}

the flat abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

along the image of

(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)

under
(
5.3
).
Combining
Lemma
5.3

and
Lemma
5.4
,
we obtain

Proposition 5.5
.

For a smooth proper
\mathbf{R}
-scheme

X
,
let

\operatorname{Qch}(X)_{\phi}

be the flat abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

and

\operatorname{Qch}(X,\phi)

the abelian category obtained by Toda’s construction from

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

corresponding to

[\phi]\in HH^{2}(X/\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}

via the isomorphism

(
5.3
)
.
Then there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Qch}(X)_{\phi}\simeq\operatorname{Qch}(X,\phi)

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear Grothendieck abelian categories.

Now,
we return to our setting.
Let

X_{0}

be a Calabi–Yau manifold with

\dim X_{0}>2

and

(X,i_{\mathbf{R}})

an
\mathbf{R}
-deformation of

X_{0}
.
Since we have

\displaystyle HT^{2}(X/\mathbf{R})=H^{2}(\mathscr{O}_{X}/\mathbf{R})\oplus H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X}/\mathbf{R})\oplus H^{0}(\wedge^{2}\mathscr{T}_{X}/\mathbf{R})\cong H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X}/\mathbf{R}),

every cocycle

\phi\in HH^{2}(X/\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}

defines an
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

(X_{\phi},i_{\mathbf{S}})

of

(X,i_{\mathbf{R}})
.
By Proposition

3.11

we have

\operatorname{Qch}(X,\phi)\simeq\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi})
.
Along square zero extensions,
deforming Calabi–Yau manifolds
and
taking the category of quasi-coherent sheaves
are compatible in the following sense.

Corollary 5.6
.

Let

X_{0}

be a Calabi–Yau manifold with

\dim X_{0}>2
,

(X,i_{\mathbf{R}})

an
\mathbf{R}
-deformation of

X_{0}
,
and

(X_{\phi},i_{\mathbf{S}})

the
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

(X,i_{\mathbf{R}})

corresponding to

[\phi]\in HH^{2}(X/\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}
.
Then there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Qch}(X)_{\phi}\simeq\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi})

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear Grothendieck abelian categories,
where

\operatorname{Qch}(X)_{\phi}

is the flat abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\operatorname{Qch}(X)

corresponding to

[\phi]

via the isomorphism

(
5.3
)
.

Remark 5.1
.

Since we have

HT^{2}(X_{\phi}/\mathbf{S})\cong H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X_{\phi}}/\mathbf{S})

by Calabi–Yau condition
and
the finite affine open cover

\mathfrak{U}=\{U_{i}\}^{N}_{i=1}

of

X

closed under intersections canonically lifts to the locally trivial deformation

\mathfrak{U}\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}=\{U_{i}\times_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}\}^{N}_{i=1}
,
one may iteratively use Corollary

5.6

along a sequence of square zero extensions.

6.
Deformations of the dg category of perfect complexes

In this section,
we review the deformation theory of dg category following the exposition from

[
Low08
]

and

[
KL09
]
.
Based on the ideas thereof,
for a higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifold
we prove the compatibility of deformations with taking the dg category of perfect complexes.
Namely,
the dg category of perfect complexes on a deformation
is Morita equivalent to
the corresponding dg deformation of a certain full dg category determined by the direction of the deformation.

6.1.
Curved
A_{\infty}
-categories

In the sequel,
by a

quiver

we will mean a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded quiver.
We choose shift functors

\Sigma^{k}

on the category

G(\mathbf{R})

of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathbf{R}
-modules.
Let

\mathfrak{a}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear quiver.
Namely,

\mathfrak{a}

consists of a set

\operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak{a})

of objects
and
a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathbf{R}
-module

\mathfrak{a}(A,A^{\prime})

for each pair

A,A^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak{a})
.
The category of quivers with a fixed set of objects admits a tensor product

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}\otimes\mathfrak{b}(A,A^{\prime})=\bigoplus_{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathfrak{a}(A^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathfrak{b}(A,A^{\prime\prime})

and
an internal hom

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}](A,A^{\prime})=[\mathfrak{a}(A,A^{\prime}),\mathfrak{b}(A,A^{\prime})].

Morphisms of degree

k

are elements of

[\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}]^{k}=\prod_{A,A^{\prime}}[\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}](A,A^{\prime})^{k}
.

The

tensor cocategory

T(\mathfrak{a})

of

\mathfrak{a}

is the quiver

\displaystyle T(\mathfrak{a})=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathfrak{a}^{\otimes n}

equipped with the comultiplication
which separates tensors.
There is a natural brace algebra structure on

[T(\mathfrak{a}),\mathfrak{a}]=\prod_{n\geq 0}[T(\mathfrak{a}),\mathfrak{a}]_{n}
,
where

\displaystyle[T(\mathfrak{a}),\mathfrak{a}]_{n}=[\mathfrak{a}^{\otimes n},\mathfrak{a}]=\prod_{A_{0},\ldots,A_{n}\in\mathfrak{a}}[\mathfrak{a}(A_{n-1},A_{n})\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{1}),\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{n})].

It is given by the operations

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}{}[T(\mathfrak{a}),\mathfrak{a}]_{n}\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}[T(\mathfrak{a}),\mathfrak{a}]_{n_{1}}\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}[T(\mathfrak{a}),\mathfrak{a}]_{n_{i}}\to[T(\mathfrak{a}),\mathfrak{a}]_{n-i+n_{1}+\cdots+n_{i}},\\
(\phi,\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i})\mapsto\phi\{\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i}\}\end{gathered}

with

\displaystyle\phi\{\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i}\}=\sum\phi(1\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1}\otimes 1\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{i}\otimes 1\otimes\cdots\otimes 1)

satisfying

\displaystyle\phi\{\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{i}\}\{\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{j}\}=\sum(-1)^{\alpha}\phi\{\psi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{1}\{\psi_{m_{1}},\ldots\},\psi_{n_{1}},\ldots,\phi_{i}\{\psi_{m_{i}}\ldots\},\psi_{n_{i}},\ldots,\psi_{j}\},

where

\alpha=\sum^{i}_{k=1}|\phi_{k}|\sum^{m_{k}-1}_{l=1}|\psi_{l}|
.
We denote by

B\mathfrak{a}

the Bar cocategory

T(\Sigma\mathfrak{a})

and
by

\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})

the brace algebra

[B\mathfrak{a},\Sigma\mathfrak{a}]
.
The

associated Hochschild object

is defined as

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})=\Sigma^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})
.
By

[
Low08
, Proposition 2.2]

the tensor coalgebra

T(\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a}))=B\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

becomes a graded bialgebra with the associative multiplication defined by the composition.

A

curved
A_{\infty}
-structure

on

\mathfrak{a}

is an element

b\in\mathbf{C}^{1}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})

satisfying

b\{b\}=0
.
The pair

(\mathfrak{a},b)

is called a

curved
A_{\infty}
-category
.
When the defining morphisms

b_{n}\colon\Sigma\mathfrak{a}^{\otimes n}\to\Sigma\mathfrak{a}

vanish for

n\geq 3
,
we call

(\mathfrak{a},b)

a

cdg category
.
The

curvature elements

of

(\mathfrak{a},b)

is the morphism

b_{0}
.
When it vanishes,
we drop “curved” and “c” from the notation.

Definition 6.1
.

(
[
Low08
, Definition 2.5]
)

For curved
A_{\infty}
-categories

(\mathfrak{a},b),(\mathfrak{a}^{\prime},b^{\prime})

with

\operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak{a})=\operatorname{Ob}(\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})

a

morphism

is a fixed object morphism of quivers

f\colon B\mathfrak{a}\to B\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
,
which is determined by morphisms

f_{n}\colon(\Sigma\mathfrak{a})^{\otimes n}\to\Sigma\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}

and
respects the comultiplications and the curved
A_{\infty}
-structures.

6.2.
Hochschild complexes of curved
A_{\infty}
-categories

The

associated Lie bracket

with the brace algebra

\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})

is defined as

\displaystyle\langle\phi,\psi\rangle=\phi\{\psi\}-(-1)^{|\phi||\psi|}\psi\{\phi\}.

Via an isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})\cong\operatorname{Coder}(B\mathfrak{a},B\mathfrak{a})

of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathbf{R}
-modules to coderivations between cocategories,
it corresponds to the commutator of coderivations.
For a curved
A_{\infty}
-structure

b

on

\mathfrak{a}

the

Hochschild differential

on

\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})

is defined as

\displaystyle d_{b}=\langle b,-\rangle\in[\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a}),\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})]^{1},\ \phi\mapsto\langle b,\phi\rangle.

In particular,

\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})

can be regarded as a dg Lie algebra.
Then

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

is known to be isomorphic to the classical Hochschild complex of

\mathfrak{a}
,
whose definition we will review later.
Since

b

naturally belongs to

B\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{1}
,
it induces a differential

\displaystyle D_{b}=[b,-]\in[B\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a}),B\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})]^{1},\ \phi\mapsto[b,\phi],

where

[-,-]

is the commutator of the multiplication given by

[
Low08
, Proposition 2.2]
.
As

D_{b}

belongs to

\operatorname{Coder}(B\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a}),B\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a}))
,
it defines a curved
A_{\infty}
- structure on

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})
.
The differential

D_{b}

gives a dg bialgebra structure on

B\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

and

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a}),\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})

become
B_{\infty}
-algebras

[
GJ
, Definition 5.2]
.

We will use the same symbol

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

to denote the bigraded object with

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}^{p,q}(\mathfrak{a})=\prod_{A_{0},\ldots,A_{q}\in\mathfrak{a}}[\mathfrak{a}(A_{q-1},A_{q})\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{1}),\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{q})]^{p}.

An element

\phi\in\mathbf{C}^{p,q}(\mathfrak{a})

is said to have the

degree

|\phi|=p
,
the

arity

\operatorname{ar}(\phi)=q
,
and the

Hochschild degree

\deg(\phi)=n=p+q
.
The total complex of Hochschild degree

n

is defined as

\mathbf{C}^{n}(\mathfrak{a})=\prod_{p+q=n}\mathbf{C}^{p,q}(\mathfrak{a})
.
Via the canonical isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Sigma^{1-q}[\mathfrak{a}(A_{q-1},A_{q})\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{1}),\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{q})]

\displaystyle\to[

\displaystyle\Sigma\mathfrak{a}(A_{q-1},A_{q})\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\Sigma\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{1}),\Sigma\mathfrak{a}(A_{0},A_{q})],

the
B_{\infty}
-structure on

\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})

is translated in terms of

\mathfrak{a}
.
For instance,
the operation

\displaystyle\operatorname{dot}\colon\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})_{q}\otimes\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})_{s}\to\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})_{q+s-1},\ (\phi,\psi)\mapsto\phi\{\psi\}

induces the classical “dot product”

\displaystyle\bullet\colon\mathbf{C}^{p,q}(\mathfrak{a})\otimes\mathbf{C}^{r,s}(\mathfrak{a})\to\mathbf{C}^{p+r,q+s-1}(\mathfrak{a})

on

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

given by

\displaystyle\phi\bullet\psi=\sum^{q-1}_{k=0}(-1)^{\beta}\phi(1^{\otimes q-k-1}\otimes\psi\otimes 1^{\otimes k}),

where

\beta=(\deg(\phi)+k+1)(\operatorname{ar}(\psi)+1)
.
We also call the bigraded object

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

the Hochschild complex of

\mathfrak{a}

and
its elements Hochschild cochains.
In the sequel,
curved
A_{\infty}
-structure on

\mathfrak{a}

will often be translated into an element of

\mathbf{C}^{2}(\mathfrak{a})

without further comments.

6.3.
Curved dg category of precomplexes

Let

\mathfrak{a}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear category.
Consider the category

\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})

of precomplexes of
\mathfrak{a}
-objects.
A

precomplex

of
\mathfrak{a}
-objects is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathfrak{a}
-objects

C

with

C^{i}\in\mathfrak{a}

together with a

predifferential
,
a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathfrak{a}
-morphism

\delta_{C}\colon C\to C

of degree 1.
For any

C,D\in\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})

the Hom-set

\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})(C,D)

is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\mathbf{R}
-module with

\displaystyle\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})(C,D)^{k}=\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{a}(C^{i},D^{i+k}).

The cdg structure

\mu\in\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{2}

on

\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})

consists of compositions

m
,
differentials

d
,
and
curvature elements

c
,
where

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}m=\mu_{2}\in\prod_{C_{0},C_{1},C_{2}\in\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})}[\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})(C_{1},C_{2})\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})(C_{0},C_{1}),\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})(C_{0},C_{2})]^{0},\\
d=\mu_{1}\in\prod_{C_{0},C_{1}\in\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})}[\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})(C_{0},C_{1}),\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})(C_{0},C_{1})]^{1},\\
c=\mu_{0}\in\prod_{C\in\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})}\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})(C,C)^{2}\end{gathered}

are given by

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}m(g,f)_{i}=(gf)_{i}=g_{i+|f|}f_{i}\colon C^{i}_{0}\to C^{i+|f|+|g|}_{2},\\
d(f)=\delta_{C_{1}}f-(-1)^{|f|}f\delta_{C_{0}},\\
c_{C}=-\delta^{2}_{C}\end{gathered}

for morphisms

f\colon C_{0}\to C_{1},g\colon C_{1}\to C_{2}

in

\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})
.
One can check that

m
,

d
,
and

c

satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}d(c)=0,\\
d^{2}=-m(c\otimes 1-1\otimes c),\\
dm=m(d\otimes 1+1\otimes d),\\
m(m\otimes 1)=m(1\otimes m).\end{gathered}

We denote by

\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{a})

the full dg subcategoy of complexes of
\mathfrak{a}
-objects,
where

\delta_{C}

become differentials.

Here,
we demonstrate
how the cdg structure is translated.
The differential

\displaystyle d_{b}=\langle b,-\rangle=\langle\Sigma c+d+\Sigma^{-1}m,-\rangle\in[\mathbf{C}_{br}(\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a})),\mathbf{C}_{br}(\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a}))]^{1}

on

\mathbf{C}_{br}(\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a}))

sends

\Sigma^{1-q}\phi\in\mathbf{C}_{br}(\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a}))

with

\phi\in\mathbf{C}^{p,q}(\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{a}))

to

\displaystyle\operatorname{dot}(\Sigma c+d+\sigma^{-1}m,\Sigma^{1-q}\phi)-(-1)^{1-q+p}\operatorname{dot}(\Sigma^{1-q}\phi,\Sigma c+d+\Sigma^{-1}m).

In terms of

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

the image corresponds to

[c+d+m,\phi]
,
where

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}{}[c,\phi]=\sum^{q-1}_{k=0}(-1)^{k+1}\phi(1^{\otimes q-k-1}\otimes c\otimes 1^{\otimes k}),\\
[d,\phi]=(-1)^{\operatorname{ar}(\phi)+1}d(\phi)+\sum^{q-1}_{k=0}(-1)^{\deg(\phi)}\phi(1^{\otimes q-k-1}\otimes c\otimes 1^{\otimes k}),\\
[m,\phi]=m(\phi\otimes 1)+(-1)^{\operatorname{ar}(\phi)+1}m(1\otimes\phi)+\sum^{q-1}_{k=0}(-1)^{k+1}\phi(1^{\otimes q-k-1}\otimes c\otimes 1^{\otimes k}).\end{gathered}

6.4.
Curved dg deformations of dg categories

Assume that

\mathfrak{a}

is an
\mathbf{R}
-linear cdg category.
A

cdg
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

\mathfrak{a}

is an
\mathbf{S}
-linear cdg structure on an
\mathbf{S}
-linear quiver

\mathfrak{b}

together with an isomorphism

\mathfrak{b}\to\mathfrak{a}[\epsilon]=\mathfrak{a}\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S}

of
\mathbf{S}
-linear quivers
whose reduction

\mathfrak{b}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathfrak{a}

induces an isomorphism of cdg categories.
Two cdg deformations

\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

are

isomorphic

if there is an isomorphism

\mathfrak{b}\to\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

of cdg categories inducing the identity on

\mathfrak{a}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{cdg}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of isomorphism classes of cdg
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{a}
.

Theorem 6.2
.

(
[
Low08
, Theorem 4.11]
)

Let

(\mathfrak{a},\mu)

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear cdg category.
Then there is a bijection

(6.1)

\displaystyle H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{cdg}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S}),\ \phi\mapsto(\mathfrak{a}[\epsilon],\mu+\phi\epsilon),\ \phi\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}.

Another cocycle

\phi^{\prime}\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}

maps to an isomorphic cdg deformation
if and only if
there is an element

h\in\mathbf{C}^{1}(\mathfrak{a})

satisfying

\phi^{\prime}-\phi=d_{\mu}(h)
.

A

partial cdg
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

\mathfrak{a}

is a cdg
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of some full cdg subcategory

\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
.
Two partial cdg deformations

\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

are

isomorphic

if there is an isomorphism

\mathfrak{b}\to\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

of cdg categories inducing the identity on

\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
.
A

morphism

of partial cdg deformations

\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

is an isomorphism of cdg deformations between

\mathfrak{b}

and
a full cdg subcategory of

\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}
.
When every morphism of

\mathfrak{b}\to\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

of partial cdg deformations is an isomorphism,
we call

\mathfrak{b}

maximal
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{p-cdg}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of morphism classes of partial cdg
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{a}

and
by

\operatorname{Def}^{mp-cdg}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of isomorphism classes of maximal partial cdg
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{a}
.

Assume further that

\mathfrak{a}

is a dg category.
For

\phi\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

the

[\phi]-\infty
part

of

\mathfrak{a}

is the full dg subcategory

\mathfrak{a}_{[\phi]-\infty}

spanned by objects

A\in\mathfrak{a}

satisfying

\displaystyle H^{2}(\pi_{0})([\phi])_{A}=0\in H^{2}(\mathfrak{a}(A,A)),

where

\pi_{0}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})\to\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})_{0}

is the projection onto the

zero part

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})_{0}=\Sigma^{-1}[T(\Sigma\mathfrak{a}),\Sigma\mathfrak{a}]_{0}=\prod_{A\in\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{a}(A,A).

For a cdg
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

\mathfrak{b}=(\bar{\mathfrak{a}},\mu+\phi\epsilon)

of

\mathfrak{a}
,
the

\infty
-part

\mathfrak{b}_{\infty}

is the full cdg subcategory
spanned by objects

B\in\mathfrak{b}

satisfying

\displaystyle(\mu+\phi\epsilon)_{0,B}=0\in\mathfrak{b}(B,B)^{2}.

It is a partial dg deformation of

\mathfrak{a}

and
a dg deformation of

\mathfrak{a}_{[\phi]-\infty}
.
More explicitly,
if we restrict

\phi

to

\mathfrak{a}_{[\phi]-\infty}
,
then

\phi_{0}

becomes a coboundary
and
there is an element

\displaystyle h\in\prod_{A\in\mathfrak{a}_{[\phi]-\infty}}\mathfrak{a}(A,A)^{1}\subset\mathbf{C}^{1}(\mathfrak{a})

with

d_{\mu}(h)=(\phi|_{\mathfrak{a}_{[\phi]-\infty}})_{0}
.
Thus
the cocycle

\phi|_{\mathfrak{a}_{[\phi]-\infty}}-d_{\mu}(h)

has trivial curvature elements.

Proposition 6.3
.

(
[
Low08
, Proposition 4.14]
)

Let

(\mathfrak{a},\mu)

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear dg category.
Then there is a map

(6.2)

\displaystyle H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{p-dg}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S}),\ \phi\mapsto(\mathfrak{a}_{[\phi]-\infty}[\epsilon],\mu+(\phi|_{\mathfrak{a}_{[\phi]-\infty}}-d_{\mu}(h))\epsilon).

6.5.
The characteristic morphism

Let

\mathfrak{i}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear category.
The canonical projection

\displaystyle\pi\colon\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{i}))\to\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})

has a
B_{\infty}
-section

(6.3)

\displaystyle\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{PCom}(\mathfrak{i})),

whose restriction to the full dg subcategory

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})

of bounded below complexes of
\mathfrak{i}
-objects
is an inverse in the homotopy category

\operatorname{Ho}(B_{\infty})

of
B_{\infty}
-algebras

[
Low08
, Theorem 3.21]
.
Consider the projection onto the zero part

(6.4)

\displaystyle\pi_{0}\colon\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i}))\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i}))_{0}=\prod_{C\in\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i})}\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i})(C,C).

Since

\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i})

is uncurved,

\pi_{0}

induces a morphism of dg algebras

[
Low08
, Proposition 2.7]
.
Composing
(
6.3
)
and
(
6.4
),
one obtains the

characteristic dg morphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})\to\prod_{C\in\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i})}\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i})(C,C).

On cohomology it induces the

characteristic morphism

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathfrak{i}}\colon H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})\to\mathfrak{Z}^{\bullet}K(\mathfrak{i})

for a linear category

\mathfrak{i}
.
Here,

\mathfrak{Z}^{\bullet}K(\mathfrak{i})

is the

graded center

of

K(\mathfrak{i})
,
i.e.,
the center

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}(\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i}))=\operatorname{Hom}(1_{\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i})},1_{\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i})})

of the graded category

\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i})
,
where

\operatorname{Hom}

denotes the graded
\mathbf{R}
-module of graded natural transformations

[
Low08
, Remark 4.6]
.
The characteristic morphism can be interpreted
in terms of deformations of categories.

Theorem 6.4
.

(
[
Low08
, Theorem 4.8]
)

Let

\mathfrak{i}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear category
and

\mathfrak{i}_{\phi}

its
\mathbf{S}
-deformation along

\phi\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})^{\oplus l}
.
Then
for each

C\in K(\mathfrak{i})

the element

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\phi)_{C}\in K(\mathfrak{i})(C,C)[2]^{\oplus l}

is the obstruction against deforming

C

to an object of

K(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})
.

Let

\mathscr{C}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category with enough injectives.
Assume that

\mathfrak{i}

is the subcategory

\operatorname{Inj}(\mathscr{C})

of injective objects.
Taking cohomology of
(
6.4
)
restricted to

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})

and
composing with the isomorphism

HH^{\bullet}_{ab}(\mathscr{C})\cong H\mathbf{C}^{\bullet}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}))

induced by
(
6.3
),
one obtains the

characteristic morphism

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathscr{C}}\colon HH^{\bullet}_{ab}(\mathscr{C})\to\mathfrak{Z}^{\bullet}(D^{+}(\mathscr{C}))

for an abelian category

\mathscr{C}
.
Here,
we use the isomorphism

HH^{\bullet}_{ab}(\mathscr{C})\cong H\mathbf{C}^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{i})

obtained from

[
LV06b
, Theorem 6.6]
.
Note that
the graded center

\mathfrak{Z}^{\bullet}(D^{+}(\mathscr{C}))

of

D^{+}(\mathscr{C})\simeq K^{+}(\mathfrak{i})

is given by the center

\mathfrak{Z}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}))
.

Corollary 6.5
.

(
[
Low08
, Corollary 4.9]
)

Let

\mathscr{C}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear abelian category with enough injectives
and

\mathscr{C}_{\phi}

its abelian
\mathbf{S}
-deformation along a cocycle

\phi\in HH^{2}_{ab}(\mathscr{C})^{\oplus l}
.
Then
for each

C\in D^{+}(\mathscr{C})

the element

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathscr{C}}(\phi)_{C}\in\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{\mathscr{C}}(C,C)^{\oplus l}

is the obstruction against deforming

C

to an object of

D^{+}(\mathscr{C}_{\phi})
.

6.6.
Maximal partial dg deformations of the dg category of bounded below complexes

Consider the map

(6.5)

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}\colon H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})^{\oplus l}\to\operatorname{Def}^{p-dg}_{\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})}(\mathbf{S}),\ \phi\mapsto(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)})_{\infty}

obtained from
(
6.2
)
and
(
6.3
).
The partial dg deformation

(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)})_{\infty}

of

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{a})

coincides with a dg deformation

(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})_{[\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)]-\infty})_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)}

of

(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})_{[\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)]-\infty})
,
where
the cdg structure

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)

restricted to the

[\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)]-\infty

part.

Theorem 6.6
.

(
[
Low08
, Theorem 4.15(iii)]
)

Let

\mathfrak{i}

be an
\mathbf{R}
-linear category
and

\mathfrak{i}_{\phi}

its
\mathbf{S}
-deformation along

\phi\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})^{\oplus l}
.
Then,
for every collection of complexes

\Gamma=\{\bar{C}\}_{C\in\mathfrak{i}_{[\phi]-\infty}}

with

\bar{C}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}=C
,
the full dg subcategory

\operatorname{Com}^{+}_{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})\subset\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})

spanned by

\Gamma

is a maximal partial dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})

representing

\rho^{\prime}(\phi)
.

From the proof,
one sees that

\operatorname{Com}^{+}_{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})

is a dg deformation of

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})_{[\operatorname{embr}(\phi)]-\infty}
.
According to

[
Low08
, Example 4.13]
,
an object

C\in\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})

belongs to

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})_{[\operatorname{embr}(\phi)]-\infty}

if and only if

\displaystyle\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\phi)_{C}=0\in K(\mathfrak{i})(C,C[2])^{\oplus l}.

Since

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\phi)_{C}

is the obstruction against deforming

C

to an object of

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})
,
we may take
a collection of one chosen lift of each unobsteucted complexe in

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})

as

\Gamma
.
Clearly,
any dg subcategory of

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})_{[\operatorname{embr}(\phi)]-\infty}

dg deforms along the restriction of

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)
.

6.7.
Hochschild cohomology of the dg category of perfect complexes

We review the definition of the classical Hochschild complex of dg categories.
Assume that

\mathfrak{a}

is a small
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant dg category,
i.e.,
all Hom-sets are cofibrant in the dg category

\operatorname{Mod}_{dg}(\mathbf{R})=\operatorname{Com}(\operatorname{Mod}(\mathbf{R}))

of complexes of
\mathbf{R}
-modules.
Recall that

N\in\operatorname{Mod}_{dg}(\mathbf{R})

is cofibrant
if its terms are projective.
For an
\mathfrak{a}
-bimodule

M\colon\mathfrak{a}^{op}\otimes\mathfrak{a}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{dg}(\mathbf{R})
,
the

Hochschild complex

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a},M)

of

\mathfrak{a}

with coefficients in

M

is the total complex of the double complex
whose
q
-th columns are given by

\displaystyle\prod_{A_{0},\ldots,A_{q}\in\mathfrak{a}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathfrak{a}(A_{q-1},A_{q})\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathfrak{a}(A_{1},A_{0}),M(A_{0},A_{q}))

with horizontal differentials

d^{q}_{Hoch}
.
When

M=\mathfrak{a}

we call

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})=\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a})

the

Hochschild complex

of

\mathfrak{a}
.
The Hochschild complex satisfies a “limited functoriality” property.
Namely,
if

j\colon\mathfrak{a}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{b}

is a fully faithful dg functor to a small
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant dg category

\mathfrak{b}
,
then there is an associated map between Hochschild complexes

\displaystyle j^{*}\colon\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{b})\to\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

given by restriction.
As mentioned above,
the Hochschild complex is isomorphic to the associated Hochschild object

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})=\Sigma^{-1}\mathbf{C}_{br}(\mathfrak{a})
.
In particular,
the Hochschild complex

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

has a
B_{\infty}
-algebra structure compatible with the map

j^{*}
.

The definition of the Hochschild complex was modified by Shukla and Quillen

[
Shu61
,
Qui70
]

to general dg categories.
Now,
we drop the assumption on

\mathfrak{a}

to be
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant
and
fix a good
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant resolution

\bar{\mathfrak{a}}\to\mathfrak{a}
,
which is a quasi-equivalence with an
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant dg category

\bar{\mathfrak{a}}

inducing surjection of Hom-sets in the graded category

[
LV06a
, Proposition-Definition 2.3.2]
.
The

Shukla complex

of

\mathfrak{a}

with coefficient

M

is defined as

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{sh}(\mathfrak{a},M)=\mathbf{C}(\bar{\mathfrak{a}},M).

According to

[
LV06a
, Section 4.2]
,
which in turn is attributed to

[
Kel
]
,
the assignment

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{sh}\colon\mathfrak{a}\mapsto\mathbf{C}_{sh}(\mathfrak{a})

defines up to canonical natural isomorphism a contravariant functor
on a suitable category of small dg categories
with values in

\operatorname{Ho}(B_{\infty})
.
In particular,

\mathbf{C}_{sh}(\mathfrak{a})

does not depend on the choice of good
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant resolutions of

\mathfrak{a}

up to canonical isomorphism.
The functor

\mathbf{C}_{sh}

satisfies some extended “limited functoriality”.
Let

j\colon\mathfrak{a}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{b}

be a fully faithful dg functor to a small dg category

\mathfrak{b}

with a good
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant resolution

\bar{\mathfrak{b}}\to\mathfrak{b}
.
One may restrict the resolution to a good
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant resolution

\bar{\mathfrak{a}}\to\mathfrak{a}

of

\mathfrak{a}
.
Then the restriction along the extended fully faithful dg functor

\bar{\mathfrak{a}}\hookrightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{b}}

defines a morphism of Shukla complexes

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{sh}(\mathfrak{b})\to\mathbf{C}_{sh}(\mathfrak{a})

still denoted by

j^{*}
.
In the sequel,
we write

\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})

for

\mathbf{C}_{sh}(\mathfrak{a})
.

Now,
we return to our setting.
Let

X_{0}

be a Calabi–Yau manifold with

\dim X_{0}>2
.
We denote by

D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{0}))

the dg category of unbounded complexes of quasi-coherent sheaves on

X_{0}
.
In our setting,
the full dg subcategory

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

of compact objects consists of perfect complexes on

X_{0}
.
The canonical embedding

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})\hookrightarrow D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{0}))

factorizes through the dg category

D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{0}))

of bounded below complexes of quasi-coherent sheaves on

X_{0}
.
Let

(X,i_{\mathbf{R}})

be an
\mathbf{R}
-deformation of

X_{0}

and

\mathfrak{i}=\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))
.
As explained above,

X

is smooth projective over

\mathbf{R}
.
The Hochschild cohomology of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

can be expressed in terms of

\mathfrak{i}
.

Lemma 6.7
.

There is an isomorphism

(6.6)

\displaystyle H\mathbf{C}^{\bullet}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}))\to H\mathbf{C}^{\bullet}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)).

Proof.

Consider the quasi-fully faithful functor

(6.7)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)\hookrightarrow D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))\to\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}),

where
the first functor is the canonical embedding
and
the second functor is a quasi-equivalence induced by the canonical equivalence

\displaystyle D^{+}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))\to K^{+}(\mathfrak{i})

of their homotopy categories

[
CNS
, Theorem A]
.
The functor
(
6.7
)
induces a morphism

(6.8)

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}))\to\mathbf{C}(D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)))\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))

of
B_{\infty}
-algebra,
which in turn induces a morphism

(6.9)

\displaystyle H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}))\to H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}(D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)))\to H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))

of Hochschild cohomology.
Since quasi-equivalences preserve Hochschld cohomology,
the first arrow in
(
6.9
)
is an isomorphism.

It remains to show that
the second arrow in
(
6.9
)
is an isomorphism.
We claim that
the restriction

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)))\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))

is an isomorphism in

\operatorname{Ho}(B_{\infty})
.
Fix a good
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant resolution

\displaystyle\overline{D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))}\to D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)).

Via the canonical embedding

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)\hookrightarrow D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))

it induces a good
\mathbf{R}
-cofibrant resolution

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)}\to\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

and
the fully faithful embedding

\displaystyle\bar{j}\colon\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)}\hookrightarrow\overline{D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))}.

We denote by

j

the induced fully faithful functor on the homotopy categories.
One can apply

[
Por10
, Theorem 1.2]

to see that
the functor

\displaystyle D(\operatorname{Qch}(X))\to\operatorname{Mod}(\operatorname{Perf}(X)),\ F\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}_{D(\operatorname{Qch}(X))}(j(-),F)

lifts to a localization

D(\operatorname{Qch}(X))\to\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Mod}_{dg}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))
,
where

\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Mod}_{dg}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))

is the derived category of right dg modules over the dg category

\operatorname{Mod}_{dg}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))

of right dg modules over

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)
.
In particular,
the lift
is fully faithful.
Then the claim follows from

[
DL13
, Proposition 5.1]
.
Similarly,
one can show that
the restriction

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)))\to\mathbf{C}(D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)))

is an isomorphism in

\operatorname{Ho}(B_{\infty})
.
Hence the restriction

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X)))\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))

is an isomorphism in

\operatorname{Ho}(B_{\infty})
,
which induces the desired isomorphism on Hochschild cohomology.
∎

6.8.
Morita deformations of the dg category of perfect complexes

Let

\operatorname{dgCat}_{\mathbf{R}}

be the category of
small
\mathbf{R}
-linear dg categories
and
dg functors.
The category

\operatorname{dgCat}_{\mathbf{R}}

has two model structures,
so called
the Dwyer–Kan model structure
and
the Morita model structure,
constructed by Tabuada respectively in

[
Tab05a
]

and

[
Tab05b
]
.
On the Dwyer–Kan model structure,
weak equivalences are given by quasi-equivalences of dg categories.
On the Morita model structure,
weak equivalences are given by Morita morphisms.
Recall that
a dg functor in

\operatorname{dgCat}_{\mathbf{R}}

is a

Morita morphism

if it induces an derived equivalence.
Also recall that
for each object

\mathfrak{a}\in\operatorname{dgCat}_{\mathbf{R}}

the derived category

\mathbf{D}(\mathfrak{a})

of right dg modules over

\mathfrak{a}

is defined as the Verdier quotient

\displaystyle[\operatorname{Mod}_{dg}(\mathfrak{a})]/[\operatorname{Acycl}(\mathfrak{a})]

of the homotopy category of the dg category

\operatorname{Mod}_{dg}(\mathfrak{a})

of right dg modules over

\mathfrak{a}

by the homotopy category of the full dg subcategory

\operatorname{Acycl}(\mathfrak{a})

of acyclic right dg modules.

We denote by

\operatorname{Ho}_{\mathbf{R}}

the localization of

\operatorname{dgCat}_{\mathbf{R}}

by weak equivalences in the Dwyer–Kan model structure
and
by

\operatorname{Hmo}_{\mathbf{R}}

the localization of

\operatorname{dgCat}_{\mathbf{R}}

by weak equivalences in the Morita model structure.
Passing to

\operatorname{Ho}_{\mathbf{R}}
,
two quasi-equivalent
\mathbf{R}
-linear dg categories

\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}

get identified.
Passing to

\operatorname{Hmo}_{\mathbf{R}}
,
two Morita equivalent
\mathbf{R}
-linear dg categories

\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}

get identified.
Recall that
two
\mathbf{R}
-linear dg categories

\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}

are said to be

Morita equivalent

if they are connected by a Morita morphism.
By

[
Töe
, Proposition 7]

or

[
Töe
, Exercise 28]

for
\mathbf{R}
-linear triangulated dg categories
Morita equivalences
coincide with
quasi-equivalences.
The dg category

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

is triangulated,
as it is pretriangulated
and
closed under homotopy direct summands.

Let

\mathfrak{a}

be a small
\mathbf{R}
-linear dg category.
A

Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

of

\mathfrak{a}

is an
\mathbf{S}
-linear dg category

\mathfrak{b}

together with a Morita equivalence

\mathfrak{b}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathfrak{a}
,
where

-\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\colon\operatorname{Hmo}_{\mathbf{S}}\to\operatorname{Hmo}_{\mathbf{R}}

is the derived functor of the base change

-\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\colon\operatorname{dgCat}_{\mathbf{S}}\to\operatorname{dgCat}_{\mathbf{R}}
.
Two Morita deformations

\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

are

isomorphic

if there is a Morita equivalence

\mathfrak{b}\to\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

inducing the identity on

\mathfrak{a}
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})

the set of isomorphism classes of Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{a}
.
By

[
KL09
, Proposition 3.3]

there is a canonical map

(6.10)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\mathbf{S})\to H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}

defined as follows.
Any Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

\mathfrak{b}

of a small
\mathbf{R}
-linear dg category

\mathfrak{a}

can be represented by a
h
-flat resolution

\overline{\mathfrak{b}}\to\mathfrak{b}
,
which defines a dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\bar{\mathfrak{b}}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}
.
Let

\phi_{\overline{\mathfrak{b}}}\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\bar{\mathfrak{b}}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}

be a Hochschild cocycle representing

\overline{\mathfrak{b}}

via the bijection
(
6.1
).
The map
(
6.10
)
sends

\mathfrak{b}

to the image

\phi_{\mathfrak{b}}

of

\phi_{\overline{\mathfrak{b}}}

under the isomorphism

H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\bar{\mathfrak{b}}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}\to H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{a})^{\oplus l}

induced by the Morita equivalence

\bar{\mathfrak{b}}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\to\mathfrak{a}
.

Theorem 6.8
.

The composition

(6.11)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)}(\mathbf{S})\to\operatorname{Def}^{cdg}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)}(\mathbf{S})

of

(
6.10
)

with the inverse of

(
6.1
)

is bijective.

Proof.

To show the injectivity,
by

[
KL09
, Proposition 3.7]

it suffices to check that

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

has bounded above cohomology,
i.e.,
the dg module

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)(E,F)

has bounded above cohomology for each

E,F\in\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)
.
Consider the spectral sequence

\displaystyle E^{p,q}_{2}=H^{p}(X,\underline{\operatorname{Ext}}^{q}_{X}(E,F))\Rightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}_{X}(E,F).

Since we have

\underline{\operatorname{Ext}}^{q}_{X}(E,F)\cong\mathcal{H}^{q}(E^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}F)
,
the cohomology

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{p+q}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)(E,F))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}_{X}(E,F)

vanishes
whenever

p,q

are sufficiently large.

To show the surjectivity,
consider the characteristic dg morphism

(6.12)

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Tw}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)))\to\prod_{E\in\operatorname{Tw}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))}\operatorname{Tw}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))(E,E)

for the dg category

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

[
Low08
, Theorem 3.19]
,
where
the first arrow is given by the
B_{\infty}
-section of the canonical projection

\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Tw}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)))\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))
.
Here,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tw}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))=\operatorname{Tw}_{\text{ilnil}}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))_{\infty}

is the
\infty
-part of the dg category of locally nilpotent twisted object over

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)
.
It is a dg enhancement of the derived category

\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))

of right dg modules over

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Tw}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))^{c}

the full dg subcategory of

\operatorname{Tw}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))

spanned by compact objects.
Its homotopy category

\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))^{c}
,
the full triangulated subcategory of

\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))

spanned by compact objects,
get identified with

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

via the Yoneda embedding
as

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

is pretriangulated
and
closed under homotopy direct summands.
When restricted to

\operatorname{Tw}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))^{c}
,
on cohomology
(
6.12
)
induces the characteristic morphism

\displaystyle\chi_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X),E}\colon H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))\to\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{X}(E,E).

Consider the quasi-fully faithful functor
(
6.7
).
One can apply

[
KL09
, Proposition 2.3]

to obtain a commutative diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
