whose horizontal arrows are the characteristic morphisms
and
whose vertical arrows are the canonical isomorphisms induced by quasi-equivalences.
Applying

[
KL09
, Proposition 2.3]

to the quasi-fully faithful functor,

(6.19)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{i}\to\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})

we obtain another commutative diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 36.7778pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-36.7778pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 89.63441pt\raise-24.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.7778pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.7778pt\raise-32.23112pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern whose horizontal arrows are the characteristic morphisms
and
whose vertical arrows are the canonical isomorphisms,
as the morphism

\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}))\to\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})

induced by the quasi-fully faithful functor
(
6.19
)
coincides with the canonical projection
and
by

[
Low08
, Proposition 3.20]

its inverse in

\operatorname{Ho}(B_{\infty})

is the
B_{\infty}
-section
(
6.3
),
which induces an isomorphism on cohomology.
Note that

\operatorname{Tw}(\mathfrak{i})

is precisely

\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i})
.

By
Lemma
6.7

any element of

H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))^{\oplus l}

can be represented by the image

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)

of

\phi\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{i})^{\oplus l}

under the
B_{\infty}
-section
(
6.3
).
We use the same symbol to denote the image under the direct sum of
(
6.6
).
Since

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

has bounded above cohomology,
by

[
KL09
, Proposition 3.12]

the map
(
6.11
)
is surjective
if
there exists a full dg subcategory

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)\subset\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

which is Morita equivalent to

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

such that

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)}(\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi))_{E}=0

for any

E\in\mathfrak{m}(\phi)

and
cocycle

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))^{\oplus l}
.
The composition of vertical arrows in
(
6.8
)
and
(
6.8
)
maps

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)

to

\phi

up to coboundary.
By Corollary

6.5

the image of

\phi

under

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{i},E}

is the obstruction against deforming

E

to an object of

D^{+}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))
.
Here,
for a cocycle

\phi\in HH^{2}_{ab}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))^{\oplus l}

we denote by

(X_{\phi},i_{\mathbf{S}})

the
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

(X,i_{\mathbf{R}})

along

\phi

via the bijection
(
5.3
).
Let

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}

be an
h
-flat resolution of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})
.
Then the dg category

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)=\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}

is a full dg subcategory of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

with a Morita equivalence

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\phi)\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

from

[
BFN10
, Theorem 1.2]
.
As each object

E\in\mathfrak{m}(\phi)

lifts to an object of

\operatorname{Perf}(X_{\phi})
,
we obtain

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\mathfrak{i}}(\phi)_{E}=0
.
Chasing the diagrams
(
6.8
)
and
(
6.8
),
we obtain

\chi^{\oplus l}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)}(\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi))_{E}=0

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.1
.

The canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{\infty}(X_{\phi})\otimes_{\operatorname{Perf}_{\infty}(\mathbf{S})}\operatorname{Perf}_{\infty}(\mathbf{R})\simeq_{\infty}\operatorname{Perf}_{\infty}(X)

of corresponding
\infty
-categories from

[
BFN10
, Theorem 1.2]

translates via

[
Coh
, Corollary 5.7]

into a Morita equivalence

(6.24)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})\otimes^{L}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(\mathbf{S})}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(\mathbf{R})\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

of dg categories,
where

-\otimes^{L}-\colon\operatorname{Hmo}_{\mathbf{S}}\times\operatorname{Hmo}_{\mathbf{S}}\to\operatorname{Hmo}_{\mathbf{S}}

is the derived pointwise tensor product of dg categories.
The left hand side of
(
6.24
)
is a triangulated dg category split-generated by objects of the form

E_{\phi}\boxtimes M

for

E_{\phi}\in\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})

and

M\in\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(\mathbf{R})
,
which maps to

E\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\pi^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}M\in\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

via the Morita equivalence.
Here,

E=E_{\phi}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}

and

\pi_{\mathbf{R}}\colon X\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbf{R}

is the structure morphism.
Hence we obtain a Morita equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})\otimes^{L}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(\mathbf{S})}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(\mathbf{R})\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

used in the above proof.

6.9.
Maximal partial dg deformations of the dg category of perfect complexes

As explained above,
the category

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})

defines a Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)
.
Let

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}

be the
h
-flat resolution of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})

from

[
CNS
, Proposition 3.10]
.
Choose one lift for each object of

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)=\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}\otimes_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}

to

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}
.
Let

\Gamma

be the collection of the choices
and

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg,\Gamma}(X_{\phi})}

the full dg subcategory spanned by objects belonging to

\Gamma
.
It gives a dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformation of

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)

representing the Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformation

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})
.
On the other hand,

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)

admits a dg deformation

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\phi)_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)}=\left(\mathfrak{m}(\phi)[\epsilon]=\mathfrak{m}(\phi)\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{S},\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)+\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)\epsilon\right),

where

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)})

and

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)\in Z^{2}\mathbf{C}(\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)})^{\oplus l}

are respectively the images
under the
B_{\infty}
-section
(
6.3
)
and
its direct sum
of
the compositions

m

in

\mathfrak{i}

and
the cocycle

\phi\in Z\mathbf{C}^{2}(\mathfrak{i})^{\oplus l}

corresponding to

\phi\in HH^{2}_{ab}(\operatorname{Qch}(X))^{\oplus l}

via the isomorphism obtained from

[
LV06b
, Theorem 6.6]
.
Here,
as above
we use the same symbol to denote the images under the compositions of
the bijections induced by the quasi-equivalences with
(
6.6
)
and
its direct sum respectively.
Also,
we use the same symbol to denote the images under the morphism

\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))\to\mathbf{C}(\mathfrak{m}(\phi))

of
B_{\infty}
-algebras induced by the Morita equivalence

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)\to\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)
.

Theorem 6.9
.

There is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg,\Gamma}(X_{\phi})}\simeq\mathfrak{m}(\phi)_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)}

of dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformations of

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)
.
In particular,
the Morita
\mathbf{S}
-deformations

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})

defines a maximal partial dg
\mathbf{S}
-deformaiton of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

along

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)
.

Proof.

Since both dg deformations share their underlying quiver

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)[\epsilon]
,
it suffices to show the coincidence of their dg structures up to coboundary.
The dg structure on

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)}

is

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)+\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)\epsilon
.
Let

\overline{D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))}

be the
h
-flat resolution of

D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))

from

[
CNS
, Proposition 3.10]
.
There is a canonical dg functor

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}\hookrightarrow\overline{D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))}

extending the canonical embedding

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})\hookrightarrow D_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))
.
The dg structure on

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg,\Gamma}(X_{\phi})}

is the restriction of that on

\overline{D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))}

by

[
Low08
, Proposition 2.6]
.

We compute the dg structure on

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}
.
Consider the quasi-fully faithful functor

(6.25)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})\hookrightarrow D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))\to\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})

where
the first functor is the canonical embedding
and
the second functor is a quasi-equivalence induced by the canonical equivalence

(6.26)

\displaystyle D^{+}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))\to K^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})

by

[
CNS
, Theorem A]
.
The functor
(
6.25
)
canonically extends to that

(6.27)

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}\hookrightarrow\overline{D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))}\to\overline{\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})}

of the
h
-flat resolutions from

[
CNS
, Proposition 3.10]
.
It induces a morphism

(6.28)

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(\overline{\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})})\to\mathbf{C}(\overline{D^{+}_{dg}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))})\to\mathbf{C}(\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})})

of
B_{\infty}
-algebras,
which in turn induces an isomorphism

(6.29)

\displaystyle H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}(\overline{\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})})\to H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}(D^{+}_{dg}(\overline{\operatorname{Qch}(X_{\phi}))})\to H^{\bullet}\mathbf{C}(\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})})

of Hochschild cohomology by
Lemma
6.7
.

Recall that

\delta\in\mathbf{C}^{1}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}))

be the differentials of objects in

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i})
.
Let

\delta+\delta^{\prime}\epsilon\in\mathbf{C}^{1}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi}))

be the differentials of objects in

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})

with

\delta^{\prime}=(\delta^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\delta^{\prime}_{l})\in\mathbf{C}^{1}(\operatorname{Com}(\mathfrak{i}))^{\oplus l}
.
Then the dg structure on

\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})

is

\displaystyle\operatorname{embr}_{\delta+\delta^{\prime}\epsilon}(m+\phi\epsilon)=(m+\phi\epsilon)\{\delta+\delta^{\prime}\epsilon,\delta+\delta^{\prime}\epsilon\}+(m+\phi\epsilon)\{\delta+\delta^{\prime}\epsilon\}+(m+\phi\epsilon).

We use the same symbol to denote the images under the composition of
(
6.28
)
with the morphism

\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi}))\to\mathbf{C}(\overline{\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})})

induced by the
h
-flat resolution.
Note that

\delta\in\mathbf{C}^{1}(\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)})

is the differentials of objects in

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)}

and

\delta+\delta^{\prime}\epsilon\in\mathbf{C}^{1}(\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})})

is the differentials of objects in

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})}

with

\delta^{\prime}=(\delta^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\delta^{\prime}_{l})\in\mathbf{C}^{1}(\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)})^{\oplus l}
,
as
(
6.28
)
is a morphism of
B_{\infty}
-algebras induced by the canonical equivalence
(
6.26
).
One can apply the same argument as in the proof of

[
Low08
, Theorem 4.15]

to obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{embr}_{\delta+\delta^{\prime}\epsilon}(m+\phi\epsilon)=\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)+\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)\epsilon+d_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)}(\delta^{\prime}_{1})\epsilon_{1}+\cdots+d_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)}(\delta^{\prime}_{l})\epsilon_{l}

on

\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg,\Gamma}(X_{\phi})}
.
Note that
up to coboundary
the image of

\displaystyle d_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)}(\delta^{\prime}_{1})\epsilon_{1}+\cdots+d_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)}(\delta^{\prime}_{l})\epsilon_{l}\in\mathbf{C}(\overline{\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\mathfrak{i}_{\phi})})^{\oplus l}

under
(
6.28
)
coincide with the images of

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{1}\epsilon_{1}+\cdots+\delta^{\prime}_{l}\epsilon_{l}\in\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi}))^{\oplus l}

under Hochschild differential

d_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)}

on

\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X))
.
Hence we obtain an isomorphism

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg,\Gamma}(X_{\phi})}

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{m}(\phi)[\epsilon],\operatorname{embr}_{\delta+\delta^{\prime}\epsilon}(m+\phi\epsilon))

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathfrak{m}(\phi)[\epsilon],\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(m)+\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}(\phi)_{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)}.

of dg deformaitons of

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)

from
(
6.1
).
∎

Remark 6.2
.

From the above theorem it follows that
the image of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\phi})

under the map
(
6.10
)
is represented by

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta}(\phi)
.

Remark 6.3
.

In general,

\mathfrak{m}(\phi)

is strictly smaller than

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)
.
For instance,
let

X_{0}

be a quintic
3
-fold of Fermat type.
By

[
AK91
, Proposition 2.1]

for any general first order deformation

X_{1}

of

X_{0}
,
there is no line in

X_{0}

which lifts to a closed subvariety of

X_{1}
.
Hence deformations of any perfect complex quasi-isomorphic to the pushforward of the structure sheaf of a line in

X_{0}

is obstructed.
This example was informed to the author by Yukinobu Toda.

7.
Deformations of higher dimensional Calabi–Yau manifolds revisited

Now,
we are ready to prove our first main result.
Consider the functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}\colon\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Set}

which sends each

A\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

to the set of isomorphism classes of Morita
A
-deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

and each morphism

B\to A

in

\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

to the map

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(B)\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(A)

induced by

-\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}
.
The deformation theory for

X_{0}

is equivalent to
that for

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

in the following sense.

Theorem 7.1
.

There is a natural isomorphism

(7.1)

\displaystyle\zeta\colon\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

of deformation functors.

Proof.

We show that
the assignment

\displaystyle(X_{A},i_{A})\mapsto(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A}),i^{*}_{A})

for each

A\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

defines a natural transformation.
Here,
we use the same symbol

i^{*}_{A}

to denote both the derived pullback functor

\displaystyle i^{*}_{A}\colon\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A})\to\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

and
the induced Morita equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A})\otimes^{L}_{A}\mathbf{k}\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0}).

The surjection

A\to\mathbf{k}

factorizes through a sequence

\displaystyle A=A_{m}\to A_{m-1}\to\cdots\to A_{1}\to\mathbf{k}

of small extensions.
Pullback of

X_{A}

yields a sequence

\displaystyle(X_{A_{m}},i_{A_{m}})\mapsto(X_{A_{m-1}},i_{A_{m-1}})\mapsto\cdots\mapsto(X_{A_{1}},i_{A_{1}})\mapsto X_{0}

of deformations of

X_{0}
.
Let

\phi_{A_{1}}\in HH^{2}(X_{0})=\text{H}^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X_{0}})

be the cocycle representing

(X_{A_{1}},i_{A_{1}})
.

By
Theorem
6.9

the Morita deformation

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{1}})

of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X)

corresponds to

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta_{0}}(\phi_{A_{1}})

via the bijection
(
6.11
).
Here,

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta_{0}}(\phi_{A_{1}})

denotes the image under the composition of

\displaystyle H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{0}))))\cong H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0}))

with the induced isomorphism

\displaystyle HH^{2}(X_{0})\cong H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{0})))\cong H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{0}))))

by the
B_{\infty}
-section of the canonical projection

\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{0})))\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{0}))))
.
Induction yields a sequence

\displaystyle[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{m}}),i^{*}_{A_{m}}]\mapsto[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{m-1}}),i^{*}_{A_{m-1}}]\mapsto\cdots\mapsto[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{1}}),i^{*}_{A_{1}}]\mapsto\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

of Morita deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})
.
In particular,

(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{m}}),i^{*}_{A_{m}})

is a Morita deformation of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

corresponding to the collection

\{\operatorname{embr}_{\delta_{n-1}}(\phi_{A_{n}})\}^{m}_{n=1}
.
Here,
each

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta_{n-1}}(\phi_{A_{n}})

denotes the image of

\phi_{A_{n}}\in H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X_{A_{n-1}}}/A_{n-1})^{\oplus l_{n-1}}
,
where

l_{n-1}

is the rank of the kernel of square zero extension

A_{n}\to A_{n-1}

as a free
A_{n-1}
-module,
under the composition of

\displaystyle H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{A_{n-1}}))))^{\oplus l_{n-1}}\cong H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{n-1}}))^{\oplus l_{n-1}}

with the induced isomorphism

\displaystyle HH^{2}(X_{A_{n-1}}/A_{n-1})^{\oplus l_{n-1}}\cong H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{A_{n-1}})))^{\oplus l_{n-1}}\cong H^{2}\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{A_{n-1}}))))^{\oplus l_{n-1}}

by the
B_{\infty}
-section of the canonical projection

\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{A_{n-1}})))\to\mathbf{C}(\operatorname{Com}^{+}(\operatorname{Inj}(\operatorname{Qch}(X_{A_{n-1}}))))
.

It follows that
the assignment defines a map

\displaystyle\zeta_{A}\colon\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(A)\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(A),\ (X_{A},i_{A})\mapsto[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A}),i^{*}_{A}].

For each morphism

f\colon B\to A

in

\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

the diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 39.8996pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.25423pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.25423pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-39.8996pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.37224pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(f)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.25423pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.73364pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.59447pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(f)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.73364pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-23.96083pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.54762pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.54762pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(A)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

commutes.
To see this,
we may assume that

f

is a square zero extension

B=\mathbf{S}

of

A=\mathbf{R}
.
Then
for any

(X_{\mathbf{S}},i_{\mathbf{S}})\in\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(\mathbf{S})

with

X_{\mathbf{S}}\times_{\mathbf{S}}\mathbf{R}\cong X_{\mathbf{R}}

we have

X_{\mathbf{S}}\cong(X_{\mathbf{R}})_{\phi}

for some cocycle

\phi\in H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X_{\mathbf{R}}}/\mathbf{R})^{\oplus l}
.
We already know that

(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\mathbf{S}}),i^{*}_{\mathbf{S}})

is the Morita deformation of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{\mathbf{R}})
.
Since

(X_{\mathbf{S}},i_{\mathbf{S}})

maps to

(X_{\mathbf{R}},i_{\mathbf{R}})
,
the derived pullback functor

i^{*}_{\mathbf{S}}

factorizes through

i^{*}_{\mathbf{R}}
.
Thus the assignments

\{\zeta_{A}\}_{A\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}}

defines a natural transformation

\zeta\colon\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}
.

It remains to show that

\zeta_{A}

is bijective for each

A\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}
.
We will proceed by induction.
Now,
assume that

\zeta_{A_{i}}

are bijective for all

1\leq i\leq n
.
In order to show the surjectivity of

\zeta_{A_{n+1}}
,
take any element

[\mathfrak{a}_{A_{n+1}},u^{*}_{A_{n+1}}]\in\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(A_{n+1})
.
By the assumption of induction,
the reduction

[\mathfrak{a}_{A_{n}},u^{*}_{A_{n}}]\in\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(A_{n})

is equal to

[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(Y_{A_{n}}),j^{*}_{A_{n}}]

for some

(Y_{A_{n}},j_{A_{n}})\in\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(A_{n})
.
Combining
Theorem
6.8

with
Theorem
6.9
,
one sees that
the Morita
A_{n+1}
-deformation

(\mathfrak{a}_{A_{n+1}},u^{*}_{A_{n+1}})

of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(Y_{A_{n}})

is represented by

\operatorname{embr}_{\delta_{n}}(\phi_{A_{n+1}})

for some cocycle

\phi_{A_{n+1}}\in H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{Y_{A_{n}}/A_{n}})^{\oplus l_{n}}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{a}_{A_{n+1}},u^{*}_{A_{n+1}}]=[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(Y_{A_{n},\phi_{A_{n+1}}}),j^{*}_{A_{n+1}}].

In order to show the injectivity,
suppose that we have

(7.6)

\displaystyle[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{n+1}}),i^{*}_{A_{n+1}}]=[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(Y_{A_{n+1}}),j^{*}_{A_{n+1}}],

i.e.,
there is a Morita equivalence

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{n+1}})\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(Y_{A_{n+1}})

reducing to the identity on

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})
.
Combining the above argument with the commutative diagram
(
7
),
we have

\displaystyle[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{n+1}}),i^{*}_{A_{n+1}}]=[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{{A_{n}},\phi_{A_{n}}}),i^{*}_{A_{n+1}}],\ [\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(Y_{A_{n+1}}),j^{*}_{A_{n+1}}]=[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(Y_{{A_{n}},\psi_{A_{n}}}),j^{*}_{A_{n+1}}]

for some elements

\displaystyle(X_{A_{n}},i_{A_{n}}),(Y_{A_{n}},j_{A_{n}})\in\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}(A_{n})

and
cocycles

\displaystyle\phi_{A_{n}}\in H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{X_{A_{n}}/A_{n}})^{\oplus l_{n}},\ \psi_{A_{n}}\in H^{1}(\mathscr{T}_{Y_{A_{n}}/A_{n}})^{\oplus l_{n}}.

Applying

-\otimes^{L}_{A_{n+1}}A_{n}
,
we obtain a Morita equivalence

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{A_{n}})\simeq_{mo}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(Y_{A_{n}})

reducing to the identity on

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})
.
By the assumption of induction
there is an isomorphim

X_{A_{n}}\cong Y_{A_{n}}

reducing to the identity on

X_{0}
.
Then
(
7.6
)
implies

[\operatorname{embr}_{\delta_{n-1}}(\phi_{A_{n}})]=[\operatorname{embr}_{\delta_{n-1}}(\psi_{A_{n}})]
,
which in turn implies

[\phi_{A_{n}}]=[\psi_{A_{n}}]
.
Thus we obtain an isomorphism

X_{A_{n+1}}\cong Y_{A_{n+1}}

reducing to the identity on

X_{0}
.
∎

Remark 7.1
.

Consider the functor

\displaystyle\widetilde{\operatorname{Def}}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}\colon\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Set}

which sends each

A\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

to the set of isomorphism classes of Morita
A
-deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

and each morphism

B\to A

in

\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

to the map

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(B)\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(A)

induced by the derived pointwise tensor product with

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(A)

over

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(B)
.
Based on Remark

6.1
,
one can rewrite the proof of
Theorem
7.1

in terms of

\widetilde{\operatorname{Def}}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

to obtain a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}\colon\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{Def}}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

of deformation functors.
In the sequel,
we will identify the deformation functors

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})},\widetilde{\operatorname{Def}}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}\colon\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Set}

without further comments.

Remark 7.2
.

Theorem
7.1

tells us that
infinitesimal deformations of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

is controlled by the Kodaira–Spencer differential graded Lie algebra

\operatorname{KS}_{X_{0}}

of

X_{0}
.
Consider the functor

\operatorname{Def}_{\operatorname{KS}_{X_{0}}}\colon\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}\to\operatorname{Set}

defined as

(7.7)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Def}_{\operatorname{KS}_{X_{0}}}(A)=\frac{\operatorname{MC}_{\operatorname{KS}_{X_{0}}}(A)}{gauge\ equivalence}

for each

A\in\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}
,
where

(7.8)

\displaystyle\operatorname{MC}_{\operatorname{KS}_{X_{0}}}(A)=\Bigl\{x\in\operatorname{KS}^{1}_{X_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbf{k}}\mathfrak{m}_{A}\ |\ dx+\frac{1}{2}[x,x]=0\Bigr\}.

Recall that
given a differential graded Lie algebra

L

and
a commutative
\mathbf{k}
-algebra

\mathfrak{m}

there exists a natural structure of differential graded Lie algebra on the tensor product

L\otimes_{\mathbf{k}}\mathfrak{m}

given by

\displaystyle d(x\otimes_{\mathbf{k}}r)=dx\otimes_{\mathbf{k}}r,\ [x\otimes_{\mathbf{k}}r,y\otimes_{\mathbf{k}}s]=[x,y]\otimes_{\mathbf{k}}rs,\ x,y\in L,\ r,s\in\mathfrak{m}.

For every surjection

A\to\mathbf{k}[t]/t^{2}

in

\operatorname{Art_{\mathbf{k}}}

the set

\operatorname{Def}_{\operatorname{KS}_{X_{0}}}(A)

consists of solutions of the extended Maurer–Cartan equation to

\mathfrak{m}_{A}
.
Giving higher order deformations of

X_{0}

is equivalent to giving solutions of the extended Maurer–Cartan equation.
Indeed,
we have

\operatorname{Def}_{\operatorname{KS}_{X_{0}}}\simeq\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}

by

[
Man09
, Example 2.3]
.

Corollary 7.2
.

The functor

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

is prorepresented by

R
.

Proof.

This follows immediately as

\operatorname{Def}_{X_{0}}

is prorepresented by

R
.
∎

Corollary 7.3
.

The functor

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

has an effective universal formal family.

Proof.

Let

(R,\tilde{\xi})

be a universal formal family for

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}
,
where

\tilde{\xi}=\{\tilde{\xi}_{n}\}_{n}

belongs to the limit

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{Def}}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(R)=\displaystyle\lim_{\longleftarrow}\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(R/\mathfrak{m}^{n}_{R})

of the inverse system

\displaystyle\cdots\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{n+2})\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{n+1})\to\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}(R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}^{n})\to\cdots

induced by the natural quotient maps

R/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}_{R}\to R/\mathfrak{m}^{n}_{R}
.
Recall that
for the universal formal family

(R,\xi)

there is a noetherian formal scheme

\mathscr{X}

over

R

such that

X_{n}\cong\mathscr{X}\times_{R}R/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}_{R}

for each

n
,
where

(X_{n},i_{n})

are
R_{n}
-deformations of

X_{0}

defining

\xi_{n}
.
By

[
GD61
, Theorem III5.4.5]

there exists a scheme

X_{R}

flat projective over

R

whose formal completion along the closed fiber

X_{0}

is isomorphic to

\mathscr{X}
.
From the proof of
Theorem
7.1

it follows that

(\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{n}),i^{*}_{n})

defines

\tilde{\xi_{n}}
.
Then by

[
BFN10
, Theorem 1.2]

the
R
-linear dg category

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R})

yields the compatible system

\{\tilde{\xi}_{n}\}_{n}

via reduction along the natural quotient maps

R/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}_{R}\to R/\mathfrak{m}^{n}_{R}
,
which means

\tilde{\xi}

is effective.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

Recall that
the Dwyer–Kan model structure on

\operatorname{dgCat}_{\mathbf{k}}

has a natural simplicial enrichment

[
Töe
, Section 5]
.
We denote by

\operatorname{dgCat}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{k}}

the underlying
\infty
-category.
There is a notion of limits in
\infty
-categories
that behaves similarly to the classical one

[
Lur09
, Chapter 4]
.
As

\operatorname{dgCat}^{\infty}_{\mathbf{k}}

is the underlying
\infty
-category of a simplicial model category,
it admits limits

[
Lur09
, Corollary 4.2.4.8]
.
Hence we obtain a limit

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{Perf}}_{dg}(X_{R})=\displaystyle\lim_{\longleftarrow}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{n})

of the inverse system

\displaystyle\cdots\to\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{n+2})\to\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{n+1})\to\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{n})\to\cdots

of small
\mathbf{k}
-linear dg categories induced by the natural quotient maps

R/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}_{R}\to R/\mathfrak{m}^{n}_{R}
.

We claim that
the limit is quasi-equivalent to

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(\mathscr{X})
.
By

[
GD61
, Corollary 5.1.3]

the canonical map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{X_{R}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathscr{X}}(\hat{\mathscr{E}},\hat{\mathscr{F}})

defined by taking the formal completion of each morphism along the closed fiber
is an isomorphism for all coherent sheaves

\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F}

on

X_{R}
.
In particular,
we may write

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{Hom}_{X_{R}}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{F})}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathscr{X}}(\hat{\mathscr{E}},\hat{\mathscr{F}}).

Since

X_{R}

is projective over a complete local noetherian ring

R
,
by

[
GD61
, Corollary III5.1.6]

the functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{coh}(X_{R})\to\operatorname{coh}(\mathscr{X}),

which sends each coherent sheaf

\mathscr{F}

on

X_{R}

to its formal completion

\hat{\mathscr{F}}

along the closed fiber
is an equivalence of abelian categories.
We obtain the induced derived equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}(X_{R})\simeq D^{b}(X_{R})\simeq D^{b}(\mathscr{X})\simeq\operatorname{Perf}(\mathscr{X}).

Hence for

E,F\in\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R})

with formal completions

\hat{E},\hat{F}\in\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(\mathscr{X})

we may write

\displaystyle\widehat{\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{X_{R}}(E,F)}=\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\mathscr{X}}(\hat{E},\hat{F}).

Now,
one sees that
the objects and morphisms in

\operatorname{Perf}(\mathscr{X})

satisfy universality with respect to the induced inverse system on homotopy categories.
Thus the dg functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(\mathscr{X})\to\widehat{\operatorname{Perf}}_{dg}(X_{R})

uniquely determined by universality of the limit is a quasi-equivalence.
Namely,
the formal completion of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R})

is quasi-equivalent to

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(\mathscr{X})
.

Corollary 7.4
.

Any effective universal formal family for

\operatorname{Def}^{mo}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})}

is algebraizable.
In particular,
an algebraization is given by

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})

where

X_{S}

is a versal deformation of

X_{0}
.

Proof.

Consider the triple

(\operatorname{Spec}S,s,\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S}))
.
Since the reduction of

X_{S}

along the natural quotient maps

S/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}_{S}\to S/\mathfrak{m}^{n}_{S}

yields a compatible system isomorphic to

\xi
,
the reduction of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})

yields a compatible system isomorphic to

\tilde{\xi}
.
Thus

(\operatorname{Spec}S,s,\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S}))

gives a versal Morita deformation of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})
.
∎

Proposition 7.5
.

There is a quasi-equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})/\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})_{0}\simeq_{qeq}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{Q(S)})

where

Q(S)

is the quotient field of

S

and

X_{Q(S)}

is the generic fiber of

X_{S}
.

Proof.

By

[
Dri04
, Theorem 3.4]

and

[
Morb
, Theorem 1.1]

we have an equivalence

\displaystyle[\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})/\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})_{0}]\simeq\operatorname{Perf}(X_{S})/\operatorname{Perf}(X_{S})_{0}\simeq\operatorname{Perf}(X_{Q(S)})

of idempotent complete triangulated categories,
where
the middle category is the Verdier quotient by the full triangulated subcategory

\operatorname{Perf}(X_{S})_{0}\subset\operatorname{Perf}(X_{S})

of perfect complexes with
S
-torsion cohomology.
Then the claim follows from

[
CNS
, Theorem B]
.
∎

Remark 7.4
.

From the proof
one sees that
the dg categorical generic fiber is a natural dg enhancement of the categorical generic fiber introduced in

[
Morb
]
,
which is in turn based on the categorical general fiber by Huybrechts–Macrì–Stellari

[
HMS11
]
.

8.
Independence from geometric realizations

Due to Corollary

7.4
,
a versal Morita deformation of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})

is given by

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})

where

X_{S}

is a versal deformatiion of

X_{0}
.
Suppose that
there is another Calabi–Yau manifold

X^{\prime}_{0}

derived-equivalent to

X_{0}
.
Since by

[
CNS
, Theorem B]

dg enhancements of

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}(X_{0})\simeq D^{b}(X_{0})\simeq D^{b}(X^{\prime}_{0})\simeq\operatorname{Perf}(X^{\prime}_{0})

are unique,
we obtain a quasi-equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{0})\to\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{0}).

Hence

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})

gives also a versal Morita deformation of

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{0})
.

By
Lemma
3.2

we may assume

X_{S}

to be smooth projective over

S
.
Then one finds a smooth projective versal deformation

X^{\prime}_{S}

over the same base.
The construction requires the deformation theory of Fourier–Mukai kernels,
which we briefly review below.
It passes through effectivizations,
i.e.,
there are effectivizations

X_{R},X^{\prime}_{R}

of

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

over the same regular affine scheme

\operatorname{Spec}R
.
Applying

[
Mora
, Corollary 4.2]

and

[
CNS
, Theorem B]
,
we obtain a quasi-equivalence

(8.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R})\simeq_{qeq}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{R}).

Unwinding the construction of versal deformations recalled in
Section

3.3
,
one sees that,
up to equivalence of deformations,
the ambiguity of

X_{S}

essentially stems from the choice of indices

i\in I

of the filtered inductive system

\{R_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
where

R_{i}

are finitely generated
T
-subalgebras of

R

whose colimit is

R
.
The versal deformations

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

over the same base are obtained by choosing the same sufficiently large index.
From this observation combined with
Theorem
7.1

and the quasi-equivalence
(
8.1
),
it is natural to expect that
the versal Morita deformations

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{S})

become quasi-equivalent close to effectivizations.
In this section,
we prove our second main result
which yields the quasi-equivalence as a corollary.

8.1.
Deformations of Fourier–Mukai kernels

Suppose that
the derived equivalence of

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

is given by a Fourier–Mukai kernel

\mathcal{P}_{0}\in D^{b}(X_{0}\times X^{\prime}_{0})
.
In order to define a relative integral functor from

D^{b}(X_{S})

to

D^{b}(X^{\prime}_{S})
,
we deform

\mathcal{P}_{0}

to a perfect complex

\mathcal{P}_{S}

on

X_{S}\times_{S}X^{\prime}_{S}
.
Here,
for a deformation

[X_{\mathbf{P}},i_{\mathbf{P}}]\in\operatorname{Def}_{X}((\mathbf{P},\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{P}}))

of a
\mathbf{k}
-scheme

X
,
by a deformation of

E\in\operatorname{Perf}(X)

over

(\mathbf{P},\mathfrak{m}_{\mathbf{P}})

we mean a pair

(E_{\mathbf{P}},u_{\mathbf{P}})
,
where

E_{\mathbf{P}}\in\operatorname{Perf}(X_{\mathbf{P}})

and

u_{\mathbf{P}}\colon E_{\mathbf{P}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{k}\to E

is an isomorphism.
Two deformations

(E_{\mathbf{P}},u_{\mathbf{P}})

and

(F_{\mathbf{P}},v_{\mathbf{P}})

are equivalent
if there is an isomorphism

E_{\mathbf{P}}\to F_{\mathbf{P}}

reducing to an isomorphism of

E
.

The
R_{n}
-deformations

X_{n},X^{\prime}_{n}

of

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

and
their fiber product

X_{n}\times_{R_{n}}X^{\prime}_{n}

form the diagram

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 10.25638pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 34.25638pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X_{n}\times_{R_{n}}X^{\prime}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.4206pt\raise-10.82141pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{q_{n}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.25638pt\raise-26.6693pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.48996pt\raise-10.82141pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p_{n}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 112.52377pt\raise-26.6793pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 119.78015pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-10.25638pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X_{n}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 58.39008pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 112.52377pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X^{\prime}_{n}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

with the natural projections

q_{n}

and

p_{n}
.
For any perfect complex

\mathcal{P}_{n}

on

X_{n}\times_{R_{n}}X^{\prime}_{n}
,
the relative integral functor

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}\left(-\right)=Rp_{n*}\left(\mathcal{P}_{n}\otimes^{L}q^{*}_{n}\left(-\right)\right)

sends each object of

D^{b}(X_{n})

to

D^{b}(X^{\prime}_{n})
.
Due to the Grothendieck–Verdier duality
the functor

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}

admits the right adjoint

\Phi^{R}_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}=\Phi_{(\mathcal{P}_{n})_{R}}

with kernel

(\mathcal{P}_{n})_{R}=\mathcal{P}^{\vee}_{n}\otimes p^{*}_{n}\omega_{\pi^{\prime}_{n}}[\dim X_{0}]
,
where

\omega_{\pi^{\prime}_{n}}

is the determinant of the relative cotangent sheaf associated with the natural projection

\pi^{\prime}_{n}\colon X^{\prime}_{n}\to\operatorname{Spec}R_{n}
.

Lemma 8.1
.

(
[
Mora
, Lemma 3.1, 3.2]
)

Assume that

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}

is an equivalence.
Then for any thickening

X_{n}\hookrightarrow X_{n+1}

there exist a thickening

X^{\prime}_{n}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}_{n+1}

and
a perfect complex

\mathcal{P}_{n+1}

on

X_{n+1}\times_{R_{n+1}}X^{\prime}_{n+1}

with an isomorphism

\mathcal{P}_{n+1}\otimes^{L}_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}\cong\mathcal{P}_{n}

such that
the integral functor

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{n+1}}\colon D^{b}(X_{n+1})\to D^{b}(X^{\prime}_{n+1})

is an equivalence.

Iterative application of
Lemma
8.1

allows us to deform the Fourier–Mukai kernel

\mathcal{P}_{0}\in D^{b}(X_{0}\times X^{\prime}_{0})

to some Fourier–Mukai kernel

\mathcal{P}_{n}\in\operatorname{Perf}(X_{n}\times_{R_{n}}X^{\prime}_{n})

for arbitrary order

n
.
We obtain a system of deformations

\mathcal{P}_{n}\in\operatorname{Perf}(X_{n}\times_{R_{n}}X^{\prime}_{n})

of

\mathcal{P}_{0}

with compatible isomorphisms

\mathcal{P}_{n+1}\otimes^{L}_{R_{n+1}}R_{n}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}
.
According to

[
Lie06
, Proposition 3.6.1]

there exists an effectivization,
i.e.,
a perfect complex
\mathcal{P}_{R}
on
X_{R}\times_{R}X^{\prime}_{R}

with compatible isomorphisms

\mathcal{P}_{R}\otimes^{L}_{R}R_{n}\to\mathcal{P}_{n}
.
Recall that
to algebrize

\mathscr{X}

we used a filtered inductive system

\{R_{i}\}_{i\in I}

of finitely generated
T
-subalgebras of

R

whose colimit is

R
.
Taking an index

i

sufficiently large,
one finds smooth projective
R_{i}
-deformations

X_{R_{i}},X^{\prime}_{R_{i}}

of

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

whose pullback along the canonical homomorphism

R_{i}\hookrightarrow R

are

X_{R},X^{\prime}_{R}
.
Since we have

X_{R}\times_{R}X^{\prime}_{R}\cong\left(X_{R_{i}}\times_{R_{i}}X^{\prime}_{R_{i}}\right)\times_{R_{i}}R
,
by

[
Lie06
, Proposition 2.2.1]

there exists a perfect complex

\mathcal{P}_{R_{i}}

on

X_{R_{i}}\times_{R_{i}}X^{\prime}_{R_{i}}

with an isomorphism

\mathcal{P}_{R_{i}}\otimes^{L}_{R_{i}}R\to\mathcal{P}_{R}
.
Finally,
the derived pullback

\mathcal{P}_{S}\in\operatorname{Perf}(X_{S}\times_{S}X^{\prime}_{S})

along

R_{i}\to S

yields a deformation of

\mathcal{P}_{0}
.

Lemma 8.2
.

(
[
Mora
, Proposition 3.3]
)

Let

\mathcal{P}_{0}

be a Fourier–Mukai kernel
defining the derived equivalence of Calabi–Yau manifolds

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

of dimension more than two.
Then there exists a perfect complex

\mathcal{P}_{S}

on the fiber product

X_{S}\times_{S}X^{\prime}_{S}

of smooth projective versal deformations
with an isomorphism

\mathcal{P}_{S}\otimes^{L}_{S}\mathbf{k}\to\mathcal{P}_{0}
.

8.2.
Inherited equivalences

The schemes

X_{R_{i}},X^{\prime}_{R_{i}}
,
and
their fiber product

X_{R_{i}}\times_{R_{i}}X^{\prime}_{R_{i}}

together with the pullbacks along
T
-algebra homomorphisms

R_{i}\to R_{j}\to R

for

i\leq j

form the commutative diagram

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X^{\prime}_{R_{i}},}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where

q_{i},p_{i}

are smooth projective of relative dimension

\dim X_{0}
.
Given a collection

\{\mathcal{P}_{i}\}_{i\in I}

with

\mathcal{P}_{i}\in\operatorname{Perf}(X_{R_{i}}\times_{R_{i}}X^{\prime}_{R_{i}})

satisfying

\mathcal{P}_{j}\cong\mathcal{P}_{R_{i}}\otimes^{L}_{R_{i}}R_{j}

and

\mathcal{P}_{R}\cong\mathcal{P}_{R_{j}}\otimes^{L}_{R_{j}}R

for all
i\leq j
,
consider the relative integral functors

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{i}}=Rp_{i*}\left(\mathcal{P}_{i}\otimes^{L}q^{*}_{i}\left(-\right)\right)\colon D^{b}(X_{R_{i}})\to D^{b}(X^{\prime}_{R_{i}}).

Since

p_{i}

is projective
and

\mathcal{P}_{i}
is of finite homological dimension,
i.e.,

\mathcal{P}_{i}\otimes^{L}q^{*}_{i}F_{R_{i}}

are bounded for each object

F_{R_{i}}\in D^{b}(X_{R_{i}})
,
one can apply

[
LST13
, Lemma 1.8]

to see that

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{i}}

send perfect complexes to perfect complexes.
We use the same symbol to denote the restricted functor.

Theorem 8.3
.

There exists an index

j\in I

such that
for all

k\geq j

the functors

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}\colon\operatorname{Perf}(X_{R_{k}})\to\operatorname{Perf}(X^{\prime}_{R_{k}})

are equivalences of triangulated categories of perfect complexes.
In particular,
the dg categories

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R_{k}}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{R_{k}})

of perfect complexes are quasi-equivalent.

Proof.

Under the assumption
one always finds deformations

[X_{R_{j}},i_{R_{j}}],[X^{\prime}_{R_{j}},i^{\prime}_{R_{j}}]

smooth projective over

(R_{j},\mathfrak{m}_{R_{j}})

for sufficiently large index

j\in I
.
Moreover,
the pullbacks along

R_{j}\to R

and

R_{j}\to S

yield respectively
effectivizations

X_{R},X^{\prime}_{R}

of universal formal families

\xi,\xi^{\prime}

and
versal deformations

(\operatorname{Spec}S,s,X_{S}),(\operatorname{Spec}S,s,X^{\prime}_{S})

of

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}
.
Recall that

(\operatorname{Spec}S,s)

is an étale neighborhood of

t

in

\operatorname{Spec}T

with

t

corresponding to the maximal ideal

(t_{1},\ldots,t_{d})\subset T
,
and
the formal completions of

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

along the closed fibers over

s

are isomorphic to

\hat{X}_{R},\hat{X}^{\prime}_{R}
.
In summary,
we have the pullback diagrams

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 16.17361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.77777pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern Let

\mathcal{P}_{0}\in D^{b}(X_{0}\times_{\mathbf{k}}X^{\prime}_{0})

be a Fourier–Mukai kernel defining the derived equivalence.
As explained above,
one can deform

\mathcal{P}_{0}

to a perfect complex

\mathcal{P}_{j}\in D^{b}(X_{R_{j}}\times_{R_{j}}X^{\prime}_{R_{j}})
.
Due to the Grothendieck–Verdier duality
the functor

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}

admits a left adjoint

\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}=\Phi_{(\mathcal{P}_{i})_{L}}

with kernel

(\mathcal{P}_{j})_{L}=\mathcal{P}^{\vee}_{R_{j}}\otimes p^{*}_{j}\omega_{\pi^{\prime}_{R_{j}}}[\dim X_{0}]
.
By

[
BV03
, Corollary 3.1.2]

the category

\operatorname{Perf}(X_{R_{j}})

is generated by some single object

E_{R_{j}}
.
Namely,
each object

F_{R_{j}}\in\operatorname{Perf}(X_{R_{j}})

can be obtained from

E_{R_{j}}

by taking
isomorphisms,
finite direct sums,
direct summands,
shifts,
and
bounded number of cones.
The counit morphism

\eta_{j}\colon\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}\to\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{Perf}(X_{R_{j}})}

gives the distinguished triangle

\displaystyle\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}(E_{R_{j}})\xrightarrow{\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}})}E_{R_{j}}\to C(E_{R_{j}})\coloneqq\operatorname{Cone}(\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}})).

For sufficiently large

k\geq j

we will show that

\eta_{k}(E_{R_{k}})

is an isomorphism
and then

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}

is fully faithful.
Similarly,
one can show that

\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}

is also fully faithful.
Thus

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}

is an equivalence,
as it is a fully faithful functor admitting a fully faithful left adjoint.

Pullback along

R_{j}\subset R_{k}

yields

(8.2)

\displaystyle\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}(E_{R_{k}})\xrightarrow{f^{*}_{jk}\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}})}E_{R_{k}}\to f^{*}_{jk}C(E_{R_{j}})

with

E_{k}=f^{*}_{jk}E_{j}

and

\mathcal{P}_{k}=(f_{jk}\times f^{\prime}_{jk})^{*}\mathcal{P}_{j}
.
Further pullback along

R_{k}\subset R

yields

\displaystyle\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}(E_{R})\xrightarrow{f^{*}_{j}\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}})}E_{R}\to f^{*}_{j}C(E_{R_{j}}),

where

f_{j}\colon X_{R}\to X_{R_{j}}

satisfies

f_{jk}\circ f_{k}=f_{j}
.
Restriction to the closed fiber

X_{0}

yields

\displaystyle\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}(E_{R}|_{X_{0}})\xrightarrow{\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{0}})}E_{R}|_{X_{0}}\to(f^{*}_{j}C(E_{R_{j}}))|_{X_{0}}.

Note that
since

f^{-1}_{j}(X_{0})=X_{0}

and
the restriction of the counit morphism is the counit morphism,
we have

(f^{*}_{j}\eta(E_{R_{j}}))|_{X_{0}}=\eta(E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{0}})
.
Each term in the above distinguished triangle is perfect
so that
we may consider the restriction to the closed fiber.
Since

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}

is an equivalence,

\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{0}})

is an isomorphism
and
we obtain a quasi-isomorphism

f^{*}_{j}C(E_{R_{j}})|_{X_{0}}\cong 0
.
Then the support of

f^{*}_{j}C(E_{R_{j}})

is a proper closed subscheme of

X_{R}

which does not contain any closed point of

X_{R}
.
Thus the quasi-isomorphism extends to

f^{*}_{j}C(E_{R_{j}})\cong 0
.
From

[
Lie06
, Proposition 2.2.1]

it follows

f^{*}_{jk}C(E_{R_{j}})\cong 0

when

k\in I

is sufficiently large.

Take any closed point

u\in\operatorname{Spec}R_{j}

whose inverse image by

g_{jk}\colon\operatorname{Spec}R_{k}\to\operatorname{Spec}R_{j}

is not empty.
We have the pullback diagrams

78.00941pt\raise-27.11641pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.4903pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g_{u,jk}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.428pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 137.40092pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 148.7551pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 166.10927pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 166.10927pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Spec}R_{k}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

Note that

f_{u,jk}

is surjective by construction
and
flat
as

g_{u,jk}

is flat.
The restriction of
(
8.2
)
to

f^{-1}_{jk}(X_{u})

yields

\displaystyle\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{u,jk}}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{u,jk}}(E_{R_{k}}|_{f^{-1}_{jk}(X_{u})})\xrightarrow{f^{*}_{u,jk}\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{u}})}E_{R_{k}}|_{f^{-1}_{jk}(X_{u})}\to f^{*}_{u,jk}C(E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{u}})\cong 0,

where

\mathcal{P}_{u,jk}=\mathcal{P}_{k}|_{f^{-1}_{jk}(X_{u})\times_{g^{-1}_{jk}(u)}f^{-1}_{jk}(X^{\prime}_{u})}
.
It follows

C(E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{u}})\cong 0

and

\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{u}})

is an isomorphism.

By

[
BV03
, Lemma 3.4.1]

the restriction

E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{u}}

is a generator of

\operatorname{Perf}(X_{u})
.
Then each object

F_{u}\in\operatorname{Perf}(X_{u})

can be obtained from

E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{u}}

by taking
isomorphisms,
finite direct sums,
direct summands,
shifts,
and
bounded number of cones.
We may assume that

E_{R_{j}}|_{X_{u}}

has no nontrivial direct summands,
as

\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}

and

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}

commute with direct sums on

\operatorname{Perf}(X^{\prime}_{u})

and

\operatorname{Perf}(X_{u})

respectively
with

\mathcal{P}_{u,j}=\mathcal{P}_{j}|_{X_{u}\times X^{\prime}_{u}}

[
BV03
, Corollary 3.3.4]
.
One inductively sees that
the counit morphism

\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}(F_{u})\to F_{u}

is an isomorphism.
In other words,
the restriction

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}

of

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}

to

X_{u}

is fully faithful.
Similarly,
the restriction

\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}

of

\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}

to

X^{\prime}_{u}

is also fully faithful.
Thus

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}

is an equivalence.

Since

X_{u}

is a smooth projective
\mathbf{k}
-variety,

\displaystyle\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}\cong\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{Perf}(X_{u})},\ \Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}\circ\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{u,j}}\cong\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{Perf}(X^{\prime}_{u})}

imply

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{u,j}*(\mathcal{P}_{u,j})_{L}\cong\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{u,j}},\ (\mathcal{P}_{u,j})_{L}*\mathcal{P}_{u,j}\cong\mathscr{O}_{\Delta^{\prime}_{u,j}}

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{u,j}\colon X_{u}\hookrightarrow X_{u}\times X_{u},\ \Delta^{\prime}_{u,j}\colon X^{\prime}_{u}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}_{u}\times X^{\prime}_{u}

are the diagonal embeddings.
Pullback by

f_{u,jk}

yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{u,jk}*(\mathcal{P}_{u,jk})_{L}\cong\mathscr{O}_{\Delta_{u,jk}},\ (\mathcal{P}_{u,jk})_{L}*\mathcal{P}_{u,jk}\cong\mathscr{O}_{\Delta^{\prime}_{u,jk}}

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{u,jk}\colon f^{-1}_{jk}(X_{u})\hookrightarrow f^{-1}_{jk}(X_{u})\times_{g^{-1}_{jk}(u)}f^{-1}_{jk}(X_{u}),\ \Delta^{\prime}_{u,jk}\colon(f^{\prime}_{jk})^{-1}(X^{\prime}_{u})\hookrightarrow(f^{\prime}_{jk})^{-1}(X^{\prime}_{u})\times_{g^{-1}_{jk}(u)}(f^{\prime}_{jk})^{-1}(X^{\prime}_{u})

are the relative diagonal embeddings.
Thus

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{u,jk}}

is an equivalence.
Since

X_{R_{k}}

is covered by the collection

\{f^{-1}_{jk}(X_{u})\}_{u}

with

u

running through all the closed points of

\operatorname{Spec}R_{j}
,
from

[
LST13
, Proposition 1.3]

it follows that

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{k}}

is an equivalence.
By the same argument,
we conclude that

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{l}}

are equivalences for all

l\geq k
.
Applying

[
CNS
, Theorem B]
,
we obtain a quasi-equivalence

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R_{l}})\simeq_{qeq}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{R_{l}})

for all

l\geq k
.
∎

Corollary 8.4
.

Let

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

be derived-equivalent Calabi–Yau manifolds of dimension more than two
and

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

their smooth projective versal deformations
over a common nonsingular affine
\mathbf{k}
-variety

\operatorname{Spec}S
.
Assume that

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

correspond to a first order approximation

R_{j}\to S

of

R_{j}\hookrightarrow R

for sufficiently large

j\in I
.
Then

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

are derived-equivalent.
In particular,
the dg categories

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S}),\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{S})

of perfect complexes are quasi-equivalent.

Proof.

By
assumption
one can apply
Theorem
8.3

to find an index

j\in I

such that

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

are the pullbacks of smooth projective families

X_{R_{j}},X^{\prime}_{R_{j}}

over

R_{j}

satisfying

\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{R_{j}})\simeq_{qeq}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{R_{j}})
.
Consider the distinguished triangle

\displaystyle\Phi^{L}_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}\circ\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{j}}(E_{R_{j}})\xrightarrow{\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}})}E_{R_{j}}\to C(\eta_{j}(E_{R_{j}}))\cong 0.

Applying the same argument in the above proof to

X_{S}\to X_{R_{j}}

instead of

X_{R_{k}}\to X_{R_{j}}
,
one sees that

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}_{S}}\colon D^{b}(X_{S})\to D^{b}(X^{\prime}_{S})

is an equivalence
with

\mathcal{P}_{S}=(f_{jS}\times f^{\prime}_{jS})^{*}\mathcal{P}_{j}
.
∎

Proposition 8.5
.

Let

X_{0},X^{\prime}_{0}

be derived-equivalent Calabi–Yau manifolds of dimension more than two
and

X_{S},X^{\prime}_{S}

smooth projective versal deformations over a common nonsingular affine variety

\operatorname{Spec}S
.
Then the dg categorical generic fibers are quasi-equivalent.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})/\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})_{0}\simeq_{qeq}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{Q(S)})\simeq_{qeq}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X^{\prime}_{Q(S)})\simeq_{qeq}\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})/\operatorname{Perf}_{dg}(X_{S})_{0},

where
the first
and
the tirhd quasi-equivalences follow from Proposition

7.5
.
The second quasi-equivalence follows from
the above corollary,

[
Mora
, Theorem 1.1]
,

[
Morb
, Corollary 4.2]
,
and

[
CNS
, Theorem B]
.
∎
